# Continuation state

Updated: 2026-07-14 (America/Toronto)

## Binding accumulated state

The campaign artifacts in this directory and the RH-relevant entries of
`KNOWLEDGE.md` have been read.  Their death certificates remain binding.
In particular, this continuation does not reopen finite Weil positivity,
boundary-modulus mollifiers, compactness-first Nyman--Beurling, inverse
spectral insertion, density-without-amplification, or Maynard--Tao work.

normalization.  A cumulative-past projection extends the argument to
countably many channels and rules out energy escape to channel index.  Thus
the entire primitive chirp mechanism is closed; a survivor must use a
genuinely non-primitive exact cancellation.

Item 126 closes the first non-primitive mechanism as well: weighted
centering, orthogonal projection, and every regular rank-one global
correction produce a fixed-tail coefficient with exactly zero weighted mean,
so it is negative somewhere unless the correction is trivial.  The active
covariance frontier is now finite-rank/global-divergence normalization rather
than a scalar correction of one spectral orbit.

Item 127 gives the general geometry behind this obstruction: under a Bochner
exact normalization and strict far-pair positivity, no normalized feature
tail can have a nonzero weak profile.  Finite-dimensional or norm-precompact
tail directions are therefore impossible without any stationary or
regular-variation hypothesis; only genuine weak escape in an
infinite-dimensional, noncompact direction field can survive.

Item 128 applies that geometry at the first nonzero asymptotic scale and
closes every continuous finite-rank correction under a common regularly
varying orbit tail.  It also closes Hilbert--Schmidt corrections under the
natural no-index-escape domain bound.  Only a non-Bochner/global-divergence
normalization, failure of a common fixed-tail law, or singular coefficient
energy escaping through the correction index remains in this family.

Item 131 closes the first genuine global-divergence mechanisms.  The
stationary Laplace flux has the correct PSD sign transition, reciprocal-theta
scale, a closable form, and K as an adiabatic zero-energy mode, but every
nonzero stationary zero-frequency multiplier has a fixed negative lag whose
theta-normalized row diverges negatively.  A literally Bochner-centered
two-point envelope instead has an explicit eventually negative center row.
Only a genuinely nonstationary infinite-channel divergence, with weak index
escape, no fixed negative lag, and local negative amplitude already at the
`K(x)K(y)` scale, remains in the covariance portfolio.

Item 132 closes every one-scale or finite-log-width realization of that
singular transition, independently of feature norm or channel speed.  Exact
row centering forces logarithmic negative capacity at least
`c exp(x/2)/K(x-D)` in the positive theta tail.  Moreover a valid positive
split must annihilate every threshold null vector `p(-D^2)K`; local
`L2`-closability then collapses the completion by item 52.  The only remaining
covariance mechanism is therefore a logarithmically multiscale,
non-locally-closable cascade satisfying the full threshold module and the
item-81 Radon/domain bridge.

Item 133 sharpens the required ultraviolet scale by splitting at
`eta_x=exp(-2x)`: essentially all compensating negative mass must lie below
that scale and occupy logarithmic width at least `c exp(x/2)/K(x)`.  The
first negative lag is therefore at most
`exp(-2x-c exp(x/2)/K(x))`.  This excludes every finite-band cascade and,
under an explicitly stronger theta-Sobolev hypothesis, every absolutely
continuous direction field.  Mere closedness does not imply that hypothesis;
the surviving cascade must remain globally nonlocal, logarithmic-order, and
compatible with all threshold-null and common-domain conditions.

Item 134 fixes the topology of the threshold-null audit.  Ordinary-`L2`
closed Gram forms are impossible by global polynomial-radical density and
the antipodal far-sign test.  The natural reciprocal-theta space is strictly
different: evaluation at any known Hardy zero gives a bounded nonzero
functional annihilating the entire threshold module.  Hence the remaining
cascade must be singular from the ordinary-`L2` viewpoint, and no weighted
density claim may be used to close it without defeating this explicit
real-zero annihilator.

Items 135 and 137 close the all-log cascade itself.  A tail cut has strictly
positive integrated ordinary majorant, whereas exact Gram centering makes
the same cut covariance nonpositive.  Item 135 proves this first for literal
Bochner features.  Item 137 proves that passing to the natural
reciprocal-theta closed-form topology does not help once the item-81 common
domain and Radon/no-boundary-residue bridge is actually verified: the cut
vector belongs to that domain and obeys the same polarization identity.
Thus a covariance survivor must carry negative prime-line or other genuine
off-diagonal distributions; a pointwise or Radon Gram majorant is closed in
every admissible form topology.  This does not prove positivity, because the
remaining distributional completion is the full Weil/Douglas factorization.
Items 138 and 140 audit that final escape sharply.  The actual negative prime
lines cancel the positive tail-cut advantage not only to leading PNT order
but to every fixed algebraic order.  The coefficients match term by term
because the Xi multiplier removes every nontrivial-zero residue, leaving the
same trivial-zero moments as the J expansion.  Item 141 evaluates the
beyond-all-orders remainder by the exact zero side and proves it is eventually
positive, uniformly without RH.  This is only one-cut positivity; demanding
the matrix inequality for all finite cut packets is form-dense Weil
positivity and hence RH-equivalent.

1. **Theta-orbit/Jensen cone (closed).**  The proposed independent mechanism
   that every positive individual theta orbit is Laguerre--Polya is false
   already for orbit `m=2`: its degree-two Jensen discriminant
   `4(a_1^2-2a_0a_2)` has a rigorous 256-bit interval strictly below zero.
   Modularity makes only the full theta sum even; it supplies no common
   interlacing of the positive orbit summands.
2. **Xi heat flow (closed as an independent mechanism).**  With
   `K_t(u)=exp(tu^2) Phi(u)` and `F_t=Fourier(K_t)`, the exact identity
   `F_t'^2-F_t F_t''=Fourier(A_t)` holds, where
   `A_t(w)=1/2 integral (2u-w)^2 K_t(u)K_t(w-u)du`.
   The first proposed proof, complete monotonicity of
   `r -> A_t(sqrt r)`, is false: any nonzero completely monotone function has
   at least an exponential lower tail, whereas the theta kernel gives
   `A_t(sqrt r)<=exp(-c exp(2sqrt r))` up to subdominant factors.  The weaker
   remaining package is strict positive definiteness of `A_t` for
   `0<t<=1/2` plus a uniform high-height no-escape theorem.  The first is
   exactly strict positivity of the Turan collision form; the second is a
   global zero-exclusion/uniformity assertion not supplied by it.  Positive
   conditional-variance structure and log-concavity do not imply positive
   definiteness (the Laplace kernel is an explicit counterexample).  With the
   complete-monotonicity mechanism gone, this package is rejected as the
   zero-collision theorem plus its missing RH-strength uniform bridge.
3. **Divisor-extremal closure (closed as a mechanism).**  The forward lemma
   actually required is: from every `CA intersect XA` record endpoint, a
   later bounded-index CA endpoint has a positive cumulative exact log-height
   increment.  A bound merely between already consecutive records is vacuous
   after a last record.  The exact increment includes prime, old-exponent, and
   possible two-prime quotient steps; the repository's new-frontier-only
   conjecture does not cover it.  This forward recovery lemma is the missing
   global record theorem, not a consequence of the local CA geometry.
4. **Cutoff-free parity (closed in this continuation).**  Suzuki's exact
   continuum theorem proves positivity, simplicity, and evenness of the ground
   state only for sufficiently small support.  His all-support self-adjoint
   extension has a real-zero characteristic function `W(a,theta;z)`, but the
   compact-local limit of normalized `W` to
   `z^2 xi(1/2-iz)/xi'(1/2-iz)` is explicitly conjectural.  Moreover the
   limit is formulated with `T_a=A_a` (shift `lambda=0`), which already
   requires `A_a>0` for every `a`; continuity of the lowest eigenvalue makes
   this nondegeneracy equivalent to RH.  Thus the Perron theorem does not
   supply the missing global bridge and this route is not live.
5. **Shifted positive Dirichlet series (closed).**  For any nonzero
   nonnegative trigonometric polynomial, its constant Fourier coefficient is
   strictly positive.  Applying it to shifts of `-zeta'/zeta` therefore forces
   an unshifted companion with real pole at `1-c`, to the right of the
   zero-induced pole at `beta-c`.  The obstruction is decisive:
   `(-zeta'/zeta)^2` itself has nonnegative Dirichlet coefficients, a double
   real pole at `1`, and double nonreal poles at every zeta zero.  Landau's
   theorem sees only the rightmost real singularity and gives no contradiction.
6. **Lee--Yang theta measure (closed as an independent mechanism).**  Exact
   sufficient lemma would realize
   the normalized measure `Phi(u)du` as a weak limit of explicitly constructed
   finite ferromagnetic magnetization laws with locally uniform moment-
   generating convergence.  The Jacobi product has elementary roots off the
   Lee--Yang circle and supplies no couplings.  The narrower Gaussian plus
   independent symmetric-Bernoulli class is rigorously impossible: convergence
   would force `3 gamma_1` to be another zeta ordinate, while an Arb interval
   evaluation excludes `zeta(1/2+3i gamma_1)=0`.  General interacting Ising
   limits remain unconstructed; asserting their existence is just unproved
   Lee--Yang membership and is rejected.
7. **Suzuki characteristic limit (closed).**  The exact truncated-convolution
   residual has both a `lambda` Green term and a full exterior convolution;
   the screw kernel does not decay, and the forcing transforms grow
   exponentially with support.  Characteristic zeros generically depend on
   `lambda`; using `lambda=0` for all widths assumes the RH-equivalent
   positivity of `A_a`, while no uniform alternative lower shift is known.
   Also, `z^2 xi/xi'` has genuine poles between consecutive real Xi zeros, so
   the paper's literal whole-plane compact-local entire-function limit is
   impossible.  A corrected punctured-plane limit plus divisor control is at
   least the missing global zero-interlacing theorem, not an independent
   boundary estimate.
8. **Bohr-lift Euler product (closed).**  For
   `F_eps=product_p(1-p^(-1/2-eps)z_p)^(-1)`, the `H^2` norm is
   `zeta(1+2eps)`, but evaluation at the zeta prime coordinates is bounded
   only when `Re(s)>1+eps`; the union gives only the classical half-plane.
   Removing the first chaos produces the honest zero-free factor
   `R(s)=product_p exp(-p^-s)/(1-p^-s)` on `Re(s)>1/2`.  Reattaching the
   discarded `sum_p p^-s` requires a branch of `log zeta`, exactly the desired
   zero-free continuation.  An explicit cylindrical zero-free sequence
   converging to `1` in `H^2` but to `0` at any non-reproducing point proves
   that norm convergence cannot preserve the needed nonvanishing.
9. **Riemann--Siegel dominance (closed).**  A rigorous smoothed two-piece
   approximate functional equation is available, but no phase-independent
   lower bound for either piece exists.  The positive-coefficient polynomial
   `product_(p<=y)(1+p^-s)` can be made arbitrarily small for every
   `1/2<sigma<=1` by Kronecker approximation sending all prime phases near
   `-1`.  Thus positivity, phase rotation, and the functional-equation weight
   cannot prove one-piece dominance; the missing inequality already is the
   full zero-free right half-strip assertion.
10. **Vertical-line infinite divisibility (closed).**  The uncompleted
    gamma/Euler ratio has an explicit positive Levy representation for
    `sigma>1`, but multiplying by `s(s-1)` introduces a uniquely determined
    absolutely continuous signed Levy density that is negative on a half-line;
    prime atoms cannot repair it.  For `sigma<=1`, the large-jump prime mass
    `sum_p p^-sigma` diverges.  Independently, infinite divisibility for all
    `sigma>1/2` would pass to the continuous `sigma downarrow1/2` limit, whose
    characteristic function has Hardy critical-line zeros and hence cannot be
    infinitely divisible.
11. **Cyclotomic zero replication (closed).**  A zeta zero occurs once in the
    conductor-one principal factor of every cyclotomic Dedekind zeta; it is not
    replicated among the primitive nonprincipal characters.  Keeping the
    repeated labels gives identical principal vectors and a rank-one Gram term
    of weight exactly equal to the replicated signal.  The primitive large
    sieve deletes this direction; restoring it restores the same linear
    diagonal cost.  Summed field discriminant/degree cost is quadratic in the
    conductor range, much larger than the number of copies.  Thus algebraic
    replication does not defeat the family-concentration death certificate.
12. **Horizontal-shift Hermite--Biehler flow (closed).**  The zeros move
    rigidly as `-gamma+i(beta-1/2-a)`; there is no interacting flow.  The
    natural normalized HB-modulus monotonicity fails in both directions by
    Arb interval examples for the actual xi function.  Its infinitesimal
    `a downarrow0` form is positivity of `Re xi'/xi` throughout the right half
    strip, which an off-line zero violates immediately and which is already
    RH-equivalent.  Euler-product signs are unavailable there.
13. **Jensen downward descent (closed).**  The derivative, Pascal, and heat
    identities all flow upward in the coefficient shift; inversion requires
    uncontrolled antiderivation constants.  The unique diagonal descent has
    multipliers `gamma_(n+j)/gamma_(n+j+1)` and fails the Pólya--Schur test
    already at `n=0,d=3`: an Arb-certified image of `(1+X)^3` has strictly
    negative discriminant.  Theta integration by parts changes the weight to
    `Phi'` rather than closing a finite coefficient recurrence.
14. **Orthogonal-polynomial partial sums (closed).**  Romik's
    Meixner--Pollaczek branch is closed at its second
    nontrivial partial sum.  With `f_2(t/2)=t^2/2-3/4` and
    `f_4(t/2)=t^4/24-17t^2/24+21/32`, writing that partial sum as
    `A+B t^2+C t^4` gives an Arb-certified discriminant in
    `[-4.52630e-5,-4.27124e-5]`; it therefore has nonreal zeros.  Two
    independent derivations agree.  The continuous-Hahn branch fails at the
    same gate: its degree-four truncation has squared-variable discriminant in
    `[-8.90236e-5,-8.59124e-5]` by Arb interval integration, hence four
    nonreal zeros, while the preceding quadratic is hyperbolic.  Thus neither
    alternating-positive orthogonal expansion transfers basis interlacing to
    its coefficient-weighted truncations.
15. **Pólya correlation convexity (closed).**  The chosen sufficient
    real-space theorem would make all generalized Laguerre forms positive if
    the weighted theta correlation kernels `K_n` were convex on the positive
    half-line.  Exact differentiation gives
    `K_n''(0)=2 integral s^(2n)Phi(s)^2(log Phi)''(s)ds<0` from strict
    log-concavity, so the first nonautomatic kernel is locally concave and the
    criterion fails analytically.
16. **Exponential-tilt interlacing (closed).**  The required simultaneous
    nonvanishing does not follow from strict log-concavity or the increasing
    ratio `tanh(delta u)`: the Fourier two-by-two kernel minor is
    `sin(t(u_2-u_1))` and changes sign.  More decisively,
    `K(u)=exp(-u^2)(u^4+u^2+15)` is strictly log-concave and superexponential,
    but its Fourier transform is proportional to
    `exp(-z^2/4)(z^4-16z^2+260)`, with roots satisfying `z^2=8+/-14i`.
    At such an off-axis root the corresponding tilted cosine and sine
    transforms vanish simultaneously.  Thus the proposed shape hypotheses
    supply no variation-diminishing theorem.
17. **Quadratic-variable Pick/Stieltjes route (closed as an independent
    mechanism).**  Let
    `G(w)=Xi(sqrt(w))`, interpreted by its entire even power series.  Exact
    current lemma: derive from the theta integral, rather than from a zero
    product, that `-G'/G` is a Stieltjes/Pick function on the slit plane.  This
    would force every zero of `G` onto the positive real axis and imply RH.
    The Stieltjes assertion is conversely implied by RH through the genus-zero
    product, so it is an exact equivalent unless theta supplies an independent
    mechanism.  The first two nontrivial theta Hankel minors are positive, but
    their exact moment kernels take both signs.  The first equals
    `(3m1*m3+15m1^2*m2-10m2^2)/1440`; its symmetrized kernel is negative at
    `(1,1,0)`.  Strictly log-concave controls make both first minors negative,
    and the theta modular integration-by-parts recurrence retains unavoidable
    mixed negative terms.  Thus raw moments, log-concavity, TP2, and modular
    differentiation give no iterable compound-Gram identity; retaining all
    Hankel inequalities alone merely renames RH.
18. **Paired eta cone (closed).**  Although `eta(s)/s` is the Laplace
    transform of a nonnegative alternating-step kernel for `Re(s)>0`, eta has
    the exact artificial zero lattice `s=1+2*pi*i*k/log 2`, `k!=0`.  On Mellin
    fibers the associated dyadic dilation has modulus `2^(1-Re(s))`, so it is
    a contraction only in the classical half-plane `Re(s)>1`.  The step
    kernel has an exact negative `2 by 2` Pólya-frequency minor and the paired
    terms have dense phases.  Dividing away the artificial lattice recovers
    zeta and no independent cone proof.
19. **Two-base positive Mellin kernels (closed).**  For `q=2,3`, set
    `E_q(s)=(1-q^(1-s))zeta(s)`.  The exact Abel kernel is
    `B_q(x)=floor(x)-q floor(x/q)>=0`, not `x-q floor(x/q)`, and
    `E_q(s)/s=integral_1^infinity B_q(x)x^(-s-1)dx`.  The two artificial zero
    lattices are disjoint, so a common zero in the right half-plane is exactly
    a zeta zero.  But the cross-kernel minors already take the exact values
    `-1` and `2` on the integer-part samples `1,3,5`, excluding sign regularity
    and Chebyshev separation.  Algebraically the pair has rank one:
    `(1-3^(1-s))E_2-(1-2^(1-s))E_3=0`.  Multiplying both positive Laplace
    kernels by `1+c exp(-as)` supplies an explicit positive-kernel control
    with common zeros arbitrarily far inside `Re(s)>1/2`.  Hence positivity of
    the two kernels does not remove their shared unknown factor, and common
    nonvanishing is just zeta nonvanishing.
20. **Theta functional-difference/Sturm route (closed).**  The Jacobi heat
    equation gives Xi as the Mellin transform of a positive self-adjoint heat
    trace, not as a characteristic determinant.  Uniqueness of Dirichlet
    series proves there is no nontrivial finite rational-coefficient shift
    recurrence among `xi(s+2k)` independent of zeta.  A positive diagonal
    operator already has spectral zeta `1+M L^(-s)`, with nonreal zeros on
    `Re(s)=log(M)/log(L)`.  Thus self-adjointness constrains the heat spectrum,
    not zeros of its spectral zeta; manufacturing a determinant equal to Xi
    is the previously rejected inverse-spectral insertion.
21. **Matching-polynomial/interlacing-family route (closed as an independent
    mechanism).**  The literal degree-two theta section has Arb-certified
    discriminant `[-2.87184692683137e-5 +/- 2.05e-29]`, so it is not a matching
    polynomial.  High-degree matching limits pass the first global Newton
    tests, but the positive `m=2` theta orbit violates the first limiting
    matching inequality by a rigorous negative interval; orbitwise addition
    is also incompatible with graph-component multiplication.  Under RH the
    unknown factors `(1-w/gamma^2)` are disjoint weighted edges, while any
    compact-local matching approximation implies RH by Heilmann--Lieb and
    Hurwitz.  With no theta deletion/contraction recursion, graph existence is
    exactly the desired real-zero factorization.
22. **Polar transport/determinant-line curvature (closed).**  The exact finite
    curvature is the Gram tensor
    `Tr(P M_q^*(I-P)M_p P)`: it measures only normal leakage, is quadratic in
    Euler tangents, and is supported on prime-power differences.  The Weil
    strain is an interior first variation, linear in individual prime-power
    shifts.  An identity coordinate cannot create the missing cross term
    because `(I-P)P=0`.  On the repository's `N=4` truncated dilation carrier,
    `D(z)=I-z 2^(-1/2)T_log2` has determinant curvature identically zero while
    `-2^(-1/2)(T^*R+RT)` has explicit nonzero diagonal entries
    `-sqrt(2)log(3/2)` and `-sqrt(2)log 2`.  Infinite rank additionally lacks
    trace-class/HS summability.  Adding a counterterm that restores the linear
    strain is precisely the unproved Weil--Sonin comparison, not a consequence
    of polar decomposition.
23. **Three follow-ups (closed).**  (i) For alternating kernels,
    `f_q E_q'-f_q'E_q=f_q^2 zeta'`, so the proposed nonvanishing is exactly
    Speiser's RH equivalent; a positive atomic Laplace transform has explicit
    nonzero critical points in `Re(s)>0`.  (ii) The Quillen compression anomaly
    is nonnegative, but its derivative is sign-indefinite: the exact rank-one
    model gives `R(a)=log cosh(a)` and `R'(a)=tanh(a)`, reversible at any base
    point.  Only its curvature is signed, returning the wrong quadratic
    tensor.  (iii) A three-state finite model has strict OS reflection
    positivity, positive weights, charge self-duality, an entire determinant,
    and partition function `1+2a cosh(z)`; for `a=9/20` its zeros give an
    explicit off-axis quartet inside the completed critical strip.  Thus
    reflection positivity plus self-duality is insufficient without an
    independent Lee--Yang stability theorem.
24. **Direct localized-Weil ground-state transform (closed).**  On the exact
    support subsystem `{0,log2,log3,log6}`, the full off-diagonal coefficient
    at separation `log6` is
    `2cosh(log6/2)-J(log6)=209/(35sqrt6)>0`, because `Lambda(6)=0` and
    `J(d)=exp(-d/2)/(1-exp(-2d))`.  Positive conjugation cannot change this to
    the nonpositive off-diagonal sign of a Dirichlet form.  On the pole-neutral
    core the rank-two pole term vanishes, but no strictly positive ground state
    lies in that core since its cosh moment is positive.  Thus the direct
    Perron transform is impossible; the constrained pole-free inequality is
    not refuted.
25. **Constraint-penalty ground-state transform (closed as posed).**  The
    advertised four-point off-diagonal gate is exactly feasible, but vacuous:
    with `u=(C,S)`, the multiplier `diag(-2,2)` cancels the full pole kernel
    `2 u^T diag(1,-1)u` for every width and leaves precisely the nonpositive
    pole-free jump distribution.  Equivalently, starting from `A_tilde`, the
    zero penalty already passes every off-diagonal sign test.  There is no
    exact point-sampled `4 by 4` diagonal balance: `J(t)~1/(2|t|)` is singular
    on the diagonal and prime shifts are delta distributions.  A legitimate
    compression requires specified `L2` basis functions and overlap integrals.
    The exact cancellation recovers only the already-known pole-free operator;
    its potential is negative and Perron structure does not give a
    nonnegative lowest eigenvalue.  Any further coefficient-side penalty plus
    positive supersolution valid for all widths would prove constrained Weil
    positivity, whose all-width statement is RH-equivalent.  Finite
    feasibility supplies no form convergence, domain identification, or
    infinite bridge, so this mechanism yields neither a proof nor a disproof.

    The requested three-variable elimination is complete.  Put `L=log 2` and,
    after pole cancellation, write the residual multiplier as
    `M=[[a,b],[b,c]]`.  All six four-point inequalities are
    `3a+b<=8/3+2L`, `2a+b<=9/8+log 3`,
    `7a+5b<=72/35`, `6a+5b+c<=36/5`,
    `21a+22b+5c<=9+8log 3`, and
    `14a+17b+5c<=8+6L`.  Only the first, third, and sixth are facets.  The
    complete feasible set is
    `a in R`,
    `b<=min(8/3+2L-3a,(72/35-7a)/5)`, and
    `c<=(8+6L-14a-17b)/5`.  Equivalently it is the vertex cone
    `v+r0(0,0,-1)+r1(5,-15,37)+r2(-25,35,-49)`, `rj>=0`, where
    `v=(148/105+5L/4,-164/105-7L/4,1556/525+73L/20)`.
    Direct rational-coefficient substitution verifies the three active
    equalities and that every recession ray decreases all six left sides.

    The diagonal request is also closed exactly: no diagonal exists on this
    delta-point carrier.  `J(0)` diverges, prime shifts are distributions, and
    Dirac point masses are outside `L2` and the form domain.  For any legitimate
    basis `phi_i`, the actual matrix is
    `A_ij=A_tilde(phi_j,phi_i)` with constraint rows
    `U_i=(<C,phi_i>,<S,phi_i>)`; only then is the balance condition
    `(A+U M U^T)h>=0`, `h_i>0`, defined.  It depends on the chosen support,
    basis/cells, and `h`, so attaching a manufactured point diagonal to the six
    inequalities is invalid rather than an unfinished computation.
26. **Pole-neutral differential pullback (closed).**  With
    `P=D^2+1/4=-d^2/dx^2+1/4`, convolution by `exp(-|x|/2)` proves that `P` is
    a bijection from compactly supported smooth functions onto the tests whose
    `exp(+/-x/2)` moments vanish.  Thus `Q_W(Pf)>=0` for all `f` is exactly RH,
    not a smaller criterion.  The pullback is fully typed on the domain
    `{f in H_0^2: Pf in D(Q_W)}`.  It leaves the prime block sign-indefinite:
    on `{phi,T_l phi}` a single shift has eigenvalues
    `+/-a||Pphi||^2`.  The digamma recurrence also exposes the negative local
    term `-||f'||^2-||f||^2/4`, and the remaining archimedean multiplier is
    negative at zero.  Therefore the differential structure supplies no
    independent positive factorization.
27. **Matrix-valued ground-state transform (closed).**  For
    `U=(cosh(x/2),sinh(x/2))`, the natural two-component Picone conductance has
    determinant
    `-J(x-y)^2 sinh((x-y)/2)^2/4`; on the exact `1`--`6` edge this is
    `-9/196`.  A full-rank PSD completion
    `G=[[C,S],[S,C]]` yields an exact matrix jump square but leaves the
    remainder `G^T(A_tilde G)`.  Prime-2 overlap gives explicit nonzero jumps
    in both `A_tilde C` and `A_tilde S`, so the remainder does not vanish;
    controlling it is the original constrained nonlocal Poincare inequality.
28. **Krein/capacity scalar route (closed as an independent mechanism).**  For parity
    restrictions of `T=A_tilde`, put
    `m_C=<C,T_e^{-1}C>` and `m_S=<S,T_o^{-1}S>`.  Conditional on exactly one
    negative even eigenvalue and a nonnegative odd block, the rank-two update
    is nonnegative precisely when `m_C<=-1/2` and `m_S<=1/2`, with
    Moore--Penrose orthogonality conditions at zero modes.  These inertia
    hypotheses are not consequences of Perron simplicity.  Exact nested
    irreducible `Z`-matrix controls cross both capacity thresholds while
    preserving Perron ground-state positivity and Herglotz resolvents; another
    nested family has arbitrarily many negative eigenvalues.  For the actual
    operator, prime thresholds give
    `m_X'=2a_n Re integral u_X(x+l)conj(u_X(x))dx`, whose sign is exactly the
    missing nodal theorem, and `T C,T S` acquire prime-step jumps so no `2 by 2`
    closure exists.  Support monotonicity assumes constrained positivity.
    Hence the scalar reduction is exact but the inertia and endpoint bounds
    are themselves the all-width Weil-strength input.
29. **Global divisor-path Schur complement (closed).**  Prime paths on
    `{1,2,3,6}` generate conductance
    `2ab/(a+b)` with `a=log2/sqrt2`, `b=log3/sqrt3`, strictly below the missing
    `209/(35sqrt6)` edge.  Generally every divisor prime-path network has
    terminal-cut capacity `<log n`, whereas the residual pole demand is
    `sqrt(n)-1/(sqrt(n)(n^2-1))>log n` for `n>=6`.  A six-point graph including
    sampled archimedean edges appears to pass, with certified conductance
    `3.24116928829>7/sqrt6`, but this is a sampling artifact: arch energy is
    two-dimensional and scales with cell width, point evaluation is unbounded,
    and the point graph omits the exact prime diagonal cancellation and the
    negative potential.  Restoring the true prime block makes the optimistic
    Schur terminal matrix indefinite.  Thus no legitimate continuum
    compression or defeat of the `N=8` separator is obtained.
30. **Translation-frame/uncertainty inequality (closed as an independent
    mechanism).**  For zero extension of a pole-neutral test `v`, put
    `R(l)=integral v(x+l)conj(v(x))dx`, `F=v_hat`, and
    `E(x)=psi_Chebyshev(x)-x`.  The two exact moment identities imply
    `integral_0^infinity[e^(l/2)R(l)+e^(-l/2)conj(R(l))]dl=0`.  Combining this
    with the digamma recurrence gives the exact cancellation formula
    `Q_W(v)=(1/(2pi)) integral B(t)|F(t)|^2dt
      - integral_[1-,infinity] x^(-1/2)[R(log x)+R(-log x)]dE(x)`,
    where
    `B(t)=Re digamma(5/4+it/2)-log pi`.  Thus the Chebyshev main term cancels
    the `-1/(t^2+1/4)` gamma atom; it does not supply positive coercivity.
    Integration by parts makes the residual threshold exact: for a family of
    growing support widths, an estimate `E(x)=O(x^theta polylog(x))` is
    uniformly integrable at precisely `theta<1/2`, with the boundary case
    requiring corresponding logarithmic control.  This is square-root PNT
    strength and hence cannot be inserted as an independent input.  The
    remainder has no fixed sign and `B(0)<0`.

    Every fixed finite-prime version is false.  If `g_L` is one on `[-L,L]`
    with fixed smooth boundary layers and `v_L=(D^2-1/4)g_L`, then both pole
    moments vanish exactly, `||v_L||_2^2=L/8+O(1)`, while the `J` energy and
    each fixed-shift energy are `O(1)`.  Embedding in width `a_L=L^2`, the
    normalized truncated potential tends a strictly negative constant.
    Hence no fixed set of translations has the proposed frame gap.  Allowing
    all prime powers avoids this quasimode only through the signed `dE`
    remainder above, which is the original zero-sensitive content rather than
    an independent uncertainty principle.

    A second exact plateau construction at width `a` strengthens the
    falsifier.  With `v_a=(-d^2+1/4)f_a`, where `f_a=1` away from fixed-width
    boundary layers, the two moments again vanish exactly, the continuous
    jump energy is `O(1)`, and a shift of length `ell` costs `O(1+ell)`.
    Meanwhile the omitted prime potential is `>>a exp(a/2)` by Chebyshev's
    unconditional `psi(X)>>X`.  Thus even growing selected sets satisfying
    `sum_(n in S_a) Lambda(n)n^(-1/2)(1+log n)=o(a exp(a/2))` cannot prove the
    full bound.  With all shifts, diagonal cancellation leaves exactly
    `-2 integral A_v(ell)[dmu(ell)-2cosh(ell/2)dell]`, because pole neutrality
    gives `integral_0^(2a)2cosh(ell/2)A_v(ell)dell=0`.  No sparse-prime or
    boundary loophole remains.

31. **Adelic entropy tensorization (closed).**  At one prime,
    `d^2/dsigma^2 log(1-p^(-sigma))^(-1)
      =sum_(k>=1)k(log p)^2p^(-ksigma)>0`, whereas the Weil coefficient at
    `p^k` is `(log p)p^(-k/2)`; their ratio `k log p` cannot be removed by one
    prime-coordinate rescaling.  More fundamentally, a reversible/Fisher
    Hessian gives the positive jump block `D_n=2c||v||^2-2cR_v(log n)`, while
    the Weil strain is `P_n=-2cR_v(log n)=D_n-2c||v||^2`.  The missing
    diagonal is strictly concave and has full rank, so conditioning on the two
    pole moments (a rank-at-most-two PSD Schur correction) cannot create it.

    This mismatch holds on the exact smooth pole-neutral core.  For
    `n=9`, `ell=log 9`, centers `x_j=(j-3/2)ell`, coefficients
    `u=(-3,7,7,-3)`, and identical disjoint smooth bumps `phi_j`, the sums
    `sum u_j exp(+/-x_j/2)` vanish exactly.  Thus
    `v=sum u_j phi_j` is pole-neutral and
    `R_v(ell)=7||phi||^2`, so
    `P_9=-(14 log 3/3)||phi||^2<0`, while the full-line reversible Hessian is
    `(218 log 3/3)||phi||^2>0`.

    The natural restricted Euler-product probability also does not exist at
    the critical exponent: for independent geometric exponents,
    `P(K_p>0)=p^(-1/2)` and `sum_p p^(-1/2)=infinity`, so Borel--Cantelli puts
    infinitely many active primes almost surely and the logarithmic coordinate
    diverges.  The `exp(x/2)` moment is finite only from `sigma>3/2`, not at
    `sigma=1/2`.

    Independent reciprocal control:
    `Z(T)=((1-2T)(1-4T))/((1-T)(1-8T))` has positive orbit coefficients
    `N_m=(2^m-1)(4^m-1)`, strictly convex Gibbs log partition, and exact
    reciprocity `Z(1/(8T))=Z(T)`, but normalized spectral values
    `1/sqrt(2),sqrt(2)` are off the critical circle.  Every nondegenerate
    duality form for `F=diag(2,4)`, `F^T H F=8H`, has
    `H=[[0,h],[h,0]]` and determinant `-h^2`.  Local convexity, tensorization,
    and reciprocity therefore do not supply a positive global polarization.
    A minimal one-site probability control is the symmetric three-state law
    with weights `(9,20,9)/38`: its entropy Hessian is positive, while its
    reciprocal zero polynomial `9w^2+20w+9` has reciprocal roots off the unit
    circle and the test `F(w)=9w+10` gives reciprocal Weil pairing `-38`.
32. **Nonlocal Sturm/total-positive resolvent (closed).**  The actual
    archimedean jump kernel is not TP2.  Since
    `J(t)=sum_(k>=0)exp(-(2k+1/2)t)`, `log J` is strictly convex.  At row
    centers `0,log(3/2)` and column centers `log(5/2),log(15/4)`, avoiding all
    prime-power shifts, its exact minor is
    `J(log(5/2))^2-J(log(5/3))J(log(15/4))=-78125/737352<0`.
    Identical sufficiently narrow nonnegative smooth bumps preserve the
    negative minor, kill potential cross terms, and avoid prime overlaps.
    The large-resolvent expansion
    `M^2<f,(T_a+M)^(-1)g> -> -<f,T_ag>` for disjoint supports transfers this
    to a legitimate negative Green-kernel TP2 minor for all large `M`.
    Positivity improvement remains true, but variation diminution is false.
    Moreover, even an STP Green kernel alone would not locate the spectral
    origin: `-d^2/dx^2-c` retains Sturm kernels while acquiring arbitrarily
    many negative eigenvalues as `c` grows.
33. **One-prime Sonin--Weil same-coordinate comparison P3-C0b (inherited
    partial reduction; convention repaired; otherwise parked).**  The existing
    `logs/atlas-kill-campaigns/p3-c0b/EVIDENCE.md` had already derived the
    common-coordinate resolvent reduction and killed the false identification
    of the Groskin `M_0` endpoint row with Mellin zero (`v_0=0`).  A new audit
    found that its preregistered polarization had its arguments reversed.
    With the source convention that `Q(g,h)` is anti-linear in `g` and linear
    in `h`, the written
    `Tr(M_g P_2 M_h^*)` has the opposite convention; for `g=iV_0,h=V_0` the
    mismatch is exact.  The correctly typed trace is
    `T(g,h)=Tr(M_h P_2 M_g^*)`.

    For reference, the inherited repaired entries are as follows.  Put `L=log 3`,
    `h_n(s)=2sin(Ls/2)/(sqrt(L)(s-2pi n/L))`,
    `d(s)=1-2^(-1/2-is)`, let `M` be the classical Sonin Mellin space with
    projection `P`, and set `G=P|d|^2P|_M`.  If `k_s` is its evaluation vector,
    then
    `T_(2,nm)=integral conj(h_n)h_m |d|^2 <G^(-1)k_s,k_s> ds`.
    The missing `sqrt(pi)` in the recorded amplification cancels from the
    range projection but is now accounted for in the unitary map.  With
    `q_nm(y)` the exact divided-sine kernel,
    `Q_(2,nm)=W_(0,2;nm)-W_(R;nm)-(log2/sqrt2)q_nm(log2)`, where
    `W_(0,2;nm)=32L sinh^2(L/4)(L^2-16pi^2mn)/
      ((L^2+16pi^2m^2)(L^2+16pi^2n^2))` and
    `W_(R;nm)=delta_nm(gamma+log(2pi))+
      integral_0^L e^(y/2)(q_nm(y)-2delta_nm)/(e^y-e^(-y))dy`.
    Thus `E_(2,nm)=Q_(2,nm)-T_(2,nm)` is an exact same-coordinate reduction;
    `q_nm(L)=0` proves that support introduces prime 2 and no prime 3 term.
    It still contains the unevaluated infinite compressed inverse
    `<G^(-1)k_s,k_s>` and therefore does not pass the campaign's P3-C0b reopen
    condition.  No finite `T_N` or `E_N` may be inferred from existing `Q_N`
    certificates.  The only active check is whether a genuinely new analytic
    operator inequality can control this inverse without assuming the desired
    sign; absent that, the route remains parked.

    The exact compressed-inverse remainder is now exposed.  Put
    `a=2^(-1/2)`, `b=log 2`, `U=M_(exp(-ibs))`, `V=U+U^*`,
    `D=I-aU`, `B=D^*D=(1+a^2)I-aV`, `Q=I-P`,
    `G=PBP|_M`, and `W=M_(|h|^2)`.  Then, with all products trace class,
    `T_2=Tr_M(G^(-1)PWBP)`, `T_infinity=Tr_M(PWP)`, and exactly
    `Delta T=T_2-T_infinity=Tr_M(G^(-1)PWQBP)
      =-(a/2)Tr_M(G^(-1)P(WQV+VQW)P)`.
    Equivalently, if `H=PWP`, `A=PVP`, and `J=PWVP`, then
    `Delta T=-a Tr_M(G^(-1)(J-(HA+AH)/2))`.  Hence the repaired comparison is
    `E_2=W_(0,2)-epsilon-ab q_g(b)
      +(a/2)Tr_M(G^(-1)P(WQV+VQW)P)`.
    This is a signed complement excursion, not a positive compression.

    Generic projection structure cannot supply its sign.  In `C^2`, take
    `U=diag(1,-1)`, project onto `(1,1)/sqrt(2)`, and use either
    `W_-=diag(1,0)` or `W_+=diag(0,1)`.  Both weights are positive and commute
    with `U`, while for `D_t=I-tU` the trace differences are respectively
    `-t/(1+t^2)` and `+t/(1+t^2)`.  At `t=a`, even the uncompressed prime
    correction has the wrong sign for `W_-`:
    `-ab Tr(W_-V)-Delta T(W_-)=sqrt(2)(1/3-log 2)<0`.
    This is not a counterexample to the actual Sonin remainder; it proves that
    any reopen now requires a Sonin-specific inequality for
    `Tr(G^(-1)(J-{H,A}/2))`, rather than positivity of the projection,
    commutation of `W` with `U`, or a Neumann expansion.

    The dyadic Feshbach attempt is also closed exactly.  With `W=D^*D` and
    `K=W^(-1)`, bounded block Gaussian elimination on `PH direct-sum QH`
    gives
    `(PWP)^(-1)=PW^(-1)P-PW^(-1)Q(QW^(-1)Q)^(-1)QW^(-1)P`.
    Moreover
    `W^(-1)=2[I+sum_(k>=1)2^(-k/2)(U^k+U^(-k))]` in operator norm.  For the
    Sonin complement `Q=A_1 join B_1`, dyadic conjugation gives
    `U^k Q U^(-k)=A_(2^k) join B_(2^(-k))`: time support grows while frequency
    support shrinks, so these joins are not nested.  The time-cutoff block is
    triangular in one orientation and the frequency-cutoff block in the
    opposite orientation; the two-sided self-adjoint series destroys both.
    Already `C=A_1UA_1` fails to commute with the prolate differential operator
    `L=-d/dx((1-x^2)d/dx)+(2pi x)^2`, since
    `[L,C]f(x)=2^(-1/2)[(3/4)f''(x/2)+3pi^2x^2f(x/2)]`.
    Thus `QW^(-1)Q` is neither block diagonal nor block triangular in the
    canonical prolate-pair decomposition.

    The Schur correction is an exact positive square and yields only
    `0<(PWP)^(-1)<=PW^(-1)P`; in a diagonal trace it is subtracted as the
    Hilbert--Schmidt norm square of
    `(QW^(-1)Q)^(-1/2)QW^(-1)PD^*M_h^*`.  An exact five-dimensional
    two-projection model couples two different canonical prolate blocks and
    makes this correction `16/3` against a leading corner `6`, leaving
    `(PWP)^(-1)=2/3`.  Hence neither the prolate angles nor dyadic nesting
    determine or perturbatively suppress the full cross-scale inverse.
34. **Support-width crossing induction (closed).**  Zero extension from
    `H_a` to `H_b`, `b>a`, preserves both norm and the localized Weil value,
    so the ground value `mu(b)<=mu(a)` unconditionally.  Every differentiable
    ground branch therefore has `mu'(a)<=0`, the opposite of the positive
    derivative needed to preclude a first downward crossing.  At an even or
    odd rank-one zero, respectively `m_C=-1/2` or `m_S=1/2`, exact
    differentiation gives `mu'=-m_X'/||T^(-1)X||^2`; the desired sign is just
    the missing capacity monotonicity, and scalar one-dimensional controls
    cross with `m_X'(0)=1/4>0` in both parities.

    The prime thresholds make the conflict explicit.  At
    `a_0=(log n)/2`, the smooth-core right derivative gains
    `-4c_n Re(h(a_0)conj(h(-a_0)))`: it is negative for even modes and
    positive for odd modes.  All other derivative terms (pole and shifted
    autocorrelation derivatives) are sign-indefinite, while the
    archimedean derivative is nonpositive.  Moreover, the closed form domain
    has only `integral log(2+|t|)|F(t)|^2<infinity`; endpoint traces and the
    classical Hadamard formula are not defined without an extra regularity
    theorem.  Thus any positive zero-crossing derivative can hold only
    vacuously after assuming the all-width nondegeneracy it was meant to prove.
35. **Centered-prime positive transport (closed as an independent
    mechanism).**  The required lemma would factor the signed measure
    `dnu=sum_n Lambda(n)n^(-1/2)delta_(log n)-2cosh(ell/2)dell`
    into elementary positive second-difference or martingale transports whose
    cost is bounded by the complete archimedean form for every pole-neutral
    autocorrelation.  Its exact dual cone is the Fourier-positive Weil cone:
    `v_R=(-d^2+1/4)(R^(-1/2)chi(x/R)e^(itx))` has both moments zero exactly and
    concentrates its spectral mass at any chosen real `t`, so any negative
    dual multiplier gives a compactly supported exact falsifier.

    Positive translation squares again retain the degree diagonal canceled by
    the prime potential; two constraints cannot remove that infinite-rank
    multiplier.  The cumulative obstruction is also exact.  If
    `V(L)=nu([0,L])`, `x=e^L`, and `E(x)=psi(x)-x`, then
    `V(log x)=-1+2/sqrt(x)+E(x)/sqrt(x)
       +(1/2)integral_1^x E(u)u^(-3/2)du`.
    Conversely, Stieltjes inversion shows that even
    `V(log x)=O((log x)^B)` implies
    `psi(x)-x=O(sqrt(x)(log x)^B)`, hence zero-freeness for `Re(s)>1/2` and RH
    by symmetry.  Finally, integrating against `V` requires control of
    `A_v'`, while the `J` energy grows only logarithmically in frequency and
    cannot control that derivative.  The proposed transport therefore either
    has the wrong diagonal or assumes square-root-PNT strength.
36. **Localized-Weil real-zero ground plus prolate limit (live; new bridge,
    inherited ground-order obstruction).**  Two November 2025 primary sources
    give a proof chain not previously recorded in the repository.  Let
    `H_lambda=L2([lambda^(-1),lambda],du/u)` and let `A_lambda` be the
    compact-resolvent self-adjoint operator of the localized Weil form.  Let
    `k_lambda=E(h_lambda)` be the prolate educated guess of
    Connes--Consani--Moscovici, normalized so that `h_lambda -> h`, where the
    Mellin transform of `k=E(h)` is the Riemann `Xi` function.

    The exact sufficient lemma needs only a sequence `lambda_j -> infinity`.
    Its ground eigenvalue must be simple with an inversion-even unit
    eigenfunction `xi_j`, and there must be nonzero scalars `c_j` such that,
    for every `0<a<1/2`,
    `|c_j|^(-1) integral_[lambda_j^(-1),lambda_j]
       |xi_j-c_j k_(lambda_j)| max(u^a,u^(-a)) du/u -> 0`.  The 2025
    distributional Caratheodory--Fejer theorem then proves that every zero of
    the centered Fourier/Mellin transform of `xi_j` is real.  The displayed
    weighted estimate transfers the proved compact-substrip convergence
    `khat_lambda -> Xi` to these real-zero transforms.  Hurwitz on disks in
    either component of `{|Im z|<1/2}\R` excludes every nonreal zero of `Xi`.
    Since a nontrivial zeta zero `rho=beta+i gamma` corresponds to
    `z_rho=gamma+i(1/2-beta)`, this chain proves RH.

    The clean unweighted `L2` sufficient rate is exact up to constants:
    `lambda_j^a ||xi_j-c_j k_(lambda_j)||_2/|c_j| -> 0` for every
    `a<1/2`, because the evaluation norm squared on `|Im z|<=a` is
    `(lambda^(2a)-lambda^(-2a))/(2a)`.  Plain `L2` convergence is not enough:
    `e_lambda=lambda^(-2a)(u^a+u^(-a))` is inversion-even with norm tending to
    zero while its `u^a`-weighted integral stays bounded away from zero.  A
    relative `O(lambda^(-1/2))` `L2` estimate would suffice on every closed
    substrip.

    Source repairs already audited: the real-zero theorem's proof needs only
    the established form core, not the stronger operator-core wording; its
    finite approximants are exactly even because their simple ground
    projections commute with reflection, not merely because they are close to
    the even subspace; subtracting the finite minimum preserves the required
    matrix class by adding a `delta_0` diagonal; and the printed proof of the
    prolate Mellin limit omits the Gaussian tail `n>lambda/u`, whose uniform
    contribution is `O(lambda^5 exp(-pi lambda^2))`.  Endpoint normalization
    `xi_lambda(lambda)=1` is unjustified on the logarithmic form domain, so the
    continuation uses unit norm and free scalars `c_j`.

    The currently exact perturbative gate is much stronger than a small
    residual.  For normalized `k_lambda`, Rayleigh value `r_lambda`, ground
    gap `Delta_lambda`, and residual `eta_lambda`, spectral projection gives
    `dist(k_lambda,C xi_lambda)^2 <=
       (r_lambda-mu_0(lambda))/Delta_lambda`, or
    `dist(k_lambda,C xi_lambda)<=eta_lambda/d_lambda`, where `d_lambda` is
    the distance from `r_lambda` to the rest of the spectrum.  Transform
    closure therefore needs the first ratio to be `o(lambda^(-2a))`, or the
    residual ratio to be `o(lambda^(-a))`, for every `a<1/2`.  It must also
    prove that the selected near-zero branch is the global ground.  The
    prolate-wave spectral gap is irrelevant unless `E` is proved to
    intertwine that operator with `A_lambda`; it currently does not.  A bare
    residual cannot defeat the inherited shrinking localized-Weil gaps or a
    hypothetical fixed negative test supplied by an off-line zeta zero.

    There is nevertheless a new exact near-zero theorem which removes the
    residual half of the conjecture.  In additive coordinates put
    `K(x)=E(h)(exp x)`, so `K` is even, `Khat` is a nonzero constant multiple
    of `Xi`, and
    `|K^(j)(x)|<=C_j exp(C_j|x|-pi exp(2|x|))`.  For `a>0`, let
    `P_a=1_[-a,a]`, `K_a=P_aK`, and `T_a=(I-P_a)K`.  The zero side of the
    explicit formula gives `QW(K,phi)=0` unconditionally for every smooth
    compactly supported `phi`, because `Khat` vanishes at every nontrivial
    zeta zero.  Consequently
    `<A_a K_a,phi>=-QW(T_a,phi)`.

    The latter cross-form is represented by an `L2([-a,a])` vector and obeys
    the rigorous bound
    `||A_aK_a||_2 <= C exp(Ca-c exp(2a))`.  For the archimedean term,
    integration by parts gives `|That_a(t)|<=D_a min(1,|t|^(-1))`, while the
    symbol is `O(log(2+|t|))`; the pole moments have the same double-
    exponential tail.  For the prime terms, split at `n=exp(2a)`:
    `sum_(n<=exp(2a)) Lambda(n)/sqrt(n)<=C a exp(a)` multiplies only the tail
    beyond `a`, and the remaining shifts are bounded by a Gaussian sum in
    `n exp(-a)`.  Both are absorbed by the displayed `D_a`.  Thus `K_a` is in
    the operator domain and the localized even spectrum contains a level at
    distance at most `C exp(Ca-c exp(2a))` from zero.  This uses no global
    `L2` Weil operator and no RH assumption.

    In fact the near-zero level is an arbitrarily large cluster.  For each
    `j>=0`, set `K_j=(-1)^j K^(2j)`, so
    `Khat_j(t)=t^(2j)Xi(t)`.  Every `K_j` is even, double-exponentially
    decaying, and in the full Weil radical; its truncation satisfies the same
    residual estimate with constants depending on `j`.  For fixed `m`, the
    first `m` such vectors are linearly independent and their truncated Gram
    matrix converges to a positive-definite limit.  Min--max applied to
    `A_a^2` therefore proves that, for some
    `D_(m,a)=C_m exp(C_m a-c_m exp(2a))`, the even operator has at least `m`
    eigenvalues in `[-D_(m,a),D_(m,a)]`.  Thus no uniform isolation of the
    `Xi` branch can be inferred from the exact residual; the relevant
    separations collapse on the same scale.

    The exact theorem also isolates the unresolved lower-bound obstruction.
    If RH is false, Weil's criterion supplies a fixed compact test `phi` with
    `QW(phi)<0`; one of its parity components has normalized value `-eta<0`.
    Zero extension then gives the corresponding parity ground
    `mu_tau(a)<=-eta` for every sufficiently large `a`, while `K_a` remains a
    super-accurate even near-zero excited branch.  Conversely, proving along
    any `a_j->infinity` that this near-zero level is the global ground gives
    `A_(a_j)>=r_j I` with `r_j->0`; applying this to every fixed compact test
    and passing to the limit proves Weil positivity and hence RH.  Therefore
    the ground-order part is already RH-strength and is not supplied by the
    new residual theorem.

    The advertised prolate perturbation does not repair this order problem.
    If `h_lambda=c_0p_(0,lambda)+c_4p_(4,lambda)`, the limiting squared weights
    are `3/11` and `8/11`.  For the best scalar `r`, its prolate residual is
    `sqrt(24)/11 |nu_4-nu_0|`, asymptotic to a positive constant times
    `lambda^2`, rather than small.  Only its Fourier-concentration defect is
    exponentially small; there is no intertwining `A_lambda E=E PW_lambda`.
    The proved `h_lambda-h=O(lambda^(-2))` yields only
    `||k_lambda-P_(log lambda)K||_2=O(lambda^(-1/2))`.  This is sufficient for
    the already-proved closed-substrip convergence of the trial transform, but
    gives no estimate at all between that trial and a selected eigenvector of
    `A_lambda`.
37. **First-crossing real-zero exclusion (closed as an independent
    reinforcement).**  The 2025 real-zero ground theorem does not by itself
    prevent a localized form from crossing zero.  Fix `c>0` and use the single
    real distribution `D_c=(1/2)delta_0-c dx` on `H_L=L2([0,L])` in the same
    convolution construction.  Exactly
    `Q_L(f,g)=<f,g>-c overline(integral f) integral g`, so, for
    `e_L=L^(-1/2)1`,
    `A_L=I-cL|e_L><e_L|`.  At `L=1/c` this is positive semidefinite with a
    simple isolated even kernel, while it is positive before and indefinite
    after.  The centered kernel transform is
    `2sin(Lz/2)/(sqrt(L)z)` and all of its zeros are real.  Thus the complete
    Caratheodory--Fejer interlacing conclusion is exactly compatible with a
    first crossing, even for a fixed distribution and a legitimate operator
    core.

    The explicit zero side supplies no missing termwise sign.  For real even
    `xi` with transform `F`, an off-line quartet contributes
    `4m Re(F(z)^2)`, not an absolute square.  The real-rooted Paley--Wiener
    function `F_A(z)=sqrt(2/A) sin(Az)/z` gives an exact counterexample: with
    `x=pi/(2A)` and `x<y<1/2`,
    `Re(F_A(x+iy)^2)=(2/A)cosh^2(Ay)(x^2-y^2)/(x^2+y^2)^2<0`.
    Its zero-sum quartet grouping is absolutely convergent because it is
    `O(1/|Re z|)` on the closed critical strip and `N(T)=O(T log T)`.

    One actual-zeta consequence survives but does not close the sign.  A
    nonzero real-rooted transform cannot vanish at `i/2`; for an even kernel
    this says `<cosh(x/2),xi>!=0`.  With
    `Q_W=A_tilde+2|C><C|-2|S><S|`, the kernel equation gives
    `A_tilde xi=-2<C,xi>C` and
    `<A_tilde xi,xi>=-2|<C,xi>|^2<0`.  Hence such a crossing cannot be
    pole-neutral, but full localized-Weil positivity does not imply the
    pole-free positivity needed to contradict it.  A genuinely arithmetic
    cancellation/uniqueness theorem remains necessary.

38. **Resolvent square and boundary-correlation bridge (closed).**  There is
    an exact finite theorem, but it produces the wrong continuum object.  Let
    `A` be a real symmetric special matrix with
    `[D,A]=|beta><eta|-|eta><beta|`, let `xi_k` be a simple even eigenvector,
    and take `lambda_(k-1)<z<lambda_k`.  With
    `R=(A-zI)^(-1)` and
    `Q_z=-R+(lambda_k-z)^(-1)I`, spectral calculus gives
    `Q_z>=0`, `ker Q_z=C xi_k`, and
    `[D,Q_z]=|R beta><R eta|-|R eta><R beta|` (up to the harmless ordering
    convention).  Put `u=R eta`.  Along a sequence `z -> lambda_k` avoiding
    the finitely many coordinate zeros of `u`, the real diagonal congruence
    `S=diag(1/u_j)` makes `S Q_z S` another positive special matrix and its
    kernel is `u odot xi_k`.

    Simplicity and evenness force `c=<eta,xi_k>!=0`: otherwise the commutator
    equation makes `D xi_k` an independent odd eigenvector at `lambda_k`
    (the exceptional constant vector has nonzero overlap).  Hence
    `u=c(lambda_k-z)^(-1)xi_k+O(1)` and, after normalization, the new kernel
    converges in every finite norm to `xi_k odot xi_k`.  The resulting
    transform has only real zeros, but this is automatic and contains no
    eigenvalue information: for `c_j=xi_j^2>=0`,
    `Im sum_j c_j/(w-b_j)=-(Im w)sum_j c_j/|w-b_j|^2`, so the Cauchy sum has
    no nonreal zero.

    It gives no control of the original eigenvector transform.  The exact
    special matrix
    `A=[[0,1,1],[1,2,1],[1,1,0]]`, with nodes `(-1,0,1)`, has eigenvalues
    `(-1,0,3)` and simple even zero eigenvector `xi=(1,-1,1)`.  Its Cauchy
    numerator is `w^2+1`, with zeros `+/-i`, whereas the coefficientwise
    square has numerator `3w^2-1` and real zeros.  Thus no converse survives.

    More decisively, coefficientwise squaring is a circular autocorrelation
    at the interval seam.  For
    `U_n=L^(-1/2)exp(2 pi i n x/L)` and a centered even
    `xi(x)=K_L(x-L/2)`, one has
    `xi_n=(-1)^n L^(-1/2) Khat_L(2 pi n/L)`.  If
    `g_L=sum xi_n^2 U_n`, then exactly
    `g_L=L^(-1/2)P_L(K_L*K_L)`, centered at `x=0=L`, while the desired
    convolution centered at `L/2` has coefficients `(-1)^n xi_n^2`.
    In centered coordinates,
    `sqrt(L) g_L^sigma(y)=sum_m (K_L*K_L)(y+L/2+mL)`, which tends to zero on
    fixed compact sets.  The alternating recentering factor destroys the
    positive-residue/standard-cyclic-vector structure.

    For compactly supported even `h=K*K`, the centered zero-extension has
    the exact transform
    `G_L(z)=exp(iLz/2)H_+(z)+exp(-iLz/2)H_+(-z)`,
    `H_+(z)=integral_0^R h(t)exp(-izt)dt`, whereas
    `hhat(z)=H_+(z)+H_+(-z)=Khat(z)^2`.  The exponential factors preclude
    compact-local convergence in either half-plane and oscillate on the real
    axis.  The double-exponential Riemann kernel obeys the same identity up
    to vanishing tails.  Consequently Hurwitz cannot transfer the finite
    real-zero conclusion to `Xi^2`; this resolvent mechanism does not reopen
    the localized-Weil route.

    Nor can separate half-plane normalization be upgraded to a
    Hermite--Biehler bridge.  An exact smooth control is
    `K(x)=exp(-x^2)+sqrt(2)exp(-2x^2)>0`, periodized on a circle of length
    `2a` and centered at `a`.  Its circular autocorrelation is the
    periodization of the strictly positive Gaussian mixture `C=K*K`.  With
    `H_a(z)=integral_0^a C_a(u)exp(izu)du`, its centered seam transform obeys
    exactly
    `G_a(z)=exp(-iaz)H_a(z)+exp(iaz)H_a(-z)` and `H_a -> H` locally uniformly
    on `C`, with Gaussian error.  Thus `exp(iaz)G_a -> H` only in the upper
    half-plane and `exp(-iaz)G_a -> H(-z)` only in the lower half-plane.
    Every finite coefficient truncation of `G_a` is real-rooted by the strict
    Cauchy/Herglotz sign, and the infinite sum inherits that fact.

    Nevertheless `H(z)+H(-z)=Khat(z)^2` has an explicit nonreal zero.  In the
    stated normalization
    `Khat(z)=sqrt(pi)exp(-z^2/8)(1+exp(-z^2/8))`, which vanishes at
    `z=2sqrt(pi)(1+i)`.  At the same point `H` is nonzero and
    `|H#|=|H|`, so the strict Hermite--Biehler inequality fails.  Separate
    one-sided Herglotz limits therefore cannot glue into real-rootedness of
    the full autocorrelation without a new inequality of the missing
    strength.

39. **Special-cluster eigenline selection (closed without fixed-zeta
    compatibility).**  The finite divided-difference/rank-two identity, exact
    parity, simple ordering, and even weighted approximation of all the
    Riemann-kernel derivative trials do not select a nonescaping eigenline.
    An exact family makes this sharp.  For `a -> infinity`, put
    `N=ceil(exp(a^2))`, `M=2N+1`,
    `epsilon=exp(-exp(3a))`, `tau=epsilon M^(-40)`, and
    `r_k=epsilon(1+k/(N+1))`.  On indices `-N,...,N`, set
    `A=diag(r_|i|)+tau|eta><eta|`.  Then for `D=diag(i)` and
    `beta_i=tau i`, exactly
    `[D,A]=|beta><eta|-|eta><beta|`; parity is exact.

    In the even basis the matrix is
    `diag(r_0,...,r_N)+tau w w^T`, `w=(1,sqrt(2),...,sqrt(2))`.
    The secular equation strictly interlaces its simple eigenvalues with the
    `r_k`.  After subtracting its simple even ground, the matrix is positive
    special and every even eigenvalue lies in `[0,2epsilon]`.  The gap of the
    diagonal is `epsilon/(N+1)` while the perturbation norm is
    `epsilon M^(-39)`, so every normalized even eigenvector is within
    `3M^(-38)` of a single cosine coordinate.

    In the centered physical basis on `[-a,a]`, this gives uniformly
    `||xi_(a,k)||_infinity<=2/sqrt(a)` and hence
    `integral_[-R,R]|xi_(a,k)|^2<=8R/a ->0` for every selection `k(a)`.
    At the same time `||xi_(a,k)||_1>=sqrt(a)`.  Therefore no rescaling of any
    simple eigenline converges even locally and globally in `L1` to a fixed
    nonzero kernel, hence none satisfies the stronger exponential-weighted
    convergence needed by item 36.  This includes the simple global ground.

    Yet if `p_(a,j)` is the projection of any fixed
    `K_j=(-1)^j K^(2j)` onto the full even Fourier band, periodic `H1`
    Parseval and Cauchy--Schwarz give, for fixed `b<1/2`,
    `integral |p_(a,j)-K_j|exp(b|x|)dx
       <= C_(j,b) a exp(ba)/(N+1)+o(1) ->0`;
    the outer tail is double-exponential.  Also
    `||Q p_(a,j)||_2<=2epsilon||p_(a,j)||_2`.  Thus the exact residual,
    Gram, and weighted-transform data can all be carried by growing linear
    combinations of individually escaping real-zero eigenlines.

    Every matrix in this control is realized by a real finite-Fourier
    distribution through the explicit finite-distribution realization
    theorem.  Its scope is nevertheless exact and limited: the distribution
    varies with `a`, the entire finite band is made small, and it is not the
    restriction of the single zeta Weil distribution.  Consequently this is
    a death certificate for any selector based only on the finite special
    identity and near-zero trial data, not for a genuinely zeta-specific
    fixed-distribution compatibility theorem.  Such a theorem must prove
    cross-scale tightness rather than assume compactness; if it yielded the
    weighted convergence of item 36, the already-proved Hurwitz chain would
    itself make it RH-strength.

40. **Pontryagin finite-index real-zero bound (proved; the proposed index
    input is itself RH-strength).**  Let a finite real symmetric parity-special matrix
    satisfy `[D,A]=|beta><eta|-|eta><beta|`, and let `xi` be a simple even
    eigenvector at `lambda`.  Put `Q=A-lambda I`,
    `kappa=n_-(Q)`, and `p=n_+(Q)`.  Then its Cauchy-numerator polynomial has,
    with algebraic multiplicity, at most `2min(kappa,p)` nonreal zeros.

    The proof is exact.  Parity gives `<beta,xi>=0`, while simplicity forces
    `<eta,xi>!=0`; normalize the latter to one.  On the quotient by `C xi`,
    the operator `D'=D-|Dxi><eta|` is self-adjoint for the nondegenerate
    `Q`-inner product of signature `(p,kappa)`.  The determinant lemma gives
    `P_xi(s)=det(D'|_(Cxi quotient)-s)`.  The direct sum of all generalized
    root spaces in the upper half-plane is totally isotropic, hence has
    dimension at most `min(p,kappa)`; conjugation doubles this count.  This
    also handles coordinate zeros and multiple roots.  The bound is sharp:
    the exact `3 by 3` special matrix in item 38 has `p=kappa=1` and numerator
    `s^2+1`.  Its sole negative vector is odd, so the *full* index, not an
    even-sector index, is essential.

    On a fixed interval the same theorem holds for a closed semibounded real
    distribution form when centered trigonometric polynomials are a form
    core, the even eigenvalue is simple and isolated, and it has finitely many
    eigenvalues below it.  Conforming Rayleigh--Ritz approximation preserves
    the exact index for large cutoffs; the finite theorem applies, and `L2`
    eigenvector convergence on the fixed interval gives compact-local
    transform convergence.  Rouche then retains the `2kappa` bound.  This
    argument avoids trying to define the infinite cyclic vector `eta`, which
    is not an `L2` vector.

    The resulting RH implication is complete under the following initially
    proposed inputs.  If on widths `a_n -> infinity` there are simple isolated even
    eigenlines `xi_n` with full lower index `kappa_n<=1`, and scalars `c_n`
    such that `c_n^(-1)xihat_n -> Xi` locally uniformly on every closed
    substrip `|Im z|<1/2`, then `Xi` has at most two nonreal zeros.  Any
    off-line zeta zero gives four distinct transformed zeros by evenness and
    conjugation (there are no real zeta zeros in `(0,1)`), a contradiction.
    Thus those inputs prove RH.

    Neither input follows from the near-zero radical.  The index must count
    all parities below the selected exact eigenvalue, and the arbitrarily
    large cluster prevents a residual from selecting an isolated eigenline.
    Moreover expanding-interval `L2` convergence is insufficient: a clean
    sufficient condition is
    `exp(ba)||xi_n-c_nK_(a_n)||_2/|c_n| ->0` for every `b<1/2` (up to the
    exact evaluation-norm factor recorded in item 36).  Item 42 below
    strengthens the audit: full-index-one at the zero threshold, or below any
    `o(1)` near-zero level, already implies RH without the transform-
    convergence input.  Inserting it as generic compactness, parity, or a
    finite-compression observation would therefore be invalid.

41. **Fixed-distribution cross-scale tightness (closed at every
    subexponential weight).**  Even using one fixed distribution at all
    widths, exact special Galerkin matrices, and a simple isolated even ground
    does not select a nonescaping eigenline.  Fix `0<b<B` and let
    `D=delta_0-sin(Bx)/(pi x)dx`, so its Fourier multiplier is
    `1-1_[-B,B]`.  Choose a nonzero real even Gevrey function `kappa`
    supported in `(-b,b)` and put `K=F^(-1)kappa`.  For any prescribed
    `alpha<1`, it can be chosen with `|K(x)|<=C exp(-c|x|^alpha)`, and `K` is
    in the full convolution radical of this same fixed `D`.

    On `[-a,a]`, the localized operator is the bounded positive contraction
    `A_a=I-T_a`, where `T_a` is the sinc-kernel prolate operator.  Its spectrum
    is `{1-lambda_n(aB)} union {1}` with
    `1>lambda_0>lambda_1>...>0`; hence its ground is simple, even, isolated,
    and its essential spectrum is the singleton `{1}`.  Trigonometric
    polynomials are an operator and form core.  The truncated radical obeys
    `||A_a P_aK||_2<=C exp(-c a^alpha)`.

    Let `E_a` project the even operator onto eigenvalues at most the square
    root of this residual.  Spectral calculus gives
    `||(I-E_a)P_aK||_2 ->0`, so the near-zero subspace recovers `K`.  Yet every
    selected normalized eigenline in that subspace escapes on compact sets.
    The trace formula gives its prolate index `n=O(a)`.  The commuting
    Sturm--Liouville eigenvalue satisfies
    `chi_n(aB)<=n(n+1)+(aB)^2`, so after physical rescaling and local
    compactness every subsequential local limit solves `-u''=q u` on `R` and
    lies in `L2(R)`; it is therefore zero.  Thus the rank of `E_a` tends to
    infinity and `K` is recovered only by an increasing number of escaping
    simple eigenlines.

    Translating to `[0,2a]`, the single restricted distribution has Fourier
    compression entries
    `q_nn=psi_a'(n)` and
    `q_mn=(psi_a(m)-psi_a(n))/(m-n)`, so every finite compression has exactly
    the rank-two/divided-difference identity.  Compactness of `T_a` gives
    norm-convergent Fourier compressions and retains any fixed simple cluster.
    No coefficient is retuned with the support width.  This therefore closes
    the varying-distribution caveat in item 39 for all universal `L2` and
    subexponential-weight arguments.

    The exact surviving distinction is exponential analyticity.  A nonzero
    bandlimited `K` cannot satisfy `exp(sigma|x|)K in L1`: its transform would
    be holomorphic in a strip and vanish on an open real interval, forcing it
    identically zero.  Thus this control cannot match the Riemann kernel's
    exponential-weight transfer on every `b<1/2`.  It also has a trivially
    positive absolutely-continuous multiplier rather than the zeta
    distribution's discrete signed zero structure.  Any remaining selector
    must use one of those two zeta-specific facts quantitatively; fixedness,
    special matrices, simplicity, and arbitrarily accurate residuals are not
    sufficient.

42. **Full localized-Weil index count (exactly RH-strength).**  One off-line
    Xi quartet forces two compactly supported negative directions, one even
    and one odd.  In centered Fourier convention the zero side is
    `Q_W(f,g)=sum_(z in Z_Xi) fhat(z) overline(ghat(conj z))`.  For an
    off-real quartet `Omega={+/-z_0,+/-conj(z_0)}` of multiplicity `m`, put
    `P_0=((z^2-z_0^2)(z^2-conj(z_0)^2))^m` and `H_0=Xi/P_0`.  This is real
    even entire, nonzero on `Omega`, and zero at every other Xi zero.

    Since `Re z_0` and `Im z_0` are nonzero, `1,z_0^2` span `C` over `R`.
    Real coefficients can therefore be chosen so that
    `F_e=(alpha+beta z^2)H_0` has `F_e(z_0)=i`, while
    `F_o=iz(alpha'+beta'z^2)H_0` has `F_o(z_0)=1`.  Quartet summation gives
    exactly `Q_W(F_e)=-4m`, `Q_W(F_o)=-4m`, and the even--odd cross term zero;
    every other zero term vanishes.

    These inverse transforms are exponentially weighted Schwartz functions.
    Even compact cutoffs converge uniformly with arbitrary horizontal
    polynomial decay on `|Im z|<=1/2`.  Since the Xi zero count is
    `O(T log T)`, their evaluation sequences converge in `ell2(Z_Xi)` and a
    sufficiently large common compact cutoff retains a negative-definite
    two-dimensional Gram matrix.  Distinct quartets give disjoint evaluation
    blocks, so `q` off-line quartets force at least `q` negative even and `q`
    negative odd directions on every sufficiently large support.

    It follows exactly that RH is equivalent to the existence of an unbounded
    width sequence with full zero-threshold index at most one: RH gives index
    zero, while false RH gives index at least two eventually.  More generally,
    the compact witnesses have a fixed negative margin `-eta`; hence for any
    localized eigenvalue `lambda_a=o(1)`, false RH gives
    `n_-(A_a-lambda_a I)>=2`.  The index-one premise considered in item 40
    thus already proves RH by itself and is not a weaker structural lemma.

    Pole-free `Z`-matrix/Perron structure does not bound the remaining
    negative levels; exact path controls have a simple positive ground and
    arbitrarily many negative eigenvalues, and the pole rank-two update can
    change the count by at most two.  Parity interlacing merely alternates
    negative levels, while zero extension propagates rather than removes
    them.  None supplies the prohibited index bound.

43. **Smallest-singular-value/squared-operator shortcut (closed).**  Passing
    from a near-zero eigenvalue to the positive operator `(A-lambda I)^2`
    does not recover a real-zero theorem.  An exact five-dimensional control
    uses nodes `(-2,-1,0,1,2)`, cyclic vector `eta=(1,1,1,1,1)`,
    `beta_j=j`, and the special matrix `Q` with every off-diagonal entry one
    and diagonal `(2/5,8/5,0,8/5,2/5)`.  Its simple even kernel is
    `xi=(5/3,-5/3,1,-5/3,5/3)`, and
    `spec(Q)={2-sqrt(209)/5,-3/5,0,3/5,2+sqrt(209)/5}`.

    The associated Cauchy numerator is exactly
    `(s^2+1)(s^2+4)`, so it has four nonreal zeros and saturates the
    Pontryagin bound with `kappa=2`.  Although `Q^2>=0` has the same simple
    kernel, its displacement commutator has rank four rather than the special
    rank two, and its extra carrier geometry retains the two negative squares
    of `Q`.  Thus minimizing `||A f||` or applying the positive ground theorem
    to a formal square cannot constrain the Xi branch; the squared localized
    operator is also no longer a difference-kernel distribution form.

44. **Actual-zeta growing translate cluster and diagonal exactization
    (proved/closed as selectors).**  Translation invariance makes every
    `K_t(x)=K(x-t)` a full Weil-radical vector.  Uniformly for `|t|<=a/2`,
    the shifted version of the item-36 tail proof gives
    `||A_a P_aK_t||_2<=C exp(Ca-c exp(2(a-|t|)))
       <=C exp(Ca-c exp(a))`.

    Choose a lattice spacing `d` so large that the sampled correlation of K
    has off-origin `ell1` norm below half its value at zero.  The bilateral
    translates `K(x-jd)` then have uniform Riesz bounds.  The even pairs
    `e_j=2^(-1/2)(K(x-jd)+K(x+jd))`,
    `1<=j<=floor(a/(2d))`, retain a uniform lower Gram bound after truncation.
    On their span, Cauchy--Schwarz and the residual bound give
    `||A_av||<=C sqrt(a)exp(Ca-c exp(a))||v||`.  Min--max for the form of
    `A_(a,+)^2` therefore proves
    `#{lambda in spec(A_(a,+)):
       |lambda|<=C sqrt(a)exp(Ca-c exp(a))}>=c_0a-O(1)`.
    Thus the actual fixed zeta distribution has a linearly growing even
    super-small cluster; fixedness and the stronger Riemann tail do not by
    themselves isolate an eigenline.

    The proposed finite diagonal exactization is algebraically valid but
    supplies no sign.  For a special compression `Q` and a coefficient vector
    `k` with no zero coordinate, put
    `Delta_i=-(Qk)_i/k_i` and `H=Q+diag(Delta)`.  Then `Hk=0` and the special
    commutator is unchanged.  Congruence by `S=diag(k_i)` turns it into the
    signed graph Laplacian
    `y^*SHSy=-sum_(i<j)q_ij k_i k_j|y_i-y_j|^2`.
    Its lower index is exactly the missing global sign theorem, not a diagonal
    balance consequence.

    Cartan avoidance can choose finite width subsequences with nonzero Xi
    samples, but it controls only denominators.  The omitted Fourier edge
    coefficients decay at the gamma-factor scale and are comparable to the
    retained edge coefficients; near small Xi samples the diagonal correction
    can be large.  Even a separately proved `||Delta||->0` would not preserve
    inertia inside the linearly growing collapsing cluster.  The exactization
    therefore cannot produce positivity or index one without assuming the
    unresolved RH-strength sign statement.

45. **Xi-radical quotient/tail rigidity (conditional lemma proved; selector
    falsified).**  Divisibility must include multiplicity: pointwise vanishing
    on the distinct Xi zeros does not prove `F/Xi` entire unless each zero
    multiplicity is matched.  Under that hypothesis an even real radical is
    `F=Xi h` with even real-entire `h`.  Double-exponential localization makes
    `F` order at most one, but Hadamard factorization still permits an infinite
    even product for `h`; it does not force a polynomial.  Only equality of
    the complete zero divisors makes `h` zero-free, hence constant by evenness
    and order one.

    A sharp conditional statement does hold.  If `h` is of exponential type
    and polynomially bounded on `R`, Paley--Wiener--Schwartz writes
    `h=muhat` for a compactly supported distribution and `f=K*mu`.  The exact
    tail
    `K(x)=2pi^2 exp(9x/2-pi exp(2x))(1+o(1))`
    and the Laplace support theorem show that a two-sided bound
    `|f(x)|<=C exp(M|x|)K(x)` forces `supp(mu)={0}`, so `h` is a polynomial;
    the bound `C K(x)` forces it constant.  The required multiplier hypotheses
    do not follow from radicality or raw order-one growth.

    More importantly, exterior leakage has no unique radical minimizer.
    With `c_a=K''(a)/K(a)=4(pi exp(2a))^2-22pi exp(2a)+O(1)`, the even radical
    `g_a=K-c_a^(-1)K''` vanishes at both endpoints, converges to K in `L2`,
    and has exterior `L2` leakage smaller than K's by
    `2(pi exp(2a))^(-2)(1+o(1))`.  Higher polynomials cancel more boundary
    jets.

    In fact polynomial radicals are dense in even `L2(R)`.  Stirling gives an
    exponential moment for `|Xi(t)|^2dt`; an analytic Fourier--Laplace
    uniqueness argument makes even polynomials dense in its even `L2` space.
    Plancherel then makes `{p(-D^2)K}` dense in even physical `L2`.  Hence for
    every fixed `a`, normalized polynomial radicals can have exterior mass
    arbitrarily close to zero, although analyticity prevents attainment by a
    nonzero compactly supported member.  Tail minimization cannot distinguish
    K.

    Finally, a zero-extended localized eigenfunction is not a global radical,
    so quotient rigidity does not apply to it.  Turning the trial residual
    into an eigenline still divides by an unproved collapsing gap, and proving
    that line to be the global ground is independently RH-strength by item
    42.  Thus the quotient mechanism yields useful structure but no valid
    finite-to-infinite or ordering bridge.

46. **Fixed discrete-entire Gaussian control (proved; order-two rather than
    Xi order-one).**  Put
    `F(z)=(1+z^4/4)exp(-z^2)sin(z^2)/z^2`.  Its real zeros include
    `+/-sqrt(pi n)`, with quadratic counting density, and it has the explicit
    off-real quartet `+/-1+/-i`.  Its inverse Fourier transform K is real,
    even, nonzero, and Gaussian-decaying: writing
    `sin(t^2)/t^2=integral_0^1 cos(ut^2)du` expresses it and all derivatives
    as mixtures of complex Gaussians bounded by a polynomial times
    `exp(-x^2/8)`.

    For `gamma_n=sqrt(pi n)`, `1/2<p<1`, and fixed `1<=c_n<=2`, the positive
    discrete form with weights `c_n gamma_n^(-p)` at `+/-gamma_n` has domain
    exactly `H^s(-a,a)`, `s=(1-p)/2 in (0,1/4)`, with equivalent form norm.
    Cell lengths are comparable to `gamma_n^(-1)`, converting the samples to
    the `H^s` Fourier weight; the low-frequency remainder is compact, and the
    superlinear zero set is a uniqueness set for every finite-exponential-
    type Paley--Wiener transform.  The associated operator is therefore
    positive, injective, and compact-resolvent on every finite interval.

    Adding one fixed negative paired mass at the first real zero preserves the
    domain and compact resolvent.  At an explicit width, choosing its size
    above the stated cosine/sine Rayleigh threshold gives a simple isolated
    even global ground; each parity block is a positive operator minus one
    rank-one form and has at most one negative level.  A single Baire-generic
    choice of the remaining comparable positive weights makes all spectra
    simple along any prescribed countable expanding width sequence.

    Every `K_j=(-1)^jK^(2j)` is in the radical of this same fixed signed
    distribution.  Tail integration by parts and
    `sum gamma_n^(-p-2)<infinity` give, on each fixed derivative span, a form
    bound `C_m(1+a)^(N_m)exp(-a^2/4)`.  Since the signed form has negative
    index at most two, min--max produces arbitrarily large Gaussian-small
    nonnegative clusters.  Finite Fourier compressions have exactly the
    special rank-two commutator: their entries are sampled Cauchy products and
    `(kappa_m-kappa_n)q_mn=b_m-b_n`.

    Thus discrete real-zero support, Gaussian/exponential decay, compact
    resolvent, simple isolated levels, fixed cross-width coefficients, and
    exact special compressions still do not identify a target radical line;
    the derivative and translate families persist.  Some simple lines in the
    growing cluster can be chosen to escape weakly, while other hypotheses
    permit nonescaping radical directions.  This model deliberately has
    order two and quadratic zero density, so it does not yet close the sharper
    Xi combination of order one, `T log T` zeros, positivity of K, and
    `exp(-c exp(2|x|))` tails.  Item 47 tests that remaining distinction.

47. **Fixed order-one, `T log T`, positive double-exponential control
    (proved; analytic selector closed).**  Put
    `a_n=(2n+sqrt(2))^(-1)`, `beta=sqrt(2)/2`, and
    `F_0(z)=product_(n>=1) cos(a_n z)`.  Local convergence follows from
    `sum a_n^2<infinity`.  Its zeros are the distinct simple real points
    `Gamma={+/-pi q(n+beta): n>=1, q>=1 odd}`: irrationality of `beta`
    excludes collisions, and the nonvanishing remainder product is locally
    convergent.  Direct harmonic summation gives
    `N_Gamma(T)=(T/pi)log T+O(T)`, while
    `log M_(F_0)(R)=sum_n log cosh(Ra_n)=(R/2)log R+O(R)`.  Thus `F_0` has
    order one and infinite type at the Xi scale.

    If `X=sum_n epsilon_n a_n` for independent Rademacher signs, `F_0` is
    its characteristic function.  The tail factors give
    `|F_0(u)|<=exp(-c|u|)`, so its law has an even nonnegative smooth density
    `K_0`.  More sharply,
    `|F_0(u-i theta)|<=F_0(-i theta)
       exp(-c u^2/(|u|+theta))` and
    `log F_0(-i theta)<=(theta/2)log theta+C theta`.
    Contour shifting with `theta` proportional to `exp(2|x|)` proves, for
    every fixed `j`,
    `|K_0^(j)(x)|<=C_j exp(-c_j exp(2|x|))`.
    Chernoff and a matching first-`N`-sign event show that the distributional
    tail has the same double-exponential scale.

    For `0<r<1/2` and `t>0`, set
    `F=F_0(1+2r cos(tz))` and
    `K=K_0+rK_0(.-t)+rK_0(.+t)`.  Then `K>=0` is even and has the preceding
    derivative tails; `F` retains exactly the real divisor `Gamma` and has
    the explicit off-real zeros
    `((2k+1)pi +/- i arcosh(1/(2r)))/t`.

    This same fixed control has compact-resolvent local forms and a
    double-exponentially accurate operator radical.  Choose `0<p<1/2`,
    `M>p`, weights
    `w_(q,n)=q^(-M)(1+pi q(n+beta))^p`, and the positive tempered measure
    `mu=sum_(gamma in Gamma)w_gamma delta_gamma`.  On `L2(-a,a)` let
    `q_a(f)=sum_Gamma w_gamma|fhat(gamma)|^2`.  Its analysis map into `ell2`
    is closed and has the compactly supported smooth functions in its domain,
    so the form is densely defined and closed.  For a finite odd `Q`, the
    union of the `q<=Q` lattices is separated: cross-lattice differences are
    an integer plus `(q-q')beta`, bounded away from zero for each of the
    finitely many pairs.  Its lower Beurling density is
    `sum_(q<=Q,q odd)1/(pi q)`.  Taking this above `a/pi`, Beurling sampling
    gives a lower frame bound for `PW_a`.  The factor `(1+|gamma|)^p` makes
    the frame tails uniformly small on a form-norm ball; finite sample heads
    are finite-dimensional.  Hence the form-domain embedding in `L2` is
    compact, and the represented positive operator `A_a` is injective with
    compact resolvent.

    Put `k_a=1_(-a,a)K`.  Since `F(gamma)=0`, one integration by parts on the
    two exterior rays gives
    `|khat_a(gamma)|<=D_a/|gamma|`, where
    `D_a<=C exp(-c exp(2a))`.  Therefore `k_a` is in the form domain.  For a
    fixed `q`-lattice the synthesis coefficients satisfy
    `(sum_n|w_(q,n)khat_a(gamma_(q,n))|^2)^(1/2)
       <=C D_a q^(-M+p-1)`.
    The separated-exponential Bessel bound on `(-a,a)` and `M>p` permit
    summation over `q`, prove `k_a in Dom(A_a)`, and yield
    `||A_a k_a||_2<=C sqrt(1+a)exp(-c exp(2a))`.
    The same proof applies to every fixed
    `(-1)^jK^(2j)`, producing arbitrarily large near-zero derivative
    clusters.
