    Thus order one, Xi-scale maximum growth and real-zero density, a
    nonnegative even inverse transform, double-exponential localization,
    one fixed positive tempered real-zero measure, all-width injective
    compact-resolvent forms, and operator-level radical residuals do not
    force all zeros real or select one radical eigenline.  The control omits
    exactly the zeta-specific signed prime-power, pole, and archimedean
    explicit-formula coupling (and deliberately does not encode its off-real
    zeros in `mu`).  Any surviving route must use that coupling
    quantitatively; the analytic package alone is now closed.

48. **Odd-half-line Riemann-radical Picone transform (proved; reflected
    prime edges obstruct positivity).**  Under the unitary odd identification
    on `x>0`, `A_o(x,y)=D(x-y)-D(x+y)`.  With
    `J(t)=exp(-t/2)/(1-exp(-2t))`, `S(x)=sinh(x/2)`,
    `c_n=Lambda(n)/sqrt(n)`, and `ell_n=log n`, its off-diagonal distribution
    is
    `-[J(|x-y|)-J(x+y)]-4S(x)S(y)
      -sum_n c_n[delta(x-y-ell_n)+delta(x-y+ell_n)]
      +sum_n c_n delta(x+y-ell_n)`.
    The omitted diagonal is the canonical archimedean finite part; no value
    is assigned to `J(0)`.  Translation prime edges therefore have the
    cooperative sign, while reflected edges `x+y=ell_n` have the opposite
    sign.

    The actual Riemann kernel `K` is strictly decreasing on the positive
    half-line.  Termwise differentiation, with
    `y=pi m^2 exp(2x)`, gives
    `K_m'=pi m^2 exp(5x/2-y)(-4y^2+15y-15/2)`.
    Above the larger quadratic root every summand is nonpositive.  Below it,
    the exact cubic formula for `K_m''` makes the `m=1` term `<-7.9`, while
    the absolute sum over `m>=2` is `<0.82`; hence `K''<0`, and `K'(0)=0`
    proves `w=-K'>0` for all `x>0`.  Since `Khat` is a nonzero multiple of
    `Xi`, differentiation of the unconditional full Weil-radical identity
    gives `A_o w=0` weakly.

    For `h=wu`, regularize the diagonal `J` singularity and then pass to the
    closed form.  The exact Picone identity is
    `Q_o(wu)=
       (1/2)iint[J(|x-y|)-J(x+y)]w(x)w(y)|u(x)-u(y)|^2
       +2iint S(x)S(y)w(x)w(y)|u(x)-u(y)|^2
       +sum_n c_n int_0^infinity w(x)w(x+ell_n)
                    |u(x+ell_n)-u(x)|^2 dx
       -(1/2)sum_n c_n int_0^ell_n w(x)w(ell_n-x)
                    |u(x)-u(ell_n-x)|^2 dx`.
    All passages converge by the double-exponential tails.  The first three
    lines are nonnegative (`J` is strictly decreasing); the reflected
    prime-power squares in the last line are genuinely negative.  Thus the
    positive exact null solution does not prove odd positivity.

    This sign obstruction is visible in a legitimate smooth compression.
    Put `A=log 2`, `B=log 3` and
    `x_1=(2A-B)/2`, `x_2=(4A-B)/2`, `x_3=(4A+B)/2`.  Then
    `x_2-x_1=A`, `x_3-x_2=B`, `x_1+x_3=log 8`, and
    `x_2+x_3=log 16`.  Narrow, disjoint, normalized smooth cells around
    these points have off-diagonal entries
    `-log(2)/sqrt(2)+O(epsilon)`,
    `-log(3)/sqrt(3)+log(2)/4+O(epsilon)`, and
    `+log(2)/sqrt(8)+O(epsilon)`.
    Other prime lines miss the cells, while the locally bounded archimedean
    and pole kernels contribute only `O(epsilon)`.  The sign triangle
    `(-,-,+)` forbids every diagonal scalar gauge.

    Globally such a gauge would have periods `log 2` and `log 3` from the
    translation edges but would have to reverse sign under
    `x -> log(n)-x`.  Irrationality of `log(2)/log(3)` makes a measurable
    function with both periods constant almost everywhere, contradicting the
    reflection rule.  A two-sheet lift only moves the target null vector to
    the anti-invariant channel `(w,-w)`; proving that channel nonnegative is
    exactly the original odd inequality.  Finally, item 42 shows directly
    that all-width odd Weil positivity is RH-equivalent: an off-line quartet
    supplies a compact negative odd witness.  The Picone formula therefore
    exposes, but does not remove, the RH-strength reflected-edge term.

49. **Independent prime-tower transfer factorization (proved; global degree
    mismatch is square-root-PNT strength).**  For one prime put
    `L=log p`, `q=p^(-1/2)`, and `T_Lh(x)=h(x+L)`.  Its complete tower is
    `Q_p(h)=-2L sum_(k>=1)q^k Re<h,T_L^k h>`, with multiplier
    `-2L(q cos(theta)-q^2)/(1-2q cos(theta)+q^2)`.  The sharp scalar
    completion is `delta_p=2Lq/(1-q)`, and the geometric series gives the
    exact square
    `Q_p+delta_p||h||^2=
       [Lq(1+q)/(1-q)]||(I-T_L)(I-qT_L)^(-1)h||^2`.
    Sharpness follows from packets concentrating at `theta=0`.  A separate
    gauge exists on each `p`-orbit, but congruence leaves inertia unchanged
    and cannot supply this diagonal price.

    For support `[-a,a]`, edgewise completion gives
    `d_(p,a)(x)=L sum_(kL<=2a)q^k
       [1_(x<=a-kL)+1_(x>=-a+kL)]`.
    Hence the total discrete degree is
    `D_a(x)=S(exp(a-x))+S(exp(a+x))`,
    `S(X)=sum_(n<=X)Lambda(n)/sqrt(n)`.  The pole autocorrelation has
    continuous degree
    `M_a(x)=4sinh((a-x)/2)+4sinh((a+x)/2)`.  Their exact mismatch is
    `V(a-x)+V(a+x)`, where
    `V(log X)=S(X)-4sinh(log(X)/2)` and Stieltjes integration yields
    `V(log X)=-1+2/sqrt(X)+E(X)/sqrt(X)
       +(1/2)int_1^X E(u)u^(-3/2)du`, `E=psi-X`.
    Conversely,
    `psi(X)=X-1+log X+sqrt(X)V(log X)
       -(1/2)int_1^X V(log u)u^(-1/2)du`.
    Therefore any polylogarithmic all-scale control of the mismatch strong
    enough to assemble the tower squares gives
    `psi(X)-X=O(sqrt(X)(log X)^B)`, excludes all zeros in `Re(s)>1/2` by the
    Mellin logarithmic derivative, and gives RH by symmetry.  The allocation
    is not a weaker diagonal lemma.

    There is a genuine but one-sided theta/divisor collapse.  Set
    `phi(y)=pi exp(5y/2)(2pi exp(2y)-3)exp(-pi exp(2y))` and
    `g_m(x)=m^(-1/2)phi(x+log m)`, so `K=sum_m g_m` and `g_m(x)>0` for
    `x>=0`.  Since `g_n(x+log Q)=sqrt(Q)g_(nQ)(x)`, absolute reindexing and
    `sum_(Q|m)Lambda(Q)=log m` prove
    `sum_(Q=p^k)Lambda(Q)Q^(-1/2)K(x+log Q)
       =sum_m(log m)g_m(x)`.
    For a fixed `p` this is a positive conditional expectation under
    `pi_x(m)=g_m(x)/K(x)`.  It does not extend to backward/reflected edges:
    the same reindexing uses `g_m(-x)`, whose summands have both signs; for
    example
    `g_2(-log 4)=(pi/8)(pi/2-3)exp(-pi/4)<0`.
    Modularity cancels the unweighted signed sum but not the valuation
    weights.  Thus independent prime sheets and the theta divisor identity
    expose the exact global obstruction without defeating it.

50. **Riemann-radical boundary capacities (exact identities; sign shortcut
    falsified).**  On `I_a=(-a,a)` put `T=P_a A_tilde P_a`,
    `k=P_aK`, `c=P_aC`, `alpha=<C,K>`, and
    `alpha_out=alpha-<c,k>`.  Since the off-support kernel of `A_tilde` is
    nonpositive,
    `r_e=-P_a A_tilde(1-P_a)K>=0` pointwise, and
    `T_e k=-2alpha c+r_e`.  If the even block is invertible and
    `m_C=<c,T_e^(-1)c>`, taking the `c` inner product after inversion gives
    exactly
    `m_C+1/2=[alpha_out+<c,T_e^(-1)r_e>]/(2alpha)`.
    Thus the required capacity `m_C<=-1/2` is precisely the nontrivial
    negative-Green estimate
    `<c,T_e^(-1)r_e><=-alpha_out`; positivity of the exterior forcing gives
    no such implication.

    With `w=-K'>0`, `s=P_aS`, `beta=<S,w>=alpha/2`,
    `beta_out=beta-<s,P_aw>`, and
    `r_o=-P_aA_tilde(1-P_a)w`, the odd equation similarly gives
    `m_S-1/2=[-beta_out-<s,T_o^(-1)r_o>]/(2beta)`.
    Here the actual zeta exterior forcing has no fixed sign.  At
    `a=1/2,x=1/10`, direct theta-tail bounds
    `K(1/2)<0.031`, `J(0.4)<1.5`,
    `w(log2-0.1)>0.13`, and `w(log2+0.1)<0.004` show that the negative
    prime-2 term is below `-0.06048`, whereas the archimedean contribution is
    below `0.0465`; hence `r_o(x)<-0.01398`.  The derivative monotonicity used
    here follows termwise because the relevant theta polynomial is positive
    for `pi exp(2t)>10`.

    A four-node exact control shows that all softer structure still cannot
    force either threshold.  At nodes `(-2,-1,1,2)` put
    `k=(B,A,A,B)`, `w=(-V,-U,U,V)` with
    `A=1`, `B=1/4`, `V=1/100`, and
    `U=(c_1A+c_2B-2s_2V)/(2s_1)>0`, where
    `c_j=cosh(j/2)`, `s_j=sinh(j/2)`.  Then
    `alpha=<C,k>=2(c_1A+c_2B)` and
    `beta=<S,w>=alpha/2`.  These jets are realized by an even positive smooth
    strictly decreasing, double-exponentially tailed `K` with `w=-K'`.
    Let `v=(A,-B,-B,A)`, `q=(-U,V,-V,U)`, `delta=1/100`,
    `Q=-delta(vv^T+qq^T)`, and
    `A_tilde=Q-2CC^T+2SS^T`.  Then `Qk=Qw=0`, all off-diagonal entries of
    `A_tilde` are negative, and the two full radical equations hold exactly.

    Compressing to the middle reflection pair gives
    `m_C=-c_1^2/(delta B^2+2c_1^2)>-1/2`, although the even exterior forcing
    is entrywise positive, while
    `m_S=s_1^2/(2s_1^2-delta V^2)>1/2` with the correct nonnegative odd
    interior block.  Hence full even/odd radicals, `w=-K'`,
    `alpha=2beta`, natural `C,S`, nonpositive off-diagonal kernel, conditional
    inertias, and the favorable even exterior sign do not imply the Krein
    bounds.  A proof must use an additional exact zeta convolution estimate;
    the boundary-capacity sign mechanism is closed.

51. **Global Riemann-kernel jump ratio (exact RH equivalence; sharp tail and
    nearest-edge flow falsifier).**  In the repository normalization put
    `C=cosh(x/2)`, `S=sinh(x/2)`, and
    `c=int C(x)K(x)dx=1/4`; evenness gives `int SK=0`.  For
    `u in C_c^infinity(R)`, define the positive jump energy
    `E_K(u)=(1/2)iint J(|x-y|)K(x)K(y)|u(x)-u(y)|^2 dxdy
       +sum_(n>=2)Lambda(n)/sqrt(n) int K(x)K(x+log n)
          |u(x+log n)-u(x)|^2 dx`.
    The diagonal singularity is integrable after the squared difference, and
    the prime series converges by the double-exponential tails.

    The unconditional radical equation `A_tilde K=-2cC` and a rigorous
    Picone cancellation on the compact smooth core give
    `Q_W(Ku)=E_K(u)-2c int CK|u|^2
       +2|int CKu|^2-2|int SKu|^2`.
    Equivalently, with `m=CK dx`, `r=S/C`,
    `D_K=int|u|^2dm-|int u dm|^2/c+|int ru dm|^2/c`, one has
    `Q_W(Ku)=E_K-2cD_K`.  Expanding
    `C(x)C(y)-S(x)S(y)=cosh((x-y)/2)` yields the sharper pairwise identity
    `2cD_K(u)=F_K(u)`, where
    `F_K=iint cosh((x-y)/2)K(x)K(y)|u(x)-u(y)|^2 dxdy`.
    Since multiplication by the strictly positive smooth K bijects the
    compact smooth core,
    `RH iff E_K(u)>=F_K(u) for every u in C_c^infinity`, or, with
    `kappa_K=inf_(F_K>0)E_K/F_K`, exactly `RH iff kappa_K>=1`.

    The threshold is unconditionally sharp.  For a fixed smooth bump `phi`,
    take `delta_R=exp(-4R)` and
    `u_R(x)=phi((x-R)/delta_R)`.  The exact K tail makes
    `D_K(u_R)=delta_R C(R)K(R)(||phi||_2^2+o(1))`.
    No prime translate overlaps the tiny cell.  The incoming prime degree is
    `P_K(R)=sum_n Lambda(n)n^(-1/2)[K(R-log n)+K(R+log n)]`.
    Ordinary PNT applied to the fixed rapidly decaying weight
    `h(y)=y^(-1/2)K(log y)` gives
    `P_K(R)/C(R)->2c=1/2`, uniformly on the cell.  Splitting the
    archimedean energy at distances `2delta_R` and `1` shows it is `o(D_K)`.
    Hence `E_K/D_K->1/2` and `E_K/F_K->1`; thus `kappa_K<=1` without RH and
    the equivalence is the sharp equality `RH iff kappa_K=1`.  No strict
    coercive reserve is available.

    The smoothed prime asymptotic does not yield square-root PNT.  The exact
    convolution equation expresses `P_K` through `C`, K, and the
    archimedean finite part; deconvolution divides by `Khat`, a nonzero
    multiple of Xi, and is singular at every zeta zero.

    A natural sharp-flow attempt is also exactly falsified.  The consecutive
    von-Mangoldt support points `1331=11^3` and `1361` have logarithmic gap
    `delta=log(1361/1331)`.  Routing every target pair through its nearest
    prime-power edge and then a short archimedean edge loads the `1361` edge,
    for an open set of endpoints, by at least
    `delta sqrt(1331*1361)/(2log1361)>1.8` times its capacity.  It fails before
    the second edge or Cauchy loss is counted.  Fractional multihop flow is
    not ruled out, but congestion at most one is precisely the displayed
    RH-equivalent domination and tail saturation permits no asymptotic loss.
    Scaling the conductances supplies generic cooperative controls with the
    same positive K null equation but arbitrarily small ratio, so only the
    exact global zeta conductance magnitudes can prove this lemma.

52. **Dense equality family forbids interval-local closable SOS proofs.**
    For every real polynomial `p`, put
    `f_p=p(-D^2)K` and `u_p=f_p/K`.  Derivative-tail estimates give
    `|u_p^(j)(x)|<=C exp(M|x|)`, so both `E_K(u_p)` and `F_K(u_p)` are finite.
    Smooth cutoffs converge in `E_K+F_K`: dominated convergence handles the
    cosh and prime tails, while a uniform local Lipschitz bound cancels the
    `J(t)~1/(2t)` singularity.  Since
    `fhat_p(t)=p(t^2)Xi(t)`, the unconditional zero-side radical identity and
    cutoff passage prove exactly `E_K(u_p)=F_K(u_p)`.

    These ratios are not dense in the global Hilbert completion for the norm
    `E_K+F_K`: the difference `E_K-F_K` is continuous in that norm, vanishes
    bilinearly on their span, and is strictly positive on an unconditional
    sufficiently-small-support even test.  They are, however, dense in even
    `L2` on every bounded interval.  Item 45 gives global `L2` density of
    `{p(-D^2)K}` by exponential-moment analytic uniqueness for
    `|Xi(t)|^2dt`; on a compact interval, division by the positive K is a
    bounded invertible multiplier.

    Consequently no proof can represent the nonzero residual
    `E_K-F_K` as a countable sum of squares of analysis operators which are
    local to bounded intervals, closable in the local `L2` topology, and
    compatible with the preceding cutoff limits.  Equality forces every
    square to annihilate every `u_p`; local density plus closability then
    makes each operator zero on the even compact core, contradicting strict
    small-support positivity.  This does not exclude a genuinely global,
    nonclosable inequality or operators closable only in the stronger
    `E_K+F_K` topology.  It does rule out the natural local Efron--Stein and
    residual-conductance SOS completions of item 51.

53. **Correction to item 49: all admissible prime edges have a positive
    theta-divisor lift; its sharp fiber gap nevertheless fails.**  The final
    signed-backward conclusion of item 49 is too strong.  Let `q` run over
    prime powers, `L_q=log q`, and `u_+/- (a)=u(+/-a)`.  Splitting a full-line
    edge into the two half-lines gives exactly
    `P_K=sum_q Lambda(q)/sqrt(q){
       sum_(sigma=+/-)int_0^infinity K(a)K(a+L_q)
          |u_sigma(a+L_q)-u_sigma(a)|^2da
       +int_0^L_q K(a)K(L_q-a)
          |u_+(L_q-a)-u_-(a)|^2da}`.
    The scaling identity gives
    `q^(-1/2)K(a+L_q)=sum_(q|m)g_m(a)` and
    `q^(-1/2)K(L_q-a)=sum_(q|m)g_m(-a)`.
    In the reflected integral `a<L_q` and `m>=q`, so
    `log(m)-a>0` and every included `g_m(-a)` is positive.  Negative-side
    same-sign edges reorient from the positive radius `a-L_q`.  Thus the
    example `g_2(-log4)<0` is algebraically true but geometrically
    inadmissible as a reflected-edge obstruction.

    With the modular coordinate `y`, the complete positive lift is
    `P_same=int_0^infinity phi(y) sum_(m<=exp y)K(y-logm)/sqrt(m)
       sum_(q|m)Lambda(q)sum_sigma
       |u_sigma(y-log(m/q))-u_sigma(y-logm)|^2dy`,
    `P_cross=int_0^infinity phi(y) sum_(m>=exp y)K(logm-y)/sqrt(m)
       sum_(q|m,m/q<=exp y)Lambda(q)
       |u_+(y-log(m/q))-u_-(logm-y)|^2dy`.
    All coefficients are positive.  The target measure also disintegrates as
    `2C(a)K(a)da=2phi(y)sum_(m<=exp y)w_y(m)dy`, with
    `w_y(m)=C(y-logm)/sqrt(m)`.

    The natural sharp fiber Poincare inequality fails inside this fully
    admissible graph.  At `y_0=log(8/3)`, the two target vertices are
    `m=1,2`; their required harmonic coefficient is
    `V=w_1w_2/(w_1+w_2)>2/5`.  The two same-side copies supply
    `2d`, `d=(log2)/sqrt2 K(log(4/3))`.  A cross auxiliary connects both
    vertices only when both `m` and `m/2` are prime powers, hence only for
    `m=2^k`, `k>=2`; exact Schur elimination contributes
    `(1/2)sum_(k>=2)c_k`,
    `c_k=(log2)2^(-k/2)K(klog2-y_0)`.  Elementary theta-tail bounds
    `K(log(4/3))<1/4`, `K(log(3/2))<1/10`, and `K(log3)<10^(-6)` give
    `G=2d+(1/2)sum c_k<0.269<2/5<V`.

    Therefore the corrected divisor lift is a real new positive structure,
    but it cannot prove the required constant fiber by fiber, even after all
    geometrically admissible reflected auxiliaries are included.  Any
    archimedean repair must couple different modular fibers globally.  An
    interval-local closable repair is impossible by item 52; a genuinely
    global nonclosable repair remains exactly the live inequality in item 51.

54. **Exact support-width derivative and strengthened divisor-fiber gap
    (both natural continuations closed).**  At the fiber
    `y_0=log(8/3)` the two lower weights simplify to
    `w_1=11sqrt(6)/24` and `w_2=7sqrt(6)/24`, so the target Schur
    conductance is exactly `V=77sqrt(6)/432=0.436598...`.  The available
    prime conductance after eliminating every admissible upper auxiliary is
    exactly
    `G=2(log 2)/sqrt(2) K(log(4/3))
       +(1/2)sum_(k>=2)(log 2)2^(-k/2)K(klog 2-y_0)`.
    Indeed an upper vertex connects both lower vertices iff it and half of it
    are prime powers, which forces `m=2^k`; all other upper vertices have
    degree one and disappear under Schur minimization.  Direct theta bounds
    sharpen item 53 to `G<0.209` (numerically `0.20809421009248...`), far
    below V.  Thus adding all fixed-fiber auxiliaries cannot repair the gap.

    The support-width induction also fails at the exact entry law.  For a
    fixed smooth physical test `h` and `q=p^k`, put
    `a_0=(log q)/2` and `c_q=Lambda(q)/sqrt(q)`.  The entering raw prime
    correlation is
    `R_q(a)=-2c_q Re int_(-a)^(a-log q)
       conjugate(h(x))h(x+log q)dx`.  It is continuous at `a_0`, but
    `R_q'(a_0+)-R_q'(a_0-)
       =-4c_q Re(conjugate(h(-a_0))h(a_0))`.
    Hence its activation is negative on a nonzero even endpoint pair and
    positive on an odd one.  In the positive ground-state transform
    `h=Ku`, the divisor-reindexed diagonal of the positive q-edge is already
    present below the threshold and is smooth through it; only this negative
    correlation activates.  The lift therefore creates no monotone positive
    boundary update.

    On Suzuki's fixed interval the full between-threshold derivative contains
    `-1/a`, endpoint correlations, translated derivative correlations, and
    the derivative of the archimedean kernel; all terms beyond the first are
    sign-indefinite.  More fundamentally, the closed localized form domain is
    only `H_log`, so endpoint values and translation derivatives need not
    exist.  The formula is rigorous on a smooth/H1 core but no
    Feynman--Hellmann endpoint formula is justified for an actual ground
    eigenfunction without a new regularity theorem.  Zero extension already
    gives the opposite global monotonicity `lambda(b)<=lambda(a)` for `b>a`,
    and a first crossing can occur between thresholds.  Thus neither prime-
    threshold telescoping nor support-width induction follows from the
    corrected positive lift; the surviving possibility must couple modular
    fibers globally and include the archimedean energy.

55. **Global separation telescope (exact) and its absolute-control
    obstruction.**  For `u in C_c^infinity(R)` define
    `A_u(l)=int K(x)K(x+l)|u(x+l)-u(x)|^2dx`, `l>=0`, and the signed
    separation measure
    `dnu(l)=sum_(n>=2)Lambda(n)n^(-1/2)delta_(log n)(dl)
       -2cosh(l/2)dl`.
    Directly grouping the two orientations in item 51 gives
    `E_K(u)-F_K(u)=int_0^infinity J(l)A_u(l)dl+int A_u dnu`.
    Its cumulative function is exactly
    `V(L)=nu([0,L])=sum_(n<=exp L)Lambda(n)/sqrt(n)-4sinh(L/2)`.
    Since `A_u(0)=0` and its K-weighted tail kills the unconditional growth
    of V, Stieltjes integration by parts is justified and yields the exact
    global telescope
    `E_K-F_K=int_0^infinity[J(l)A_u(l)-V(l)A_u'(l)]dl`.
    Thus a modular-global or divisor-global argument has not removed the
    mismatch: it must prove `int V A_u'<=int J A_u` on this constrained class
    of separation profiles.

    Polynomial all-scale control of V cannot be the proof.  RH and its
    standard square-root PNT error imply `V(L)=O((1+L)^3)`.  Conversely item
    49's exact Stieltjes inversion shows that any bound
    `|V(L)|=O((1+L)^B)` implies
    `psi(X)-X=O(sqrt(X)(log X)^B)` and therefore RH.  Such a bound is already
    equivalent in strength to the desired conclusion.

    Nor can one dominate the telescope absolutely or pointwise.  For a real
    compact smooth cutoff chi with overlap, let
    `u_N(x)=chi(x)exp(iNx)`.  Then
    `A_N(l)=P(l)-2B(l)cos(Nl)`, where P and B are smooth, independent of N,
    and `B>b>0` on some interval `I subset (0,log2)`.  On I one has exactly
    `V(l)=-4sinh(l/2)<0`; at points with `sin(Nl)=-1`, the integrand
    `J A_N-V A_N'=-cN+O(1)` is negative.  In fact
    `int_I|V A_N'|>=cN`, whereas
    `int_0^infinity J A_N=O(log N)` (use `J(l)~1/(2l)` and
    `A_N(l)=O(l^2)+O(1-cos(Nl))` near zero).  Consequently every BV,
    pointwise, or absolute-value telescope loses by a linear-versus-logarithm
    factor.  A surviving global proof would need sign-sensitive cancellation
    of V against the restricted autocorrelation profiles, not a magnitude
    estimate or local square decomposition.

56. **Electrical dual and boundary-at-infinity audit.**  Orient the positive
    archimedean and prime conductances of `E_K`, let `B_E` be their gradient,
    and let `B_F` be the gradient on the complete demand graph with measure
    `2cosh((y-x)/2)K(x)K(y)dxdy`, `x<y`.  Douglas factorization on the form
    completion gives the exact equivalence
    `E_K>=F_K iff B_F=T B_E` for a contraction T.  Equivalently every demand
    current h has a linearly selected energy flow j with the same divergence
    and `||j||<=||h||`.  Positive commodity-by-commodity path routing is only
    a stronger sufficient realization.  Half-line and all set-cut capacity
    inequalities are necessary but not sufficient: on three vertices the
    residual conductances `w_12=w_23=1,w_13=-3/4` make every cut nonnegative
    while their quadratic value at `(1,0,-1)` is `-1`.  Thus a Hall/cut proof
    needs genuinely Hilbertian, not merely mass-transport, control.

    The natural global Green boundary is exact but cannot supply the sign.
    For `f=Ku`, `G_0(x)=exp(-|x|/2)`, and `H=G_0*f`, put
    `A_+(f)=int exp(x/2)f(x)dx` and
    `A_-(f)=int exp(-x/2)f(x)dx`; these are the two asymptotic traces of H.
    Direct expansion gives
    `F_K=2c int CK|u|^2-2Re(A_+ conjugate(A_-))`.
    The product is positive on real even f and negative on real odd f, and it
    is incomplete: if `f=(-D^2+1/4)g`, `g in C_c^infinity`, then both traces
    vanish although a sufficiently small-support choice has `Q_W(f)>0`.
    Every finite theta endpoint Wronskian also vanishes because phi has a
    double-exponential tail whereas the radical ratios grow only
    exponentially.  The remaining escaping-tail functional has exact value
    `-Q_W(chi_R f_p)` on a global radical `f_p`; assigning it the desired sign
    is precisely all-width localized Weil positivity.  Contour boundary data
    instead divides by Xi and reinserts its zero divisor.  Hence finite Green
    or theta boundaries are sign-indefinite/incomplete, while the nonlocal
    boundaries are the original RH equivalent.

57. **Sharp reversible Markov-gap reformulation (live mechanism; standard
    curvature routes falsified).**  Let `mu(dx)=C(x)K(x)dx`, `mu(R)=c=1/4`.
    Define
    `P_K(u)=E_K(u)-2c[int|u|^2dmu-|int u dmu|^2/c]`.
    The identity in item 51 becomes
    `Q_W(Ku)=P_K(u)-2|int S(x)K(x)u(x)dx|^2`.
    Therefore the concrete central lemma
    `E_K(u)>=2c Var_mu(u)` would give negative index at most one for Q_W;
    item 42's two negative directions from any off-line quartet would then
    prove RH.  Conversely RH gives the lemma, so it is an exact RH-equivalent
    Markov spectral-gap statement, but it has the additional exploitable
    structure of a positive reversible jump form.

    On the smooth core its nonpositive generator is
    `Lu(x)=C(x)^(-1){int J(|x-y|)K(y)[u(y)-u(x)]dy
       +sum_q c_q[K(x+logq)(u(x+logq)-u(x))
                    +K(x-logq)(u(x-logq)-u(x))]}`,
    `c_q=Lambda(q)/sqrt(q)`.  For every polynomial p, the centered
    `u_p=p(-D^2)K/K` is a genuine weak/operator eigenfunction at the sharp
    threshold `-L=2c=1/2`; choosing `p(-1/4)=0` makes its mu-mean zero.
    These give infinite multiplicity at `1/2`, while tail packets also
    saturate it, so there can be no strict reserve or compact-resolvent
    argument.  The task is exactly to exclude spectrum in `(0,1/2)`.

    A sharp Bakry--Emery proof is impossible.  If `CD(1/2,infinity)` held,
    equality for a threshold eigenfunction would force its carré du champ to
    be harmonic and hence constant by irreducibility.  For
    `u=(-K''+K/4)/K`, the exact K tail instead gives
    `Gamma(u)(x)>=c exp(7x)` along the positive tail (while its mu integral is
    finite), so it is nonconstant.  Stronger curvature bounds also fail.
    Bounded-rate Cheeger estimates collapse when `J(t)~1/(2|t|)` is
    truncated, the fixed theta fibers fail item 54's constant, and nearest-
    edge canonical paths overload item 51.  These are method falsifiers, not
    a counterexample to the actual Markov gap.  A surviving proof must be an
    exact global no-bound-state/factorization argument at an infinitely
    degenerate essential threshold.

58. **Theta law-of-total-variance and decomposable-current class closed.**
    The Markov measure `mu=CK dx` has an exact theta mixture.  For
    `y>=0`, `N=floor(exp y)`, both exponential tilts of K have common marginal
    `r(y)=phi(y)[exp(y/2)H_N+exp(-y/2)N]`; under mu the conditional states are
    `x=sigma(y-log m)` with weights proportional to
    `exp(y/2)/m+exp(-y/2)`, `sigma=+/-`, `m<=exp y`.  Thus the Markov variance
    splits exactly into conditional variance plus the variance of the
    conditional mean.  The prime energy has the positive divisor-fiber
    decomposition of item 53, but it cannot control the first term with the
    sharp constant.  At `y=log 3`, on either sign, the weights are
    `a_j=(3+j)/(2j sqrt(3))`, `j=1,2,3`.  For values `(0,1,0)` the variance is
    `15/(17sqrt(3))`, whereas the only prime edge contributes
    `b=(log2)/sqrt2 K(log(3/2))<1/4`; hence
    `b<(1/2)Var`.  The elementary theta bound is strict on a y-neighborhood.

    This also gives a class-level dual obstruction.  In the canonical
    conditional-expectation/Douglas factorization, residual currents are
    decomposable at fixed y and marginal currents live on the y-network.  The
    residual contraction norm is the essential supremum of the reciprocal
    fiber gaps and exceeds one by the preceding open interval.  Lifted
    archimedean conductance is absolutely continuous in `(y,z)`, so it places
    zero mass on `y=z`; concentrating it in `|y-z|<epsilon` to repair these
    physical states costs order `1/epsilon` in squared current norm.  Thus no
    decomposable conditional-variance proof survives, even with synchronous
    arch regularization.  A successful theta current must mix the conditional
    and marginal blocks nonlocally; its unconstrained optimum is the full
    Douglas factorization and hence the RH-equivalent Markov gap.

59. **Threshold Picone, nodal capacity, and commutator audits.**  Let
    `T=-L`, `H=T-1/2`, `P=-D^2+1/4`,
    `F_0=PK`, and `phi=F_0/K`.  Integration by parts gives
    `int phi dmu=0`, and radicality gives `H phi=0`.  For tests away from the
    zeros of F_0 the exact jump Picone identity is
    `<phi v,H phi v>_mu=(1/2)iint W(x-y)F_0(x)F_0(y)
       |v(x)-v(y)|^2dxdy`.
    Since F_0 is positive centrally and negative in the tails, this is a
    signed, not positive, conductance.  Applying P to the divisor lift also
    destroys its sign: the admissible `q=2,m=2,x=0` summand has parameter
    `4pi` and is strictly negative.

    With `B=C'K-CK'`, one has `B'=CF_0`, `B(0)=B(infinity)=0`; hence the
    positive and negative nodal masses of `CF_0` agree exactly.  At any nodal
    boundary r, the equation `Hphi(r)=0` likewise makes the absolute same-
    sign and opposite-sign incident capacities equal.  If the zero has odd
    order m, the two separately positive Picone pieces for a generic smooth
    u diverge with the identical leading term
    `kappa_r log(1/epsilon)` for `m=1`, or
    `kappa_r epsilon^(1-m)/(m-1)` for `m>1`; only their signed finite part is
    defined on the actual form domain.  The sharp two-domain capacity constant
    is therefore at least one with no reserve, while proving that its
    renormalized value is at most one is exactly `<u,Hu>>=0`.  A conventional
    Birman--Schwinger or Dirichlet-to-Neumann split is not closed on the
    required domain.

    The derivative cyclic structure is also insufficient.  Put
    `R u=K^(-1)(-D^2)(Ku)` and `g=R1+1/4=(-K''+K/4)/K`.  On polynomials in R,
    the exact identities are
    `m(R^j1)=c(-1/4)^j` and `[T,R]f=2g m(f)`; every centered polynomial vector
    lies at threshold `1/2`.  A reversible three-state Markov Laplacian
    `L=(1/168)[[40,-8,-32],[-8,31,-23],[-32,-23,55]]`, with mass inner
    product `(1/12)sum`, has `L1=0`, threshold vector `(2,1,-3)` at `1/2`,
    lower vector `(-4,5,-1)` at `1/4`, and an operator R satisfying the same
    moment recurrence and rank-one commutator.  It lifts to an irreducible
    reflection-symmetric six-state chain.  Thus reversible positivity,
    reflection, a one-node radial threshold vector, the infinite cyclic
    threshold pattern, and the rank-one commutator do not order the
    orthogonal sector.  Item 32 already excludes the missing generic TP2
    oscillation mechanism.  Finite-order Darboux factors, positive connected
    jump gradients, local square families, and strict threshold Mourre
    estimates are additionally excluded by the infinite-dimensional kernel.

60. **Vector-valued threshold frames do not remove the sign.**  Let
    `Phi=(phi_j)` be any real finite or square-summable frame of threshold
    eigenfunctions, `T phi_j=(1/2)phi_j`, put `r=||Phi||` and
    `n=Phi/r`.  The exact vector Picone calculation gives, for `u=rf`,
    `<u,(T-1/2)u>_mu=(1/2)iint dC r(x)r(y){
       <n(x),n(y)>(|f(x)|^2+|f(y)|^2)
       -2Re(f(x)conjugate(f(y)))}`.
    The two-by-two edge matrix has eigenvalues `g-1,g+1`,
    `g=<n(x),n(y)>`; it is positive semidefinite only when `g=1`.
    Since the archimedean conductance joins every two distinct points, this
    would force all frame directions to be globally positively collinear and
    reduce to the scalar nodal transform.  Equivalently the covariant square
    carries an unavoidable negative angular potential
    `V_Phi(x)=r(x)^(-1)int r(y)[1-<n(x),n(y)>]q(x,dy)>=0`.
    Proving that the covariant energy dominates this potential is again the
    full Markov gap, with equality and no reserve on every frame component.

    The obstruction occurs for the actual theta frame, not only abstractly.
    With `f_0=(-D^2+1/4)K`, `f_2=-f_0''`, and
    `Phi=(f_0/K,f_2/K)`, direct theta bounds give
    `f_0(0) in (8,9)`, `f_2(0) in (408,409)`,
    `f_0(0.2) in (1,2)`, and `f_2(0.2) in (-21,-20)`, hence the frame Gram
    across the genuine arch edge `(0,0.2)` is strictly negative.  More
    generally `int Phi dmu=0`; a continuous nonvanishing mean-zero frame
    cannot have nonnegative pairwise Gram everywhere because the double
    integral of that Gram is zero while it is positive near the diagonal.
    Positive changes of fiber metric leave the Cauchy--Schwarz rank-one
    obstruction unchanged, and an indefinite metric forfeits a positive
    square.  Thus finite/infinite, translated, magnetic, and covariant frame
    transforms only package the missing sign as angular curvature.

61. **Analytic-translate uniqueness (closed at item 62 except for an
    RH-strength tail gate).**  If a
    hypothetical even mean-zero eigenfunction v has eigenvalue below `1/2`,
    spectral orthogonality to the derivative threshold family implies that
    every Taylor coefficient at zero of
    `F_v(a)=int v(x)C(x)K(x-a)dx` vanishes.  Cauchy--Schwarz with
    `mu=CK dx` and the complex double-exponential K bounds makes this
    correlation holomorphic in a fixed neighborhood of zero for every
    `v in L2(mu)`, so it vanishes locally.  Local analyticity alone is not
    injective.  For any real Hardy zero gamma of Xi,
    `v_gamma(x)=cos(gamma x)/C(x)` is nonzero, even, mean zero, and in
    `L2(mu)`, while
    `F_(v_gamma)(a)=cos(gamma a)Xi(gamma)=0` for every real a.  Therefore even
    the full real translate family has a nontrivial annihilator; Fourier
    injectivity would require additional integrability excluding spectral
    distributions supported at the real zeros.

    The only surviving version is a genuine eigenfunction tail theorem strong
    enough to place `vC` in an injective function class (for example L2 or
    L1), not merely to make F analytic or vC tempered.  The prime-convolution
    tail equation has leading degree `1/2`; its continuous PNT main term
    cancels for an even mean-zero v, but unconditional PNT controls the
    remainder only subexponentially.  Whether the subthreshold equation
    bootstraps to the required stronger decay, without a square-root-PNT or
    zero-free input equivalent to RH, is the exact live analytic lemma.

62. **Subthreshold exterior decay and translate identity (proved; Fourier
    division still blocked by the zero divisor).**  Suppose an even mean-zero
    Markov eigenfunction satisfies `Tv=lambda v`, `0<lambda<1/2`, and put
    `delta=1/2-lambda`.  Ordinary PNT applied to the fixed K-window shows that
    the exterior Dirichlet compression tends to the killing threshold `1/2`.
    In the unitary `sqrt(CK)` coordinates, Chebyshev plus the exact theta tail
    bounds the core--tail prime and archimedean blocks by
    `exp[-(pi/2-o(1))exp(2R)]`.  The exterior resolvent therefore gives
    `||1_(|x|>R)v||_(L2(mu))
       <=C_delta exp[-(pi/2-o(1))exp(2R)]`.
    This sharp square-root-of-`K/C` rate uses no square-root PNT.

    It is enough to extend
    `F(a)=int v(x)C(x)K(x-a)dx` holomorphically to the full connected strip
    `|Im a|<pi/4`.  The exact translated-radical identity is
    `[T-(1/2)(I-Pi_0)](K(.-a)/K)
       =2c sinh(a/2) S/C`.
    Pairing with v proves `(lambda-1/2)F(a)
       =2c<v,S/C>_mu sinh(a/2)`; hence F vanishes identically for even v.
    This completes all domain, tail, and analytic-continuation steps of the
    proposed translate argument.

    Fourier division remains invalid.  For every real zero gamma of Xi,
    `v_gamma=cos(gamma x)/C(x)` is a nonzero even mean-zero member of
    `L2(mu)` and has `F_gamma(a)=cos(gamma a)Xi(gamma)=0` throughout the
    strip.  A sufficient closing hypothesis is `Cv in Lp(R)` for some
    `1<=p<=2`; Hausdorff--Young/Plancherel then makes its Fourier transform a
    function, so multiplication by Xi (nonzero almost everywhere) is
    injective.  The proved exterior estimate does not imply this: it is
    saturated by the `K/C` tail and permits `Cv` to be a bounded oscillatory
    distribution.  After the PNT main term cancels, strengthening the tail to
    the required class demands polynomial control of the cumulative mismatch
    V, which item 49 proves is RH-equivalent.  An exact reversible integral
    Markov control can keep every derivative mode at `1/2`, put one such
    `v_gamma` at `1/2-delta`, and retain positive rates, so reversibility plus
    the entire threshold family cannot supply the missing integrability.
    The remaining spectral-synthesis question must use an actual zeta
    identity distinguishing real-zero-supported modes; analyticity alone is
    exhausted.

63. **Exact mean-periodic/RKHS synthesis reduction (coordinates only, no
    sign).**  Multiplication `Uv=Cv` is unitary from `L2(CK dx)` to
    `L2(r dx)`, `r=K/C`.  The weighted Fourier image is an RKHS `E_r` with
    kernel `R(z-conjugate(w))`, `R=Fourier(r)`.  In its even sector the
    constant maps to Xi and the threshold family maps to
    `{p(z^2)Xi(z)}`.  Hence the exact translate-annihilator is
    `N=M_Xi^perp`, where
    `M_Xi=closure_{E_r}{p(z^2)Xi(z)}`.
    Root exponential-polynomials for every zero of Xi lie in N.  Their closed
    span equals N iff the nontrivial hereditary-completeness/division theorem
    `M_Xi={F in E_r: F and the required jets vanish at every Xi zero}` holds;
    item 45's density in `L2(|Xi|^2dt)` has a different norm and does not prove
    this synthesis.

    Even granting synthesis, the sign is unchanged.  For distinct positive
    real zeros gamma_j, the root-mode Gram is
    `G_jk=[R(gamma_j-gamma_k)+R(gamma_j+gamma_k)]/2>_matrix 0`, while the
    action matrix is exactly
    `A_jk=Q_W((K/C)cos(gamma_j x),(K/C)cos(gamma_k x))`, equivalently its
    convergent prime-plus-arch Markov formula.  On the zero side, with
    `F_j(z)=[R(z-gamma_j)+R(z+gamma_j)]/2`, every off-line quartet contributes
    `4m Re[(sum c_jF_j(z))^2]`.  A synthesized subthreshold eigenvector solves
    `Ac=(lambda-1/2)Gc`.  Thus proving `A>=0` on all finite systems and passing
    through synthesis is literally Weil positivity in a complete root-mode
    basis.  Reversibility supplies only Hermiticity: an exact positive-rate
    Markov control realizes `A=-delta gg^*/G_(gamma,gamma)` while preserving
    every derivative mode at threshold.  Spectral synthesis may describe the
    complement but does not order it independently of the RH-equivalent form.

64. **Selberg multi-hop current (exact finite overload and all-hop tail
    deficit).**  Selberg's identity
    `Lambda(n)log n+(Lambda*Lambda)(n)=(mu*log^2)(n)` gives, after critical
    weighting and partial summation, cumulative positive left side
    `4sqrt(X)+O(sqrt(X)/log X)` against target `2sqrt(X)+o(sqrt X)`, so the
    natural ordered two-prime allocation has coefficient
    `d_(q,r)=c_qc_r/[2log(qr)]`, `c_q=Lambda(q)/sqrt(q)`.  On the physical
    series path `-log(qr)->-log r->0`, edge capacities are
    `C_1=c_qK_(qr)K_r`, `C_2=c_rK_rK_0`, and the exact effective capacity is
    `(C_1^(-1)+C_2^(-1))^(-1)`.

    At `n=6`, pooling both orders `(2,3),(3,2)`, the allocated conductance is
    `D_6=c_2c_3K_6K_0/log6`, while their combined effective capacity is at
    most `K_6(c_2+c_3)K_2`.  Elementary theta bounds
    `K_0>2/5`, `K_2<1/500`, `c_2>2/5`, `c_3>3/5`,
    `c_2+c_3<6/5`, and `log6<9/5` give overload
    `D_6/(kappa_(2,3)+kappa_(3,2))>200/9`; this precedes all shared-edge
    congestion and Cauchy losses.  The signed Mobius right side cannot be a
    positive replacement.

    Iteration does not restore capacity.  Normalizing at the first edge,
    two-hop tail capacity is less than `1/160`, three-hop capacity is less
    than `10^(-12)`, and even overcounting every ordered prime-power
    factorization `a_Q(m)<=m^2` gives total all-hop remainder
    `sum_(m>=2)a_Q(m)K(logm)/(sqrt(m)K_0)<1/100`.  The corresponding backlog
    shift has norm below `1/100` on polynomially weighted sup spaces, so it is
    Neumann-invertible.  Any polynomial bound for an iteratively repaired
    backlog therefore gives the same polynomial bound for V and, by items 49
    and 55, square-root PNT/RH.  Selberg iteration is thus physically
    falsified as a contraction and algebraically returns the exact RH-strength
    cumulative mismatch.

65. **Radial order and monotone rearrangement fail for the actual q=2
    reflection.**  On even functions the half-line generator has arch kernel
    `J(|a-b|)+J(a+b)` and prime jumps `a->a+logq` and
    `a->|a-logq|`.  Put `l=log2`, `s_0=l/4`, take
    `a=s_0-epsilon<b=s_0+epsilon`, and choose a smooth increasing step whose
    transition is at `3l/4`.  It has equal value zero at a,b.  The reflected
    q=2 jump sends a above the step and b below it, so
    `L_rad f(a)-L_rad f(b)->c_2K(3l/4)/C(l/4)>0`; the outward q=2,
    all q>=3, and arch differences tend to zero by uniform convergence and
    dominated convergence.  This violates the generator contact condition
    for an order-preserving semigroup.

    The weaker Rayleigh rearrangement fails on the same four-cell geometry.
    Centers `l/4-e,l/4+e,3l/4-e,3l/4+e` have reflected q=2 rectangles of
    mass order eta on the crossed pairs, while arch and nonresonant-prime
    pair masses are order `eta^2`.  The radially ordered signs `(-,-,+,+)`
    pay both q=2 edges, whereas the equimeasurable interlaced assignment
    `(-,+,+,-)` pays neither; outside/diagonal contributions can be made
    sign-independent.  Hence increasing rearrangement can strictly increase
    the actual E_K energy.  Stochastic monotonicity and monotone-minimizer
    ordering are both unavailable; nonlocal nodal domains join all same-sign
    components and do not bound the number of ordered sign changes.

66. **Translation-copy negative-count comparison saturates rather than
    contradicts.**  For a compact pole-neutral f with `Q_W(f)=-eta`, its
    translate keeps the numerator but has generalized norm
    `N_R=int|f(x-R)|^2C(x)/K(x)dx` with
    `log N_R=(pi+o(1))exp(2R)`, so its negative Rayleigh value is only
    `-exp[-(pi+o(1))exp(2R)]`.  Individual negative translated diagonals do
    not give a growing negative index.  The exact translation-invariant
    control `Q_0(f)=-|fhat(gamma)|^2` has a rank-one negative Gram on every
    number of disjoint translates; an off-line quartet similarly has bounded
    exponential-Gram rank.  Thus separation does not make the negative
    directions independent.

    The exterior estimates are exactly saturated.  In normalized coordinates
    the rank-one control vector has square `K/C`; its tail obeys
    `int_R^infinity K/C
       =2pi exp(2R)exp[-pi exp(2R)](1+o(1))`.  Hence the core--tail singular
    value has logarithm `-(pi/2)exp(2R)+R+O(1)`, while the exterior negative
    eigenvalue has logarithm `-pi exp(2R)+2R+O(1)`, exactly its square.
    Schur-complement lower bounds and the sharp upper bound in item 62 meet at
    the same constant, so no eigenvalue-count contradiction or strict
    exterior reserve remains without a new zeta-specific independence
    theorem.

67. **The integrated spectral polynomial and its theta-Krylov/Feshbach
    iteration are exact repackagings of the Markov gap.**  On the form core,
    let `G_u=A_0u+sum_q c_qB_qu`, where `A_0` is the compensated archimedean
    difference operator and `B_q` the two `+/-logq` prime differences.  Then
    exactly
    `<u,T(T-1/2)u>_m=int(K/C)|G_u|^2-(1/2)E_K(u)`.
    Its prime--prime coefficients are `Lambda(q)Lambda(r)/sqrt(qr)`, but their
    profiles depend separately on q and r through the intermediate shifted
    K/C weights.  They therefore do not collapse to a Selberg convolution.
    The finite physical star on `0,log2,log3` has both nonzero eigenvalues
    below 1/2 (by the exact exit-rate bounds), so its spectral polynomial is
    strictly negative on its two-dimensional mean-zero space.  Archimedean
    and global-prime signed cancellation is essential.

    For the theta lift `I` and positive upstairs operator `Ttilde`, use the
    pulled-back closed form unless `Ran I` has the required operator domains.
    Closed Krylov differences give a block Jacobi matrix with `A_0=T` and
    Schur recursion
    `F_n(z)=A_n-z-B_n^*F_(n+1)(z)^(-1)B_n`.  At z=1/2,
    `A_0-1/2=F_0(1/2)+B_0^*F_1(1/2)^(-1)B_0`; the last term has no fixed sign.
    The PSD matrix `[[1/4,1/4],[1/4,1/4]]`, with spectrum `{0,1/2}`, already
    has compression `A_0=1/4<1/2` and `F_1(1/2)=-1/4`.  In the actual N=6
    diamond, both ordered-path interior denominators are strictly negative:
    `Delta_3<K_3(1/1000+1/3-1/2)<0` and
    `Delta_2<K_2(1/750+1/4-1/2)<0`.  Thus no positive Stieltjes fraction is
    available at the threshold.  The threshold is infinitely degenerate, so
    a boundary Feshbach limit would additionally require an unproved range
    condition and uniform tail resolvent bound.  The complete Krylov hierarchy
    is the spectral-measure continued fraction; forcing its boundary sign is
    precisely `T>=1/2` on mean zero.

68. **Mean-periodic residue observability has exactly the off-line Weil
    sign.**  Put `r=K/C` and
    `R(s)=int_R r(x)exp(-sx)dx`.  For a zero mode
    `w_k=exp((rho_k-1/2)x)`, the incoming prime-tail transform factors as
    `R(s)(-zeta'/zeta)(rho_k+s)`.  Its residue at another zero rho_j is
    `-m_jR(rho_j-rho_k)`, so any synthesized invariant coefficient vector
    would satisfy the exact residue system
    `(lambda-1/2)a_j=m_j sum_k R(rho_j-rho_k)a_k`, after the required contour
    remainder is controlled.  On critical-line zeros this R-matrix is the
    positive Gram of exponentials in `L2(r)`.

    For a functional-equation pair
    `rho_+=1/2+a+i gamma`, `rho_-=1/2-a+i gamma`, however, the two-by-two block
    is `[[R(0),R(2a)],[R(2a),R(0)]]`.  Since r is positive and even,
    `R(2a)=2int_0^infinity r(x)cosh(2ax)dx>R(0)` for nonzero a.  The vector
    `(1,-1)`, proportional to `sinh(ax)exp(i gamma x)`, has strictly negative
    residue form.  It belongs to `L2(r)` because r has a double-exponential
    tail.  Finite-mode invariance is not automatic: eliminating all other
    output residues is exactly the hereditary-completeness/interpolation gate
    of item 63.  Thus synthesis remains unresolved and the proposed
    observability sign is violated precisely by an off-line pair; proving it
    positive would already exclude that pair and supplies no independent
    uncertainty mechanism.

69. **A Pontryagin/sign-changing Doob transform does not bound the negative
    index.**  For a real threshold eigenfunction phi of a reversible jump
    operator, the exact identity is
    `<phi f,(T-lambda)phi f>=(1/2)sum_(x,y)c_xy phi_x phi_y
      |f_x-f_y|^2`.
    With a threshold frame Phi and a symmetric fiber metric J, normalize
    `r_x^2=Phi_x^TJPhi_x` and `g_xy=n_x^TJn_y`.  Each transformed edge has
    matrix `[[g_xy,-1],[-1,g_xy]]`, with eigenvalues `g_xy+/-1`.  A Lorentz
    metric is positive only when all normalized vectors occupy one time cone;
    mean-zero reflection frames necessarily meet opposite sheets.  A direct
    integral of pointwise index-one fibers has no global index-one bound.

    An exact positive-rate control occurs on the eight-cycle.  With uniform
    mass and
    `(Tu)_j=(3/16)(2u_j-u_(j-1)-u_(j+1))
       +(1/64)sum_(k!=j)(u_j-u_k)`,
    the Fourier eigenvalues are `0`,
    `1/2-3sqrt(2)/16` (multiplicity two), `1/2` (multiplicity two),
    `1/2+3sqrt(2)/16` (multiplicity two), and `7/8`.  Thus the mean-zero
    shifted operator has negative index two despite the complete threshold
    frame `(cos(pi j/2),sin(pi j/2))` and reflection symmetry.  Reflection-
    compatible index-one metrics make its squared norm alternate sign; even
    without that constraint, opposite points satisfy `Phi_(j+2)=-Phi_j` and
    are joined by a positive edge.  Functional-equation reflection therefore
    supplies no one-negative-square theorem.  Dominating the opposite-sheet
    terms is exactly the original shifted Markov form.

70. **Translated positive radicals cannot be made cooperative by a positive
    constraint correction.**  Let Q be the full Weil form and
    `h_a(x)=K(x-a)`.  Its zero-side transform is
    `exp(-iat)Xi(t)`, so `Qh_a=0` weakly.  Its C,S moment vector is
    `c(cosh(a/2),sinh(a/2))`.  Any PSD correction R supported on the two
    constraint functions and satisfying `Rh_a=0` must in dimension two be
    `R=tau g_a tensor g_a`, `tau>=0`, where
    `g_a(x)=-sinh(a/2)C(x)+cosh(a/2)S(x)=sinh((x-a)/2)`.
    Thus `B=Q-R` still has the strictly positive null function h_a, and
    `Q=B+R`; if B had nonpositive off-diagonal kernel, its ground-state
    transform would prove Q nonnegative.

    The sign requirement fails exactly at the zero of the only admissible
    correction.  Since `g_a(a)=0`, the correction does not change the
    off-diagonal coefficient between a and `a+log6`.  There is no prime atom
    at 6, and the continuous coefficient is translation invariant and equals
    `2cosh(log6/2)-J(log6)=209/(35sqrt6)>0`.  Hence B is not cooperative for
    any a or tau.  This defeats the new translated-null-vector feature
    directly rather than by finite diagonal sampling.  Allowing a signed
    correction loses the implication `Q=B+R>=0`; controlling its negative
    part is again the full Weil form.

71. **Prime-weight spectral flow has a moving threshold and indefinite
    crossing form.**  For `E_t=E_arch+tE_prime`, `0<=t<=1`, the common forms
    are Kato type-(B) away from t=0, and Persson's theorem plus the exact tail
    packets gives `inf spec_ess(T_t)=t/2`.  A simple isolated branch satisfies
    `lambda'(t)=E_prime(u_t)`, so relative to the moving edge its derivative
    is `E_prime(u_t)-(1/2)||u_t||^2`, with no fixed sign.  At t=1 every
    derivative radical has
    `E_t(u_p)-(t/2)||u_p||^2=(1-t)E_arch(u_p)>=0`; this endpoint equality
    family does not constrain unrelated branches below the edge.

    An exact reversible three-state control uses, on mean zero,
    `A=(1/8)P_1+(1/4)P_2` and `B=(1/4)(P_1+P_2)`, where
    `e_1=(1,-1,0)/sqrt2`, `e_2=(1,1,-2)/sqrt6`.  Both A and B are Markov
    Laplacians.  For `T_t=A+tB`, the branches are `1/8+t/4` and `1/4+t/4`:
    the first ends at `3/8<1/2`, while the second ends exactly at 1/2 with
    equal arch and prime energies.  Appending a sector `A=0,B=(1/2)I` gives
    the exact essential edge t/2.  A determinant repair would require a
    uniform boundary resolvent at an infinitely degenerate non-Fredholm
    threshold and relative trace-class control; that missing no-bound-state
    estimate is the Markov gap itself.

72. **The differential commutator is rank one only on the threshold cyclic
    family, not on a subthreshold eigenfunction.**  Let A_0 be the
    translation-invariant pole-free Weil operator, `P=-D^2`,
    `R=K^(-1)PK`, and `r=K/C`.  The exact Markov identity is
    `A_0(Ku)=C(T-1/2)u`.  Since A_0 commutes with P, direct differentiation on
    the common smooth core gives, with `F=A_0(Ku)`,
    `[T,R]u=(2r'/K)F'+(r''/K)F`.
    For `u=p(R)1`, `F=-2m(u)C`, and only then this reduces to the item-59
    formula `[T,R]u=2g m(u)`.  For a hypothetical eigenfunction
    `Tv=lambda v` it instead becomes
    `[T,R]v=(lambda-1/2){2(log r)'v'-g v}`,
    whenever the required derivatives exist.  It is generically nonzero, so
    R does not preserve the eigenspace.

    The failure is visible at principal-symbol level.  The local archimedean
    symbol is `r(x)log|xi|`; commutation with the second-order leading symbol
    of R produces `2i r'(x)xi log|xi|`, while prime shifts contribute only
    order `|xi|`.  A mean-zero localized oscillatory test therefore directly
    contradicts the alleged global rank-one identity.  Moreover `D(T)` has
    only logarithmic local regularity and does not imply `D(R)`, nor that Rv
    lies in `D(T)`.  A delta-interaction Schrödinger operator supplies an
    exact reflection-symmetric domain control: its formal Laplacian
    commutator vanishes off the interaction, but its subthreshold bound state
    has a derivative jump and is outside the Laplacian domain.

    What survives exactly is `H R=R^*H`, `H=T-1/2`, with
    `R^*=C^(-1)PC`.  The bracket on an eigenfunction is
    `(lambda-1/2)(R^*-R)v`; its diagonal virial form vanishes identically
    because `(CK(log r)')'+CKg=0`.  Thus it supplies symmetry, not a sign.
    Conditional on the false invariance, Cayley--Hamilton and the
    constant-coefficient ODE argument would indeed force `Kv=0`; the invalid
    extension is exactly the essential step, not a missing technicality.

73. **The surviving Krein intertwining has none of the hypotheses needed to
    force an invariant negative subspace.**  The metric
    `[u,v]=<Hu,v>`, `H=T-1/2`, has the infinite derivative-radical kernel and
    spectrum accumulating at zero; its quotient norm is incomplete.  Passing
    to `sign(H)` requires the unbounded factor `|H|^(-1/2)`.  The differential
    conjugate R is closed but has empty resolvent on `L2(CK)`: for compactly
    supported forcing, every Green solution of `(-D^2-z)f=h` has only
    exponential tails, while membership after division by K requires
    double-exponential decay.  Hence neither its resolvent, Cayley transform,
    nor heat transform is a bounded operator on the Markov Hilbert space.

    The actual constant mode already saturates the defect:
    `H1=-(1/2)1`, `R1=-(1/4)1+g`, and `Hg=0`.  Thus its negative line is
    invariant only modulo `ker H`; the complete polynomial cyclic space is
    R-invariant but has a rank-one negative form and an infinite radical, and
    R acts there by degree raising with no Hilbert eigenvector.  Any quotient
    Cayley--Hamilton conclusion has only `p(R)v in ker H`, not `p(R)v=0`, so
    the constant-coefficient ODE has a nonzero threshold-radical right side.
    A reversible three-state control realizes `HR=R^*H`, the constant mode,
    a threshold vector, and a second negative eigenvector; direct sums give
    arbitrary negative index and infinite threshold kernel.  Pontryagin
    theory preserves an index supplied in its hypotheses and cannot prove
    that the index is one here.

74. **The rank-one corrected differential symmetrizer still has empty
    resolvent.**  Put `P_0` for the constant projection,
    `A=T-(1/2)(I-P_0)`, `g=R1+1/4`, and `B=R-gP_0`.  On the common core the
    exact relations `P_0R=-(1/4)P_0`, `R^*P_0=-(1/4)P_0`, `Ag=0`, and
    `HR=R^*H` give `AB=B^*A`.  Also B reduces constants and mean zero:
    `B=-(1/4)P_0 direct-sum R|_(1^perp)`.  Thus its only Hilbert eigenline is
    the constants at `-1/4`: any other eigenvector would make `f=Ku` a
    nonzero full-line solution of `(-D^2-eta)f=0` in
    `L2(C/K dx)`, impossible because `C/K` grows double-exponentially.

    The correction does not repair surjectivity.  For compact mean-zero
    forcing F, solving `(R-z)u=h` requires
    `(-D^2-z)(Ku)=F` with a solution vanishing faster than every exponential
    at both ends.  Outside the support it is homogeneous, so the two exact
    compatibility conditions are
    `int exp(kappa x)F(x)dx=int exp(-kappa x)F(x)dx=0`, `kappa^2=-z`
    (zeroth and first moments at z=0).  Mean zero removes only one unrelated
    functional, and generic compact forcing violates the others.  Hence
    `B-z` is not onto for any z distinct from `-1/4`, while at `-1/4` it has
    the constant kernel.  The centered correction therefore leaves an empty
    resolvent, the infinite A-kernel, and zero accumulation.  No bounded
    Cayley transform or Pontryagin invariant-subspace theorem results; the
    fact that B has only the constant eigenline cannot itself create the
    missing invariant negative subspace.  This is not repaired by abstractly
    postulating a bounded resolvent: on `ell2(N_0) direct-sum L2(0,1)`, take
    `A=(-|e_0><e_0|) direct-sum M_x` and
    `B=S_forward direct-sum M_x`.  Then `AB=B^*A`, A has one negative
    direction, an infinite kernel and positive spectrum accumulating at zero,
    B is bounded with nonempty resolvent, yet B has no eigenvalue or finite-
    dimensional invariant subspace.  Adding a null constant line with
    eigenvalue `-1/4` reproduces the corrected point spectrum.  The actual
    centered radical has the same shift-extension pathology:
    `v_j=R^j1-(-1/4)^j1` lies in `ker A` and
    `Bv_j=v_(j+1)-(-1/4)^jv_1`.  A quotient negative class can therefore be
    absorbed by the infinite radical without an invariant Hilbert lift.

    The parity cokernel makes the empty-resolvent statement exact.  In
    `L2(C/K)`, the odd adjoint has eigenvectors `sinh(ax)/C` for every
    spectral parameter `-a^2` (and `x/C` at zero).  The even adjoint has
    `[cosh(ax)-(M(a)/c)C]/C`, `M(a)=int K cosh(ax)`, at every nonresonant
    parameter, with a direct two-dimensional resonant space at `-1/4`.
    These are admissible because K/C kills all exponential growth.  Hence
    every complex number lies in the spectrum of each relevant maximal
    realization, not merely in an unverified range defect.

75. **Exact covariance crossing kernels exclude pointwise, TP, Stein, and
    local-Hardy proofs.**  Let `m=CK`, `M(s)=int_(-infinity)^s m`, and
    `c=1/4`.  For absolutely continuous u,
    `Var_mu(u)=iint H(s,t)u'(s)conjugate(u'(t))dsdt`, where
    `H(s,t)=M(min(s,t))[c-M(max(s,t))]/c`.  Orienting all jump edges gives
    `E_K(u)=iint G(s,t)u'(s)conjugate(u'(t))dsdt`, with
    `G_A(s,t)=int_(x<=min)int_(y>=max)J(y-x)K(x)K(y)dydx` and
    `G_P(s,t)=sum_(ell_q>=|s-t|)c_q
       int_(max(s,t)-ell_q)^(min(s,t))K(x)K(x+ell_q)dx`.
    Thus the gap is exactly Loewner positivity of `G-2cH` on the
    zero-integral derivative range.

    The stronger entrywise comparison is false for the actual kernel.  Set
    `a=(log2)/2` and `A_tail=int_a^infinity CK`.  Evenness gives
    `2cH(-a,a)=2A_tail^2`.  Since
    `J(log2)=4/(3sqrt2)<1`, the arch crossing is `<A_tail^2`; the q=2 prime
    interval has zero length.  Direct rational theta-tail bounds give the
    total q>=3 crossing `<6.6*10^(-5)` and `A_tail>0.010008`, hence
    `[G-2cH](-a,a)<-3.4*10^(-5)`.  Therefore the residual is not pointwise
    nonnegative or TP and has no positive crossing-measure, entrywise Schur,
    or scalar Hardy surplus.  Independently, localized oscillations have
    jump energy `O(log N)` but every positive local derivative minorant costs
    order `N^2`; no Muckenhoupt/Stein local bridge exists.  A genuinely
    nonlocal Loewner factorization remains the central gap, not a consequence
    of the one-dimensional covariance formula.

76. **The independent-product theta arch lift collapses its chart labels;
    diagonal label couplings are the precise remaining escape.**  Let
    `Omega={(y,m,sigma):y>=0,1<=m<=exp(y),sigma=+/-}`,
    `pi(y,m,sigma)=sigma(y-logm)`, and give it density
    `phi(y)w_y(m)dy`,
    `w_y(m)=C(y-logm)/sqrt(m)=(exp(y/2)/m+exp(-y/2))/2`.
    Then `pi_*nu=mu=CKdx`; its y-marginal and conditional law reproduce the
    item-58 theta variance decomposition.  Same-side prime edges become
    fixed-y divisor moves, and the reflected chart gives the corresponding
    cross-sign moves.  Expanding both K factors lifts the arch energy exactly
    to the sum over all chart pairs with kernel
    `J(|pi(omega)-pi(omega')|)`.

    This is not a product/Gibbs enlargement.  Along every pair of charts
    representing the same physical point, `J(h)=1/(2|h|)+O(1)`.  For chart
    functions f_m,f_n on a compact physical interval, the truncated cross
    energy contains
    `c int_(epsilon<|h|<delta)||f_m-translate_h f_n||_2^2 dh/|h|`
    and equals
    `2c log(1/epsilon)||f_m-f_n||_2^2+o(log(1/epsilon))`.
    Finite energy therefore forces all chart values to agree almost
    everywhere, exactly `F=u composed pi`.  On that domain the lifted form
    and variance reduce identically to `E_K` and `Var_mu`.  Finite-rate label
    resampling vanishes on pullbacks, while allowing independent labels gives
    infinite arch energy.  Thus the direct product cross-fiber lift does not
    repair the failed fixed-y gap; it collapses to the original RH-equivalent
    form by an actual local singularity argument.

    This does not exclude disintegrating each physical arch edge by a
    nonproduct symmetric coupling of the two conditional theta label laws.  A
    maximal or quantile coupling can become diagonal as the physical points
    coalesce and avoid the logarithmic label cost.  It must be constructed
    measurably, made compatible with the divisor moves, and given an
    independent sharp gap.  That coupled-label construction is the live
    latent mechanism; it is not supplied by the product expansion.

77. **Every true conditional-label coupling has the original Markov operator
    as a reducing block.**  Let `nu(dx,dm)=mu(dx)pi_x(dm)` and disintegrate a
    physical symmetric conductance C by measurable symmetric couplings
    `gamma_(x,y)` whose marginals are `pi_x,pi_y`.  For the pullback isometry
    `Ju(x,m)=u(x)` and conditional expectation `J^*`, exact marginal
    cancellation gives
    `E_tilde(Ju,F)=E(u,J^*F)`.  Jensen shows that `P=JJ^*` preserves the
    closed form domain and the two sectors are form-orthogonal.  Hence the
    represented self-adjoint generator reduces as
    `T_tilde=J T J^* direct-sum T_perp`, including the logarithmically
    singular arch domain, and
    `gap(T_tilde)=min(gap(T),inf spec(T_perp))`.  An upstairs sharp gap for
    any such Gibbs lift already contains the physical gap as a reducing
    block.

    The quantile coupling is a canonical exact example.  If `F_k(x)` are the
    conditional cumulative probabilities, couple labels by overlap of the
    intervals `[F_(k-1)(x),F_k(x))`.  It is measurable, reversible, and its
    mismatch probability is
    `|h|sum_k|F_k'(x)|+o(|h|)` as `y=x+h`; multiplication by
    `J(|h|)~1/(2|h|)` gives a finite local label-change density and avoids the
    item-76 collapse.  Nevertheless the reducing-block identity is exact.

    The item-53 divisor lift is importantly outside this no-go.  On a
    same-side q-edge its deterministic mass `m=qn -> n` has the correct
    destination marginal but a source marginal supported on multiples of q,
    with density ratio `sqrt(q)K(a)/K(a+logq)>1` relative to `pi_a`; it cannot
    be completed positively to a true coupling.  Thus it gives only a form
    compression, not a lumping.  Its fixed-y gap fails at item 54, so the
    remaining theta mechanism must retain this non-lumpable divisor source
    and obtain a genuinely cross-y archimedean repair.

78. **The exponential Green decomposition cannot repair the negative
    crossing entry by positive parallel allocation.**  Since
    `J(d)=sum_(k>=0)exp[-(2k+1/2)d]`, for `s<=t` the arch crossing kernel is
    exactly
    `G_A(s,t)=sum_k A_k(s)B_k(t)`, where
    `A_k(s)=int_(-infinity)^s exp(alpha_k x)K(x)dx` and
    `B_k(t)=int_t^infinity exp(-alpha_k y)K(y)dy`.
    Each `A_k(min)B_k(max)` is a positive one-dimensional Green kernel.  Each
    prime crossing is likewise the positive interval Gram
    `c_q int K(x)K(x+ell_q)
      1_[x,x+ell_q](s)1_[x,x+ell_q](t)dx`.

    A termwise Krein-string/Wronskian comparison or positive parallel-sum
    allocation splits the target H among these components and proves every
    surplus by a maximum-principle Green comparison or positive interval
    Gram.  Such surpluses have nonnegative pointwise kernels, so their sum
    would force `(G-2cH)(s,t)>=0` everywhere.  Item 75's exact value at
    `s=-(log2)/2,t=(log2)/2` is strictly negative.  Hence every allocation in
    this positive componentwise class fails.  A Loewner-positive surplus with
    signed cross-component kernels is not excluded, but it abandons the
    Green maximum principle and is exactly the full nonlocal Markov-gap
    factorization rather than a consequence of the exponential expansion.

79. **The canonical non-lumpable divisor/Gibbs lift fails both block gaps and
    its finite-energy domain is not Gibbs-stable.**  On the theta carrier let
    `P_y` be conditional expectation over `(m,sigma)` at fixed y and `P_d`
    conditional expectation over y at fixed `(m,sigma)`.  The product arch
    form of item 76 forces all physical chart representatives to agree.  But
    applying `P_y` to a pullback shifts its conditional mean by `log m`; if
    the result again had finite arch energy, periods `log2` and `log3` would
    force that mean to be constant.  Applying `P_d` similarly produces chart
    constants which finite energy forces to agree.  Except on the trivial
    sector, neither Gibbs half-step preserves the form domain.  Projecting
    back after each step destroys tensorization and produces a global
    compression.

    The two natural component comparisons also fail exactly.  At
    `y=log3`, one sign has weights
    `a_j=(3+j)/(2j sqrt3)`, `j=1,2,3`; for values `(0,1,0)` its variance is
    `15/(17sqrt3)`, while the complete fixed-y divisor source is
    `b=(log2)/sqrt2 K(log(3/2))<1/4<Var/2`, strictly on a neighborhood.  On a
    fixed label chart m, the ratio of arch conductance to the canonical
    y-refresh is
    `s_m J(|y-z|)/[C(y-logm)C(z-logm)]`, which tends to zero exponentially as
    z tends to infinity.  Thus neither conditional block has the sharp
    half-gap, and random-scan/two-block tensorization cannot supply it.
    Cross-compensating after the physical projection is precisely a full
    nonlocal Schur/Douglas inequality for `E_K`, not a canonical Gibbs
    consequence.  This closes the constructed non-lumpable theta lift while
    leaving no numerical quotient claim in the state.

80. **The first-order Darboux index mismatch is absorbed by the infinite
    threshold module.**  Put `D_K=K^(-1)D K`, `D_C=C^(-1)D C`, and
    `H=T-1/2`.  Translation invariance of the pole-free convolution gives the
    exact core intertwining `H(D_K-z)=(D_C-z)H`, and
    `D_K^*=-D_C` in `L2(CK)`.  Under multiplication by K and C these are D on
    the reciprocal spaces `L2((C/K)dx)` and `L2((K/C)dx)`.  Their maximal
    Callias realizations are Fredholm for every complex z:
    `ker(D_K-z)=0`, `coker(D_K-z)=span{exp(-conjugate(z)x)/C}`, so the index
    is -1; `ker(D_C-z)=span{exp(zx)/C}`, `coker(D_C-z)=0`, so the index is +1.
    Neither has a resolvent point.

    Index conjugacy would contradict `-1!=+1` only if H were a bounded
    Fredholm map between the graph complexes.  It is not: its kernel contains
    the infinite D_K-invariant derivative-radical chain, zero is the essential
    threshold and its range is not closed.  Every subcritical
    double-exponential rigging justified by item 62 still contains that whole
    chain.  Moreover item 62 supplies no local derivative and permits `Cv` to
    be bounded oscillatory, so a hypothetical subthreshold eigenfunction need
    not lie in either first-order graph domain.  On such an eigenfunction the
    intertwining also does not preserve the eigenspace.

    An exact actual-structure control is
    `H_0=-C^(-1) S K`, where S is any positive Gaussian convolution commuting
    with D.  In normalized coordinates it is the nonzero negative compact
    operator `-sqrt(K/C) S sqrt(K/C)`, hence has a negative eigenvalue and
    strong tails while satisfying the same first-order intertwining and index
    mismatch.  Thus the mismatch measures the reciprocal-weight cokernel and
    infinite radical, not the sign of H.

81. **Regular PSD feature completions cannot supply the cooperative
    ground-state decomposition.**  Write the required off-diagonal majorant
    as
    `q(t)=2cosh(t/2)-J(t)`.  If a positive-semidefinite kernel
    `R(x,y)=<Phi(x),Phi(y)>` satisfies `RK=0` and `R(x,y)>=q(|x-y|)` away
    from the diagonal, then `B=Q_W-R` has the desired cooperative
    off-diagonal sign.  The plastic constant `p`, defined by
    `p^3-p-1=0`, gives the exact sign threshold
    `q(t)>=0 iff t>=log p`; in particular, for `t>=log2`, the sharp simple
    bound used here is `q(t)>=(5/6)exp(t/2)`.

    Fix `D=log p`.  Positivity and `RK=0` force, for every x, the necessary
    local cancellation estimate
    `||Phi(x)|| int_(x-D)^(x+D)||Phi(y)||K(y)dy
       >= c_a exp(x/2)`
    once the far positive contribution is tested on any fixed central
    interval `[-a,a]`.  Here `c_a>0` is explicit.  Since the theta tail obeys
    `K(y)<=4pi^2 exp(9y/2-pi exp(2y))`, this contradicts every diagonal
    bound `R(t,t)<=M^2 exp(2A|t|)`.  It therefore excludes all finite
    theta/derivative/radical feature families and every exponentially bounded
    feature completion.

    Finite translated-radical ratios do not escape by their much faster
    growth.  For `Phi_j(x)=K(x-a_j)/K(x)-1`, the normalized feature directions
    become positively collinear uniformly on every fixed-width positive-tail
    window, whereas `RK=0` plus the preceding sign threshold requires an
    obtuse pair in each D-window.  Thus every finite translate family also
    fails.  A surviving feature map needs reciprocal-double-exponential norm
    and continuing direction rotation on tail windows.

    The implication from such a completion to RH also needs a stronger domain
    hypothesis than off-diagonal sign.  It is valid if B is a closed
    lower-semibounded jump form, its off-diagonal part is a negative Radon
    measure, K belongs to its extended domain, `BK=0`, and no
    derivative-of-delta diagonal term remains; then the ground-state/Picone
    identity gives `B(Ku,Ku)>=0`, while `R>=0`, hence `Q_W>=0`.  Without
    those hypotheses the implication is false: on the circle
    `B=(-D^2)^2-alpha(-D^2)`, `alpha>1`, is closed and semibounded, has zero
    off-diagonal distribution and `B1=0`, but is negative on `exp(ix)`.

    No unconditional no-go can exist, because if `Q_W>=0` one may take
    `R=Q_W` and `B=0`.  More quantitatively, a regular completion must cancel
    a far contribution `(1/4+o(1))exp(x/2)`.  Near-diagonal logarithmic
    cancellation alone requires
    `log(delta/epsilon_x) >= c exp(x/2)/K(x)`, of order
    `exp(pi exp(2x)-4x)`; a local differential correction is incompatible
    with form domination because its polynomial Fourier growth exceeds the
    logarithmic order of `Q_W`.  Hence the only unexcluded completion must
    reproduce the full prime atoms and ultraviolet singularity of the Weil
    form.  That is the original positivity problem rather than an independent
    completion mechanism.

82. **Sharp Erbar--Maas entropic curvature is ruled out by the exact
    threshold equality family.**  For a reversible jump process in the
    logarithmic-mean transport calculus, write
    `A(rho,psi)=<rhohat grad psi,grad psi>_e` and
    `B(rho,psi)=(1/2)<Deltahat rho grad psi,grad psi>_e
       -<rhohat grad psi,grad Delta psi>_e`, with `Delta=-T`.
    For `rho=1+epsilon h`, the logarithmic mean has expansion
    `rhohat=1+epsilon(h_x+h_y)/2+O(epsilon^2)`.  If
    `T psi=kappa psi`, differentiation at equilibrium gives exactly
    `d/depsilon|_0 [B(1+epsilon h,psi)-kappa A(1+epsilon h,psi)]
       =(1/2)int Delta h Gamma(psi)dmu`.
    At equilibrium the undifferentiated expression is
    `||Tpsi||^2-kappa E_K(psi)=0`.

    Hence a bound `B>=kappa A` for both signs of every bounded compact-core h
    forces `int Delta h Gamma(psi)dmu=0` for every such h.  Since
    `Gamma(psi)` is integrable, it defines a finite invariant measure.
    Strict positivity of the archimedean conductance makes the process
    irreducible, so uniqueness of finite invariant measures forces
    `Gamma(psi)` to be constant almost everywhere.

    Apply this at `kappa=1/2` to the exact threshold eigenfunction
    `psi=(-K''+K/4)/K`.  It belongs to the operator domain and has finite
    energy, but the positive theta tail gives
    `Gamma(psi)(x)>=c exp(7x)` for all sufficiently large positive x, so its
    carre du champ is not constant.  Thus Erbar--Maas entropic Ricci
    curvature at least `1/2` is false for the actual form.  Any proposed
    continuum variant that excludes this psi or the perturbations
    `1+/-epsilon h` cannot imply the full L2 gap without a separate domain
    approximation theorem.  Weighted W1 contraction without a Bochner
    inequality is not covered by this argument.

83. **The W1 theorem-to-gap bridge is valid, but every componentwise path
    coupling fails at the reflected prime geometry.**  Let `P_t=exp(tL)` and
    let d be a separating lower-semicontinuous metric.  If
    `W_d(P_t(x,.),P_t(y,.))<=exp(-t/2)d(x,y)` for all x,y,t, if `P_s` maps
    `L2(mu)` into `Lip_d` for some s>0, and if the item-62 exterior theorem is
    used to isolate spectrum below `1/2`, then the sharp Markov gap follows.
    Indeed a subthreshold eigenfunction `Tf=lambda f` becomes Lipschitz and
    would satisfy simultaneously
    `Lip_d(P_t f)=exp(-lambda t)Lip_d(f)` and
    `Lip_d(P_t f)<=exp(-t/2)Lip_d(f)`, impossible for
    `0<lambda<1/2`.  Thus the regularization hypothesis is essential; W1
    contraction by itself does not imply an L2 gap on this noncompact space.

    A noncircular local target uses finite Levy truncations.  Remove
    archimedean jumps of length below epsilon, call the resulting finite jump
    measure `Q_x^epsilon`, choose
    `Lambda_epsilon(x,y)>=max(Q_x^epsilon(R),Q_y^epsilon(R))`, and set
    `M_x=Q_x^epsilon+(Lambda_epsilon-Q_x^epsilon(R))delta_x`, with the
    analogous definition at y.  The exact coarse generator curvature is
    `kappa_d^epsilon(x,y)=
       [Lambda_epsilon d(x,y)-W_d(M_x,M_y)]/d(x,y)`.
    A uniform lower bound `liminf_(epsilon->0)kappa>=1/2`, compatible tight
    optimal couplings, and the preceding regularization would prove RH via
    item 57.  This is a concrete transport inequality, not yet a proof.

    It cannot be proved channel by channel with a path metric
    `d_h(x,y)=|h(x)-h(y)|`, `h'=w>0`.  Requiring every reversible translation
    `x<->x+log q` to be locally nonexpanding gives both
    `w(x+log q)<=w(x)` and its reverse.  Hence w has periods log 2 and log 3;
    their irrational ratio and continuity force w to be constant.  On the
    even half-line, put `ell=log2`, `F(x)=ell-x`, and
    `r(x)=c_2K(F(x))/C(x)`.  At `x=ell/4`, exact theta differentiation gives
    `r'(x)>0`.  The synchronous/maximal reflected-channel contribution is
    `r(x)[w(F(x))/w(x)-1]+r'(x)d_h(x,F(x))/w(x)`; for constant w this is the
    strictly positive value `r'(ell/4)ell/2`, i.e. strictly negative coarse
    curvature.  Matching the outward and reflected q=2 excesses cannot remove
    this term; the reflected imbalance dominates the outward derivative by
    explicit one-term theta bounds.

    Therefore every componentwise or tensorized prime/arch coupling is
    closed.  A surviving W1 proof must use a genuinely joint optimal coupling
    which cross-matches q=2 excess mass with different prime or archimedean
    channels, and most likely a non-path metric carrying explicit prime
    shortcuts.  No such uniform coupling or regularization theorem has yet
    been established.

84. **Uniform regeneration and metric-independent smoothing do not close the
    W1 bridge.**  There is no generator-level Doeblin decomposition with a
    state-independent reset measure of any positive rate.  If the proposed
    reset measure has a nonatomic part on a bounded central interval, the
    archimedean jump density from `x->+infinity` is
    `J(|x-y|)K(y)/C(x)=O(exp(-x))K(y)exp(y/2)`, while the prime part is
    atomic, so the common minorant is zero.  If it is purely atomic, each atom
    would have to lie in `{x+/-log q:q a prime power}` for every real x; the
    intersection is empty and the archimedean kernel has no atoms.  Thus a
    reset construction must be pair-dependent and genuinely multistep.

    Tail prime jumps do converge, after `x-log n=y`, to the common reset law
    `2exp(-y/2)K(y)dy` of total mass `1/2`.  Turning this asymptotic into a
    uniform exact coupling requires controlling the signed prime-tower
    mismatch at every translate.  The elementary routing estimate recovers
    the cumulative V of items 49 and 55; polynomial uniform control of V is
    already RH-strength.  Hence ordinary PNT proves only asymptotic coupling,
    with no sign at the sharp constant.

    The convenient global regularization hypothesis in item 83 also fails
    for every locally finite path metric at each fixed time.  Locally the
    symbol is `T~a(x)log|xi|`, where `a(x)=K(x)/C(x)->0`.  Packets
    `chi(x-R)exp(iNx)` give a normalized derivative after time s of order
    `N^(1-sa(R))/w(R)` for path density w; choosing R with `sa(R)<1` and then
    N large makes the `L2->Lip_d` norm infinite.  This is consistent with the
    unbounded exact threshold eigenfunctions.  A proof may use the weaker
    eigenfunction-specific statement, but item 62 supplies only exterior L2
    decay and not the required Lipschitz bound.  The active W1 route therefore
    needs both a joint non-path coupling and a new subthreshold-only
    regularity theorem.

85. **Breaking Xi divisibility also breaks the only closed transform action.**
    Put `H=T-1/2` and let `A_0` be the translation-invariant pole-free Weil
    convolution, so `H(f/K)=C^(-1)A_0f` on its domain.  Suppose a proposed
    analytic family has
    `H(f/K)=r+alpha*1+beta*S/C`, with `r in ker H`.  Both pole vectors lie in
    `Ran H`: `1=-2H1`, while differentiation of `A_0K=-2cC` gives
    `S/C=-(1/c)H(K'/K)`.  Since the range of a self-adjoint H is orthogonal to
    its kernel, projection onto `ker H` forces `r=0`.  A hidden threshold
    component therefore cannot enlarge the closed action.

    The bilateral Fourier--Laplace symbol of `A_0` is the normalized
    logarithmic derivative of xi, with a pole of nonzero residue at every
    distinct nontrivial-zero parameter.  If `A_0f` lies in the C,S pole span,
    its transform has only the two pole frequencies `+/-i/2`; removability of
    all nontrivial-zero poles forces `fhat(z_rho)=0` for every rho.  This uses
    no spectral-synthesis theorem.  The Hilbert-domain condition
    `f/K in L2(CK)` is `f in L2(C/K)`; the double-exponential reciprocal
    weight makes f integrable against every exponential, so `fhat` is entire
    and the transform argument is legitimate.

    Thus translations, derivatives, and convolution heat flows of K all have
    `fhat=m*Xi` and retain the noninjective common zero divisor.  The modes
    `cos(gamma x)/C` and `sin(gamma x)/C` at any real Xi zero annihilate every
    such correlation.  Dilation `K(ax)` and tilt `exp(ax)K(x)` instead have
    transforms `a^(-1)Xi(t/a)` and `Xi(t-ia)`; for a fixed zero gamma these
    are generically nonzero, so their Weil action contains the full zero
    exponential sum and does not close in the pole/threshold span.  Hence a
    second injective scale family cannot simultaneously retain the exact
    action used by the eigenfunction argument; canceling its zero
    coefficients would explicitly reinsert the zero divisor.

86. **Optimal cross-channel coupling fails for the natural bounded concave
    full-line metrics.**  For
    `d_a(x,y)=1-exp(-a|x-y|)` or `min(1,a|x-y|)`, ordinary PNT and theta-tail
    tightness give the exact inward-prime limits
    `beta_R^+ => beta^+(dz)=2exp(-z/2)K(z)dz` from `+R`, and
    `beta_R^- => beta^-(dz)=2exp(z/2)K(z)dz` from `-R`.
    Both have mass `1/2`, but they are distinct reflections.  Outward-prime
    mass and the archimedean contribution against uniformly bounded
    `Lip_(d_a)` tests vanish in these opposite tails.

    Let `g(z)=d_a(z,[0,infinity))`.  Then
    `int g(d beta^+-d beta^-)
       =2int_(-infinity)^0 d_a(z,0)K(z)
          [exp(-z/2)-exp(z/2)]dz>0`.
    Retain a compact interval on which this is positive, scale its values
    strictly inside `[0,1]`, prescribe the saturated endpoint values at
    `+R,-R`, and use McShane extension and clamping.  Strict triangle slack
    gives a global 1-Lipschitz Kantorovich potential `f_R`.  PNT convergence
    then yields a fixed `b_a>0` in the generator dual inequality, so
    `Lf_R(R)-Lf_R(-R)>-(1/2)d_a(R,-R)` for all sufficiently large R.  This
