    contradicts the necessary derivative of rate-`1/2` W1 contraction and is
    independent of the chosen coupling.

    The domain is valid: Lipschitz increments cancel `J(h)~1/(2|h|)` and the
    prime series converges absolutely.  These bounded metrics also cannot
    satisfy uniform `L2->Lip` smoothing, since the known unbounded threshold
    eigenfunction `(-K''+K/4)/K` remains unbounded under every `P_s`.
    Eigenfunction-specific subthreshold regularity is still not proved.

87. **The one-sided half-mass Faber--Krahn shortcut is false exactly at the
    threshold eigenfunction.**  The proposed sufficient lemma was
    `E_K(f)>=(1/2)||f||_mu^2` whenever f is supported on a measurable A with
    `mu(A)<=c/2`.  It would indeed imply the Markov gap: if
    `Tv=lambda v`, `int v dmu=0`, and `lambda<1/2`, then Markov truncation
    gives `v_+/- in D(E)` and
    `E(v_+/-)<=E(v,v_+/-)=lambda||v_+/-||^2`; at least one sign support has
    measure at most `c/2`.

    Take instead the exact sign-changing threshold eigenfunction
    `psi=(-K''+K/4)/K`, `Tpsi=(1/2)psi`.  Both sign sets have positive
    measure.  For a jump form,
    `E(psi,psi_+)=E(psi_+)-E(psi_-,psi_+)`, and the cross term is strictly
    negative because the archimedean conductance is positive between the two
    sign sets.  Consequently
    `E(psi_+)<(1/2)||psi_+||^2`, and identically for the negative part.  At
    least one of these is an exact form-domain counterexample with support
    mass at most `c/2`.  Thus nodal truncation cannot replace the signed
    cross-set interaction; adding that interaction recovers the full gap.

88. **Diamagnetic parity transfer loses exactly the magnetic-frustration
    term.**  Fold the full jump form to `(0,infinity)` with
    `mu_+=2CK dx`.  Every folded edge has a sign: `sigma=+1` on same-sign
    archimedean and translation-prime edges, while `sigma=-1` on cross-sign
    archimedean and reflected-prime edges.  The closed restrictions are
    `E_o(u)=(1/2)int c(dx,dy)|u(x)-sigma_xy u(y)|^2` and
    `E_e(v)=(1/2)int c(dx,dy)|v(x)-v(y)|^2`.  Edgewise diamagnetism gives
    `E_e(|u|)<=E_o(u)` and, by finite-rate truncation followed by monotone
    closed-form convergence,
    `|exp(-tT_o)u|<=exp(-tT_e)|u|`.  The singular corner kernel is integrable
    in the folded two-dimensional form, so no unproved trace at zero enters.

    Even a sharp even gap yields only
    `E_o(u)>=(1/2){||u||^2-|<|u|,1>|^2/||1||^2}`.  Semigroup domination has
    the same stationary remainder.  Subtracting `1/2` is not a Feynman--Kac
    killing, because the even shifted operator has a negative constant mode;
    replacing the identity by `I-P_0` adds a nonlocal rank-one term.

    The loss is structural.  On three vertices take unsigned weights
    `w_12=w_23=3/8`, `w_13=1/16`; the unsigned Laplacian has spectrum
    `{0,1/2,9/8}`.  Give edge 13 magnetic sign - and the other edges sign +.
    The signed Laplacian has bottom `(5-sqrt(19))/8<1/2`.  Its connected
    six-state double cover is a positive reversible reflection-symmetric
    chain whose even quotient has gap `1/2` and whose odd quotient has that
    smaller bottom.  In the actual form, item 48's log2/log3 sign triangle
    forbids a scalar gauge and its Picone identity displays the negative
    reflected-prime squares.  Therefore even transport plus diamagnetism,
    reversibility, or a two-sheet lift cannot prove the odd bound.  The exact
    missing magnetic-frustration inequality is algebraically the odd
    `1/2` lower bound itself.

89. **The actual odd Weil semigroup and every resolvent fail eventual
    positivity because the threshold kernel is not a lattice.**  On
    `H_+=L2((0,infinity),2CK dx)`, let `H_o=T_o-1/2`, `s=S/C`, and
    `W_o=H_o-2|s><s|`.  This is a lower-bounded self-adjoint rank-one
    perturbation on `D(T_o)`.  Its essential spectral bottom is zero by the
    item-62 exterior compression and odd tail packets.  The positive radical
    `u_0=(-K')/K` lies in `D(W_o)`, satisfies `W_ou_0=0`, and is strictly
    positive.

    If `exp(-tW_o)` were individually eventually positive and a negative
    spectral bottom `lambda_*` existed, then
    `exp(t lambda_*)exp(-tW_o)` would converge strongly to a nonzero positive
    bottom spectral projection.  Its range would contain `g>=0`, but
    self-adjoint orthogonality gives `<g,u_0>=0`, impossible.  Eventual
    positivity would therefore force `W_o>=0`; then the semigroup would
    converge strongly to the kernel projection `P_0`, making `P_0` positive.
    A positive orthogonal projection has modulus-closed range: for
    `h=P_0h`, positivity gives `|h|<=P_0|h|`, and norm contractivity forces
    equality.

    The actual kernel is not modulus closed.  Put `w=-K'`, `P=-D^2`.
    Translation invariance and `A_ow=0` give
    `u_j=P^jw/K in ker W_o` for all j, with cutoff convergence in the closed
    domain.  The ratio `u_1/u_0=-K'''/K'` is nonconstant and is strictly
    monotone far in the positive theta tail.  Choose x0 there with
    `2x0!=log n` for every prime power and form a linear combination h of
    `u_0,u_1` having a simple zero at x0 and no other nearby zero.  Then
    `K|h|` has a local cusp.

    Applying the actual diagonal archimedean finite part to
    `phi(x)=|x|` produces, for `0<|x|<delta`, the non-smooth term
    `-delta+|x|log(delta/|x|)+|x|`.  The cross archimedean kernel, S-rank
    term, diagonal multiplication, and every translated/reflected prime term
    are smooth near x0; the choice `2x0!=log n` excludes a reflected cusp,
    and theta tails justify differentiated summation.  Hence
    `A_o(K|h|)` cannot vanish, so `|h|` is not in the kernel.  This
    contradicts positivity of `P_0` and rules out eventual positivity.

    A positive resolvent is impossible by the same argument: normalized
    powers first converge to a negative bottom projection if one exists;
    otherwise they converge to the nonpositive kernel projection.  Thus
    neither convolution powers nor resolvent positivity can turn the positive
    odd radical into a proof.

90. **Every scalar positive Feynman--Kac dominator of the odd Markov sector
    has bottom below `1/2`.**  On the positive half-line the odd archimedean
    kernel is already cooperative because `J(|a-b|)>J(a+b)`.  Translation
    prime edges have difference squares, while reflected edges have
    `|v(a)+v(log n-a)|^2`.  The canonical modulus operator `H_abs` keeps the
    former terms and replaces every reflected plus-square by the corresponding
    minus-square, giving rigorous Kato domination after finite-rate truncation
    and closed-form passage.

    Test it on `v=w/K`, `w=-K'>0`.  The exact radical identity and
    `int S(-K')=(1/2)int CK=c/2=1/8` give
    `E_K(v)-(1/2)||v||_mu^2=1/32`.  Modulus replacement subtracts
    `4sum_n c_n I_n`, where
    `I_n=int_0^(log n)w(a)w(log n-a)da`.  The n=2 term alone is larger than
    `1/32`: on `[8/25,log2-8/25]` both arguments lie in
    `[8/25,19/50]`, direct one-term theta differentiation gives `w>4/5`,
    hence `I_2>4/125`; and `c_2=log2/sqrt2>23/50`.  Therefore
    `4c_2I_2>368/6250>1/32` and
    `<v,H_abs v><(1/2)||v||^2` exactly.

    This is not peculiar to the canonical choice.  Generator-level Kato
    necessity forces any scalar positive dominator to have off-diagonal jump
    magnitude at least the absolute odd coefficient and no larger diagonal
    killing than the original.  Its quadratic value on positive v is
    therefore at most that of `H_abs`.  The item-48 smooth sign triangle also
    gives nontrivial real and U(1) gauge holonomy.  Thus no scalar modulus,
    gauge, or Feynman--Kac transfer can combine an even transport gap with the
    odd sector; a two-sheet signed lift simply restores the original
    anti-invariant gap.

91. **Translate annihilation forces infinitely many sign changes, but the
    negative ground state has no Sturm bound.**  For `a>0` put
    `w_a(x)=K(x-a)-K(x+a)>0` on the positive half-line.  If a real locally
    integrable g satisfies `int g(x)w_a(x)dx=0` for every a and has finitely
    many sign changes, then g is zero.  Indeed, after the last sign change and
    for `a>R`, its positive mass on `[a-delta,a+delta]` is at most a constant
    times `K(a-R)`, because `w_a>=K(delta)/2` there while
    `w_a(x)<=K(a-R)` on the fixed opposite-sign core.  Tiling and the
    double-exponential K tail give `g in L1`.  The odd extension G then obeys
    `K*G=0`; hence `Xi Ghat=0`.  Continuity of `Ghat` and discreteness of the
    real Xi zeros force `Ghat=0`.

    Every `w_a/K` is an exact odd Weil radical.  Thus a negative eigenfunction
    v of `W_o` is orthogonal to all of them, and the lemma applied to `g=Cv`
    proves that v has infinitely many sign changes.  Absolute convergence is
    just Hilbert Cauchy--Schwarz; no spectral synthesis or RH is used.

    The needed converse ground-state nodal theorem is unavailable for the
    actual structure.  Item 32's negative TP2 Green minor destroys variation
    diminution, item 48's `(-,-,+)` triangle prevents a cooperative gauge,
    and infinitely many reflected prime lines prevent a finite-frustration
    count.  A same-coordinate control makes the logical gap exact: if M is
    the closed span of the positive radicals and gamma is a real Xi zero,
    `sin(gamma x)/C lies in M^perp`; the bounded operator
    `P_(M^perp)-2|phi><phi|` has the same positive radical continuum and
    essential zero threshold but a simple negative bottom with infinitely
    many signs and the item-62 tail.  Actual far-tail oscillatory packets also
    have local cost `(K(R)/C(R))log N->0`.  Hence radical orthogonality,
    threshold tails, and self-adjointness cannot bound nodal count; a new
    arithmetic oscillation theorem would already carry the missing sign.

92. **Prime holonomy supplies no positive-capacity magnetic cycles, and the
    q=2 translation-first allocation has essentially zero central capacity.**
    On the half-line, every finite word in prime translations and reflections
    is affine, `W(a)=epsilon*a+b`, with epsilon equal to `(-1)` to the number
    of reflections.  A pi-flux word has `epsilon=-1` and closes only at the
    single point `a=b/2`, a zero-conductance set.  A word closing on positive
    measure is the identity and has trivial flux.  Countably many dense
    log2/log3 fixed points therefore do not produce a positive cycle measure.

    The item-48 three-cell holonomy also has an exact continuum zero mode.
    With its centers x1,x2,x3, assign the same odd smooth profile `phi(t)` on
    all three cells.  The q=2 and q=3 translation squares and the q=8 and q=16
    reflected squares vanish identically, including their orientation.  A
    center-sampled magnetic triangle erases this mode and cannot certify a
    continuum cycle gap.  Adding a deterministically chosen arch edge is
    invalid: its marginal lies on a one-dimensional graph and is singular to
    the two-dimensional archimedean/pole conductance, hence has infinite L2
    congestion.

    The same obstruction is quantitative for q=2.  Put `ell=log2`,
    `R(x)=ell-x`, and `w=-K'`.  Its reflected demand on `0<x<ell/2` is
    `D_2=c_2int w(x)w(Rx)|u(x)-u(Rx)|^2dx`.  Any routing whose first edge is
    one of the two q=2 translations requires
    `w(x+ell)/w(Rx)+w(Rx+ell)/w(x)>=1`.  Near `x=ell/2` the left side is
    `2w(3ell/2)/w(ell/2)<10^(-5)` by one-term theta bounds.  Thus essentially
    all central demand must diffuse into the arch/pole network before using a
    translation.  A curve-valued arch repair remains singular for the same
    dimension reason.

    The legitimate continuum cut `A=(0,ell/2)` does not violate the total
    diffuse arch/pole capacity, so this is not a counterexample to the actual
    inequality.  What remains is a two-parameter multistep cycle measure with
    all prime and arch marginals dominated at congestion one.  Without an
    explicit such measure, this is precisely the full odd Picone inequality,
    not a magnetic consequence of dense finite cycles.

93. **The U/Abel chord metric exactly closes the opposite-tail reset cost and
    is the live transport candidate.**  Put `U=-(log K)'`.  Strict
    log-concavity from item 15 makes U strictly increasing and odd.  With
    `s=tanh(x/2)` the exact differentiated radical identity gives
    `L U=-(1/2)U-cs`, `c=1/4`.  The two tail reset laws are
    `beta^+(dz)=2exp(-z/2)K(z)dz` and
    `beta^-(dz)=2exp(z/2)K(z)dz`, each of mass `1/2`.  The latter strictly
    stochastically dominates the former, and integration by parts gives
    `int U(d beta^--d beta^+)=4int U S K=-4int S K'=2c=1/2`.

    Let `T_beta` be their increasing quantile transport.  It is fixed-point
    free and `T_beta(z)>z`.  Choose a bounded nonconstant continuous
    locally-Lipschitz invariant v, `v(T_beta z)=v(z)`, by defining a matching
    loop on one compact fundamental interval and extending along its orbits.
    For epsilon positive define the Euclidean feature embedding
    `F_epsilon(x)=(U(x),epsilon v(x))` and
    `d_epsilon(x,y)=|F_epsilon(x)-F_epsilon(y)|_2`.  This metric is separating,
    continuous, locally `O(|x-y|)` and hence compatible with the
    `J(h)~1/(2|h|)` Dini condition.  It has finite mu second moment, and a
    nonconstant invariant v makes it genuinely non-path.

    The quantile plan `(id,T_beta)#beta^+` cancels the v-coordinate and has
    cost `int[U(T_beta z)-U(z)]d beta^+=1/2`.  Since U is 1-Lipschitz for
    `d_epsilon`, Kantorovich duality gives the reverse inequality.  Therefore
    exactly `W_(d_epsilon)(beta^+,beta^-)=1/2`; no opposite-tail residual is
    hidden in the construction.  PNT passage is valid with the U weight
    because `KU=-K'` retains theta-Schwartz tails.

    This tail identity is not the global coupling.  Full finite-R curvature
    may fail at reflected q=2 jumps, and no tight family of optimal truncated
    couplings has yet been constructed.  Item 94 removes the former
    eigenfunction-specific Lipschitz hypothesis; all-pairs contraction is now
    the only unresolved lemma on this route.

94. **Finite-second-moment W1 contraction implies the sharp L2 gap without
    semigroup smoothing (proved).**  Normalize `pi=mu/c`.  Let d be a
    separating metric whose bounded Lipschitz functions are dense in
    `L2(pi)`, and suppose
    `M_2=iint d(x,y)^2dpi(x)dpi(y)<infinity`.  If
    `W_d(P_t(x,.),P_t(y,.))<=exp(-t/2)d(x,y)` for every x,y,t, then for every
    bounded d-Lipschitz f, Kantorovich duality gives
    `Lip_d(P_tf)<=exp(-t/2)Lip_d(f)`.  Consequently
    `Var_pi(P_tf)<=(1/2)M_2 Lip_d(f)^2 exp(-t)`.

    On centered f, self-adjoint spectral calculus writes the left side as
    `int_[0,infinity)exp(-2lambda t)dnu_f(lambda)`.  For each `a<1/2`,
    multiplication by `exp(2at)` and passage to infinity prove that the
    spectral mass on `[0,a]` vanishes.  Taking a countable union and then
    using density shows that the spectral projection of T on `[0,1/2)`
    vanishes on the mean-zero space.  Thus the item-57 Markov gap follows.
    No pointwise representative or regularization of a hypothetical
    eigenfunction is used.

    The hypotheses hold for every item-93 chord metric.  Indeed
    `d_epsilon^2<=2U(x)^2+2U(y)^2+4epsilon^2||v||_infinity^2`, and the theta
    tail gives `U in L2(pi)`.  Also U is a continuous strictly increasing
    bijection of the line.  After pushing pi forward by U, bounded
    compactly-supported Euclidean-Lipschitz functions of U pull back to
    bounded `d_epsilon`-Lipschitz functions and are dense in `L2(pi)`.
    Therefore contraction alone would close the route; item 95 then supplies
    a direct obstruction for the first calibrated metric family.

95. **The U path metric and all sufficiently small Abel chords have an exact
    full-generator curvature obstruction.**  For a finite Levy cutoff and
    `d_U(x,y)=|U(x)-U(y)|`, write every contact potential as
    `f'=theta U'`, `|theta|<=1`.  Differentiating the full generator and using
    Fubini gives a signed coefficient measure `M_(x,eta)`; optimizing theta
    pointwise yields
    `kappa_eta(x)=1/2+c sech(x/2)^2/(2U'(x))
       -2M_(x,eta)^-(R)/(C(x)U'(x))`.
    This is the joint Kantorovich optimum and already includes cross-channel
    cancellation.

    A simpler exact contact supplies a wide rational margin.  Put `ell=log2`,
    `x=3ell/4`, `A=-U(ell/8)`, and in the U-coordinate define
    `g(z)=z-2A` for `z<=A`, `g(z)=-z` for `A<=z<=0`, and `g(z)=z` for
    `z>=0`.  It is 1-Lipschitz and locally equals z at `U(x)`.  Stieltjes
    integration gives the exact correction
    `d_r L(g(U)-U)(x)=2int_A^0 partial_r Q_r^-(z)dz`.

    On physical thresholds `a in [-ell/8,0]`, every backward prime target is
    included.  The q=2 rate derivative, after multiplication by C(x), is
    `c_2K(ell/4)[U(ell/4)-(1/2)tanh(x/2)]>1/2`; every q>=3 derivative is
    positive.  The archimedean loss is at most
    `[-J'(x)+(C'/C)J(x)]int_(-infinity)^0K
       <(17/8)(1/8)=17/64`.
    Also `U'(x)C(x)<40`, `U(ell/8)>3/2`, and `U'(x)>36`.  Therefore the
    wrong-sign mass exceeds `45/5120`, while half the exact drift reserve
    `c s'(x)/(2U'(x))` is below `1/576`.  Hence
    `-(L(g o U))'(x)/U'(x)<1/2` strictly.  The cumulative interval stays a
    positive distance from the J singularity, so this is uniform in the Levy
    cutoff and dominated convergence removes it.  Thus no joint coupling of
    all prime and archimedean channels can give rate `1/2` for `d_U`.
    The 200-bit Arb certificate is
    `scripts/u_abel_curvature_certificate.py`.

    For a smooth chord `Phi_epsilon=(U,epsilon v)`, combine F and v in the
    unit tangent direction at x.  The resulting global 1-Lipschitz contact has
    witnessed curvature
    `-[U'(LF)'(x)+epsilon^2v'(Lv)'(x)]/
       [U'(x)^2+epsilon^2v'(x)^2]`.
    It converges to the strict d_U countervalue as epsilon tends to zero.
    Hence every sufficiently small chord fails; if `v'(x)=0`, the same
    obstruction holds for every epsilon.

96. **Any surviving smooth Abel chord is forced to vanish on every distinct Xi zero
    (proved reduction; folded completeness unresolved).**  Let v be a
    nonconstant smooth bounded invariant of the tail quantile map,
    `v(T_beta x)=v(x)`.  Its Abel cells have length
    `Delta(x)=[x/(2pi)]exp(-2x)(1+O(1/x))`, with uniform distortion.  Every
    nonconstant smooth loop therefore has, in each large cell, positive and
    negative derivative intervals of length comparable to Delta and
    `|v'|` comparable to `1/Delta`.

    The globally 1-Lipschitz secant feature potential for
    `Phi_epsilon=(U,epsilon v)` shows that rate-`1/2` contraction necessarily
    implies `-epsilon^2v'(Hv)'<=cU's'`.  The translated Weil expansion for
    `H=T-1/2`, together with the theta-Schwartz bounds on `Kv`, gives a uniform
    bound on `(Hv)''`.  Comparing the two derivative-sign intervals in each
    shrinking Abel cell then proves `(Hv)'=O(xexp(-x))`.

    Put `F=Kv`.  The translated explicit formula is absolutely locally
    differentiable because `Fhat` is rapidly decreasing in every fixed strip
    and `N(T)=O(TlogT)`.  A one-sided Laplace transform of the estimate on Hv
    has a holomorphic continuation past every exponent
    `i z_rho=(Re(rho)-1/2)+i Im(rho)`.  Removability of each resulting pole
    forces `Fhat(z_rho)=0` at every distinct nontrivial Xi zero; this uses only
    `0<Re(rho)<1`, not RH.  No multiplicity upgrade is implicit: the
    logarithmic derivative has a simple pole whose residue is the zero
    multiplicity, so this argument kills only the value of Fhat.  Full
    divisibility would require independent jet vanishing and is not claimed.

    On a compact fundamental cell I0 this condition is exactly
    `int_(I0)w(y)G_rho(y)dy=0` for every rho, where
    `G_rho(y)=sum_(n in Z)K(T_beta^n y)exp(-iz_rho T_beta^n y)
       (T_beta^n)'(y)`.
    The series is smoothly absolutely convergent.  Constants solve the system
    because their unfolded transform is Xi.  No proof yet shows that they are
    the only solutions, and no nonconstant solution has been constructed.
    This compact zero-set/completeness system is the exact current lemma; merely
    invoking the hereditary-completeness assertion rejected in item 63 would
    be circular.

97. **Full-divisor bounded-radical rigidity holds, but curvature cannot see
    zero multiplicities.**  Suppose the stronger hypothesis
    `Fhat=Xi h` with h entire holds for `F=Kv` and bounded v.  Positivity of K
    gives `|Fhat(t+iy)|<=||v||_infinity Xi(iy)` and hence `|h(iy)|` bounded.
    Functional-equation bounds on `Im z=+/-1` and strip
    Phragmen--Lindelof make h exponential type at most `pi/4`.
    Paley--Wiener--Schwartz represents `h(i.)` by a distribution supported in
    `[-pi/4,pi/4]`.  The exposed-support theorem for its Fourier--Laplace
    transform on the analytic arc `a -> exp(2ia)`, applied to the exact theta
    tail, shows that `|Kv|<=MK` forces this support to be `{0}`.  Boundedness
    removes derivatives of the point mass, so h and v are constant.

    This theorem does not apply to item 96.  At a multiplicity-m zero of Xi,
    `Xi'/Xi=m/(z-z_0)+holomorphic`; the Weil action detects only `Fhat(z_0)`.
    Translation derivatives merely multiply the same vanished residue.
    Transform jets require physical moments or modulations, but those destroy
    the exact Abel-quantile tail equality.  Thus neither simplicity nor full
    divisibility has been assumed; multiplicity is an exact obstruction to
    using the conditional rigidity theorem.

98. **Both deterministic sharp tail couplings have exact full-generator
    countercontacts.**  For an Abel phase-circle feature
    `p=(cos(2pi h),sin(2pi h))`, `h(T_beta x)=h(x)+1`, the chord metric equals
    d_U on every pair `(x,T_beta x)`.  Exact theta/CDF bounds put
    `T_beta(3log2/4)` in `(3log2/4,7log2/8)`.  The item-95 folded potential is
    a saturating contact on this pair, and its full-generator defect is
    strictly positive throughout that interval.  Hence every phase and every
    radius fail, not merely small perturbations.  The certificate is included
    in `scripts/u_abel_curvature_certificate.py`.

    There is another exact tail plan: keep the common part of `beta^+` and
    `beta^-` on the diagonal and reflect the negative surplus to the positive
    deficit.  Any metric adjoining an arbitrary even scalar or vector feature
    to U equals d_U on every reflection pair.  At `x=log2/4`, an explicit odd
    folded potential has slope +1 on `[-U(log2/2),U(log2/2)]`, slope -1 out to
    `+/-U(3log2/4)`, and slope +1 thereafter.  It saturates `(-x,x)`.  The q=2
    term is `>3/25`, the complete archimedean loss is `<13/200`, and the U
    drift reserve is `<11/500`, leaving a two-endpoint generator defect
    `>66/1000`.  All q>=3 terms have the favorable sign.  Thus every
    reflection-preserving even-feature metric fails for every feature scale.
    The 160-bit Arb certificate is
    `scripts/even_feature_reflection_certificate.py`.

    These results do not exclude a genuinely nondeterministic ordered tail
    coupling.  A sharp U-calibrated metric would need such a coupling supported
    on pairs where its cost equals `U(y)-U(x)` and where every saturating
    d_U-Lipschitz contact obeys the generator inequality.  Feasibility of that
    support relation is the live finite-measure/Hall gate; neither deterministic
    graph may be reused.

99. **The universal sharp-tail Hall/Strassen gate is closed by an exact
    certificate.**  Put `d_U(x,y)=|U(x)-U(y)|`.  Define `x R y`, for `x<=y`,
    when every d_U-Lipschitz contact satisfying
    `f(y)-f(x)=U(y)-U(x)` obeys
    `Lf(y)-Lf(x)+(U(y)-U(x))/2<=0`; include the diagonal.  If
    `Qhat_x=U_#Q_x` is the full jump kernel in U-coordinates, then for `x<y`,
    `u=U(x)`, `v=U(y)`, the exact contact supremum is
    `-c[tanh(y/2)-tanh(x/2)]
       +2int_(-infinity)^u[A_(x,y)(alpha)]_+dalpha
       +2int_v^infinity[-B_(x,y)(alpha)]_+dalpha`,
    where
    `A=Qhat_y(-infinity,alpha]-Qhat_x(-infinity,alpha]` and
    `B=Qhat_y[alpha,infinity)-Qhat_x[alpha,infinity)`.  This follows by
    writing a contact as `g(xi)=xi+H(xi)`, with H zero on `[u,v]` and
    `H' in [-2,0]` outside, and optimizing pointwise after Fubini.
    Levy cutoffs pass by dominated convergence: the contact is unchanged
    between its endpoints, the singular cumulative terms have the
    nonselected sign, and theta-Schwartz bounds control the spatial and prime
    tails.  The formula is lower semicontinuous (continuous off the diagonal),
    so R is closed.  It is transitive because a contact across `x<z` also
    contacts each subpair `x<y<z`, and the two generator defects add.  Thus R
    is a closed preorder, a fact needed below rather than an assumed diagonal
    decomposition of a coupling.

    Let `I=[3/80,21/80]`, `ell=log 2`, and `theta=2/5`.  For
    `a,b in I`, an explicit contact at `(-a,b)` folds its U-slope from +1 to
    -1 on the two exterior intervals
    `[b-ell,b-ell+theta(ell-a-b)]` and
    `[ell-a-theta(ell-a-b),ell-a]`.  For the left fold set
    `q_0=ell-b`,
    `q_1=(3/5)ell+(2/5)a-(3/5)b`, and
    `M=2[U(q_0)-U(q_1)]`.  Its selected q=2 contribution is
    `P(a,b)=M(ell/sqrt(2))
       [K(ell-b)/C(b)-K(ell+a)/C(a)]`.
    Its exact archimedean correction is
    `A(a,b)=int_(q_1)^(q_0)2[U(t)-U(q_1)]K(t)
       [J(b+t)/C(b)-J(t-a)/C(a)]dt
       +M int_(q_0)^infinity K(t)
       [J(b+t)/C(b)-J(t-a)/C(a)]dt`.
    The full contact defect is bounded below by
    `P(a,b)+P(b,a)+A(a,b)+A(b,a)
       -c[tanh(a/2)+tanh(b/2)]`.
    Every omitted `q>=3` term is nonnegative: the two fold plateaus have the
    favorable orientation and
    `z -> K(log(q)-z)/C(z)` is increasing on the required compact range,
    since its logarithmic derivative is
    `U(log(q)-z)-tanh(z/2)/2>0`.

    The 144-bit Arb certificate
    `scripts/universal_tail_hall_certificate.py` proves this lower bound
    strictly positive on all 1024 exact-rational boxes covering `I^2`.  It
    also proves the endpoint monotonicities, `U'>0`, separation from the J
    pole, all theta derivative tails, and the infinite-integral bound
    `K(t)<=K(1)exp[-40(t-1)]` for `t>=1`.  Hence
    `R intersect((-I) times I)` is empty.

    Finally subtract the common measure
    `lambda(dz)=2exp(-|z|/2)K(z)dz` from the tail laws beta+ and beta-.  The
    residual source at `-t` and target at `t` have identical density
    `r(t)=4K(t)sinh(t/2)`, `t>0`.  If an R-coupling of beta+ to beta- existed,
    closed-preorder Strassen duality for finite equal-mass Borel measures on
    the Polish line would give all R-increasing integral
    inequalities.  Subtracting the same lambda preserves them, and Strassen's
    theorem would reconstruct an R-coupling of the residual laws.  The same
    certificate proves
    `int_I r > int_((0,infinity)\I)r`; thus every residual coupling charges
    `(-I) times I` by at least `2int_I r-int r>0`, a contradiction.

    For any metric `d>=d_U`, equality in the sharp tail Kantorovich lower
    bound forces its reset coupling to be ordered and supported on pairs with
    `d=d_U`.  Rate-1/2 contraction would make each such equality pair belong
    to R.  The preceding contradiction therefore excludes every genuinely
    nondeterministic sharp U-calibrated chord as well as the deterministic
    plans in item 98.  No finite-to-infinite extrapolation or sampled
    positivity is used.

100. **General path-metric curvature is an exact linear supersolution
    problem (live).**  Let `h'=w>0`, `d_h(x,y)=|h(x)-h(y)|`, and first impose
    a finite Levy cutoff.  In the distributional representation
    `(Lf)'(x)=int f'(z)M_x(dz)`, the atom at x has coefficient minus the total
    cutoff rate, every moving prime target `x+/-log q` has positive atom
    `r_q^+/-=c_qK(x+/-log q)/C(x)`, and the remaining densities are
    `M_x(z)=A_L(x,z)-sum_(log q>x-z)(r_q^-)'(x)` for `z<x`, and
    `M_x(z)=A_R(x,z)+sum_(log q>z-x)(r_q^+)'(x)` for `z>x`, where
    `A_L=C(x)^(-1)int_(-infinity)^z
       [-J'(x-y)+(tanh(x/2)/2)J(x-y)]K(y)dy>0`,
    `A_R=C(x)^(-1)int_z^infinity
       [-J'(y-x)-(tanh(x/2)/2)J(y-x)]K(y)dy>0`, and
    `(r_q^+/-)'=-r_q^+/-[U(x+/-log q)+tanh(x/2)/2]`.

    A local path contact has `f'(x)=w(x)` and `|f'|<=w`.  Comparing it with
    `f=h` and optimizing pointwise gives exactly
    `sup_contact (Lf)'(x)=(Lh)'(x)+2int M_x^-(dz)w(z)`;
    the positive prime-target atoms never enter the negative part.  Therefore
    local rate-1/2 curvature for every contact is equivalent to the
    positive-homogeneous linear family
    `B_x[w]=-(Lh)'(x)-w(x)/2-2int M_x^-w>=0` for every x.
    Cutoff removal and the admissible class still require the same local Dini
    control as in item 95.  The formula specializes to item 95 for `w=U'`.
    The exact current subproblem is to construct a continuous positive
    supersolution w with the required tails and density properties, or to
    exhibit a finite analytic positive left/Farkas functional contradicting
    all such w.  A grid degeneration is only a discovery signal and cannot
    settle this subproblem.

101. **Prime-bearing Garland triangles have insufficient mass; pure-arch
    triangles have zero tail gap (mixed class live).**  Put
    `I(l)=int_R K(x)K(x+l)dx` and
    `P=sum_(q=p^k)c_q I(log q)`.  Evenness, monotonicity of
    `U=-(log K)'`, and `K(t)<=K(a)exp[-U(a)(t-a)]` for `t>=a` give
    `I(l)<=2K(0)int_(l/2)^infinity K(t)dt
       <=2K(0)K(l/2)/U(l/2)`.
    The 160-bit Arb certificate
    `scripts/triangle_prime_mass_certificate.py` proves the q=2 contribution
    `<1/125`, the q=3 contribution `<13/20000`, and `K(0)<1/2`.
    For q>=4, overcounting all integers and using
    `K(log q/2)<=4pi^2 q^(9/4)exp(-pi q)`, `U>1`, gives the majorant
    `b_q=4pi^2 log(q)q^(7/4)exp(-pi q)`.  The certificate proves
    `b_4<11/5000` and `b_(m+1)/b_m<1/8`, hence the tail is `<13/5000`.
    Therefore exactly
    `P<1/125+13/20000+13/5000=9/800<1/72`.

    For an S3-symmetric triangle measure tau, write `nu=tau_1` and
    `eta=tau_12`.  If `6eta` is dominated by the physical ordered edge
    conductance, `6nu=r mu`, and every conditional link has normalized gap
    at least lambda, the Garland calculation gives skeleton gap at least
    `r(2-1/lambda)`.  Since a normalized link gap is at most 2 and
    `mu(R)=c=1/4`, total triangle mass T satisfies `r=24T`; reaching the
    required 1/2 therefore needs `T>=1/72` even in the best case.
    Each prime edge consumes its ordered physical prime mass, whose total is
    `2P`.  Consequently, if `T_k` is the mass of triangles with k prime
    edges, `sum kT_k<=P`.

    The support geometry further forbids k=2.  After fixing the two discrete
    prime powers and orientations, two prime edges put a triple on a
    one-dimensional affine set; its third-edge marginal lies on a fixed
    displacement line and is singular to the two-dimensional archimedean
    conductance.  Domination therefore forces the third displacement to be a
    prime-power logarithm too.  The resulting multiplicative relation makes
    all three prime powers powers of the same prime.  Thus the only types are
    k=0, k=1, and k=3, with `T_1+3T_3<=P`.  Every entirely prime-bearing
    complex has `T<=P<1/72` and cannot reach the sharp Garland constant.

    A purely archimedean skeleton cannot help alone: domination by the arch
    conductance and the item-51 tail packets give its Rayleigh gap zero under
    any constant marginal relation `6nu=r mu`.  These two scalar statements
    do not yet exclude a mixture carrying arbitrary pure-arch triangle mass
    together with a small prime-bearing part; the live Hodge sublemma is a
    quantitative link-gap/prime-budget inequality for that mixed class.

    Total prime-bearing mass alone cannot supply that inequality.  If
    `tau=tau_0+tau_p`, `h=dnu_p/dnu`, and P is the Markov operator of eta,
    the exact link identities are
    `int E_(link,x)(u)dnu=E_eta(u)` and
    `int Var_(m_x)(u)dnu=||u||_nu^2-||Pu||_nu^2`.
    A uniform link gap lambda implies graph gap
    `delta>=2-1/lambda`, while
    `E_(eta_p)(u)<=2int|u|^2h dnu`.  Tail packets only force a local lower
    intensity of h on those packets, not a lower value of `int h`.
    Indeed, on a nonatomic probability space `X=A disjoint-union B` with
    `pi(B)=epsilon`, take `tau=T pi^3`, color
    `tau_0=1_(A^3)tau`, and `tau_p=tau-tau_0`.  Every link and the skeleton
    have gap 1, although the prime-colored fraction
    `1-(1-epsilon)^3` tends to zero and the pure part has no coercivity on B.
    This abstract countermodel lacks the physical prime-line support; it
    proves that the remaining obstruction must use local tail intensity and
    prime/arch capacity geometry rather than P/T alone.

102. **Every dominated S3-symmetric triangle/Garland skeleton has global gap
    zero.**  Let `e=a+p` be the ordered physical arch-plus-prime edge measure,
    let tau be a finite S3-symmetric triple measure of mass T, put
    `nu=tau_1=4T mu`, `eta=tau_12`, and assume `6eta<=e`.  Mutual singularity
    of the diffuse arch measure and the prime shift lines, together with item
    101's support argument, decomposes tau into types `tau_0,tau_1,tau_3`
    having respectively zero, one, and three prime edges; exactly two is
    impossible.

    For a one-prime triangle with prime edge AB and arch closures AZ,ZB,
    `|u(A)-u(B)|^2<=2[|u(A)-u(Z)|^2+|u(Z)-u(B)|^2]`.
    If `eta_(1,p)` and `eta_(1,a)` denote its prime and arch pair marginals,
    this gives `E_(eta_(1,p))<=2E_(eta_(1,a))`.  Since
    `6(eta_0+eta_(1,a))<=a`, it follows with the exact orientation factors
    that
    `E_(eta_0)+E_(eta_1)
       <=3E_(eta_0+eta_(1,a))<=E_a/2`.
    The item-51 shrinking positive-tail packets make this `o(Var_mu)`.

    Every all-prime triangle has sorted vertices
    `(x,x+a log p,x+(a+b)log p)`, `a,b>=1`, for one base prime p.  Disintegrate
    its sorted measure as `w_(p,a,b)(x)dx` and let `m_L,m_M,m_R` be the three
    vertex-role pushforwards.  Charge L to the oriented edge L->M, M to M->R,
    and R to R->L.  These are distinct oriented edges, so domination gives
    without shared-capacity double counting
    `3nu_3=m_L+m_M+m_R<=2d^+ +d_(>=2)^-`, where
    `d^+(R)=K(R)sum_(q=p^k)c_qK(R+log q)` and
    `d_(>=2)^-(R)=K(R)sum_(p,k>=2)(log p)p^(-k/2)
       K(R-klog p)`.
    The first ratio `d^+/mu` tends to zero by the theta double-exponential
    tail.  For
    `W_j=sum_(j<=klogp<j+1,k>=2)(log p)p^(-k/2)`, overcounting primes by
    integers gives
    `W_j<=(exp(1/2)/log2)(j+1)^2`.  Splitting at `j<=2R+3` and using
    theta-Schwartz decay afterward proves the remaining convolution is
    `O((1+R)^3)` uniformly on `|x-R|<=1`; division by
    `C(R) asymp exp(R/2)` yields `d_(>=2)^-/mu ->0`.  Hence
    `nu_3=o(mu)` uniformly on the item-51 cells.

    Those cells have width below log2, so an all-prime triangle meets a cell
    in at most one vertex.  Direct six-orientation counting therefore gives
    `E_(eta_3)(u_R)=int|u_R|^2dnu_3=o(D_R)`, where
    `D_R=int|u_R|^2dmu`.  Together with the preceding arch estimate and
    `Var_nu(u_R)=4T D_R(1+o(1))`, this proves
    `E_eta(u_R)/Var_nu(u_R)->0` for every admissible tau.  Thus every such
    triangle skeleton has spectral gap exactly zero.  Uniform positive link
    gaps and Garland's identity cannot prove any positive physical gap, let
    alone 1/2; arbitrary mixtures of all three allowed support types are
    included.

103. **Every diagonal continuum covariance of centered translated radicals
    fails the item-81 PSD completion.**  For
    `phi_a(x)=K(x-a)/K(x)-1`, let
    `R_w(x,y)=int phi_a(x)phi_a(y)w(da)` with an arbitrary positive Borel
    measure w, assuming only `phi_.(x) in L2(w)` for every x.  This already
    includes unbounded support, infinite total mass, and singular mass near
    `a=0`.  Put `h(a)=1-K(a)/K(0)`.  Then `phi_a(0)=-h(a)`, `h>=0`, and h
    vanishes only at zero.  Strict decrease of K in `|x|` gives, for x>0,
    `phi_a(x)>0` exactly when `0<a<2x`; it is nonpositive outside.

    The positive part of `R_w(x,0)=-int phi_a(x)h(a)w(da)` can therefore
    come only from `a<=0` or `a>=2x`, and is at most the w-integral of h on
    those sets.  This integral is finite.  Near zero, `h(a)=O(a^2)`, while
    `phi_a(x_0)=aU(x_0)+O(a^2)` at any fixed `x_0>0`; the assumed L2 condition
    implies `int_(|a|<delta)a^2w(da)<infinity`.  Away from zero h is bounded
    below, so `R_w(0,0)=int h^2dw<infinity` makes w finite there.  In
    particular the `a>=2x` positive tail tends to zero.

    If w gives positive mass to `(0,infinity)`, choose
    `0<alpha<beta` with `w[alpha,beta]>0`.  Uniform theta-tail asymptotics on
    that compact a-interval give
    `inf_(a in [alpha,beta])K(x-a)/K(x)
       >= exp(c_alpha exp(2x))` for all large x.  Since
    `h>=h(alpha)>0`, this interval forces `R_w(x,0)->-infinity`.  That
    contradicts the required item-81 inequality
    `R_w(x,0)>=q(x)=2cosh(x/2)-J(x)>0` for `x>=log(plastic constant)`.
    Hence w must be supported on `(-infinity,0]`; but then
    `0<=R_w(x,0)<=int_(a<=0)h(a)dw` is uniformly bounded, whereas
    `q(x) asymp exp(x/2)` diverges.  This is the second contradiction.

    Thus no independent/diagonal L2 mixture of continuum translates can be
    the required PSD feature map.  The proof does not cover a positive
    non-diagonal covariance operator coupling different translate parameters;
    that operator-valued tail-saddle problem is the exact remaining member of
    this feature subfamily.

104. **A strict Farkas functional excludes every admissible increasing path
    metric.**  Retain item 100's notation, put `T=-L`, `m=CK`, `c=1/4`,
    `s=tanh(x/2)`, `S=sinh(x/2)`, and `U=-(log K)'`.  The exact radical
    identity is `(T-1/2)U=cs`.  If `h'=w>0`, differentiation of the finite
    Levy truncation gives
    `(Lf)'=-lambda_x f'(x)+sum_q[r_q^+f'(x+ell_q)
       +r_q^-f'(x-ell_q)]+int M_x(z)f'(z)dz`,
    where the moving target atoms are positive and
    `M_x=A_L-sum_(ell_q>x-z)(r_q^-)'` for `z<x`,
    `M_x=A_R+sum_(ell_q>z-x)(r_q^+)'` for `z>x`, with `A_L,A_R` exactly as
    in item 100.  Optimizing over every local contact
    `f'(x)=w(x)`, `|f'|<=w`, therefore gives the necessary and sufficient
    local inequality
    `B_x[w]=((T-1/2)h)'(x)-2int M_x^-(z)w(z)dz>=0`.

    Define the positive even Farkas weight and residual tail by
    `phi(x)=-int_(-infinity)^x m(t)U(t)dt` and
    `R(z)=int_z^infinity S(t)K(t)dt`, extended evenly, and put
    `N(z)=int phi(x)M_x^-(z)dx`.  Since `phi'=-mU`, detailed balance,
    self-adjointness, integration by parts, and `(T-1/2)U=cs` give exactly
    `int phi(x)B_x[w]dx
       =int w(z)[cR(z)-2N(z)]dz`.

    It remains to compare the two coefficients, and the comparison is
    strict.  Write `ell=log 2`.  On `0<=z<=ell/2`, take
    `x=z-ell+delta`, `1/10<=delta<=11/40`.  The q=2 forward derivative
    contributes
    `D_2=(ell/(sqrt(2)C(x)))K(z+delta)
       [U(z+delta)+s(x)/2]` to `M_x^-`, while the only adverse continuous
    term is `A_R`.  Here `phi(x)>=C(x)K(x)`.  The deterministic 160-bit Arb
    certificate `scripts/path_metric_farkas_certificate.py` verifies
    `D_2>A_R` on every `(z,delta)` box and, on every z box,
    `2int CK(D_2-A_R)ddelta>(101/100)cR(z)`.  An independent patched rerun
    certified lower endpoints
    `0.0013209806291691262`, `0.0015152773998934278`,
    `0.0046417843422330430`, and `0.0079719258836335307` on the four
    rational subintervals covering `[0,ell/2]`; no reporting minimum can
    skip a failing box because positivity is asserted inside the cell loop.

    For `z>=ell/2`, choose k nearest `z/ell`, put `L=kell`, and integrate
    `x=z-L+delta` over
    `1/(4U(z))<=delta<=1/(2U(z))`.  The q=`2^k` derivative is negative and
    all other included forward derivatives have the same sign.  The explicit
    bounds `U>=37/5`, `U'/U<=15/4`,
    `K(z+delta)>=14K(z)/25`, `phi/C>=7/100`, and `D>=10A_R` yield
    `2N(z)>=A exp(-L/2)K(z)`, where
    `A=(9/5)(7/100)(731/740)(7/50)ell
       =0.0120784080942859...`.
    Monotonicity of U also gives
    `cR(z)<=K(z)/8[e^(z/2)/(U-1/2)-e^(-z/2)/(U+1/2)]`.
    The same certificate proves the remaining endpoint inequality with a
    one-percent reserve and that the bracketed comparison decreases on
    `[ell/2,ell]`; the theta expansion gives `U'>2U` and hence the same
    monotonicity for `z>=ell`.  Reflection covers negative z.  Consequently
    `2N(z)>(101/100)cR(z)` everywhere, contradicting the integrated
    nonnegativity of every positive w.

    The limiting steps do not assume curvature for a truncated surrogate.
    First use finite prime and Levy cutoffs only to justify differentiation,
    Fubini, and detailed balance.  Compactly cut off phi, then remove the
    spatial cutoff using theta-Schwartz decay and `h in L2(mu)`, which is
    supplied by the finite metric second moment needed in item 94.  Absolute
    theta decay removes the prime cutoff, and reversible-form convergence
    removes the Levy cutoff.  The lower-bound rectangles stay a fixed
    positive distance from the diagonal.  Near the diagonal
    `A_(L/R)~K(x)/(2C(x)|z-x|)>0`, so `M_x^-` vanishes and the J singularity
    never enters N; Tonelli handles the remaining nonnegative integrals.
    Thus no continuous positive path density with the item-94 domain and
    second-moment properties can give sharp rate-1/2 W1 contraction.

105. **Every exact-marginal cross-y arch coupling fails with the
    non-lumpable divisor prime lift.**  Let J pull a physical function back to
    the theta carrier and let `V=ker J^*` be the vertical label sector.  For
    any symmetric archimedean disintegration whose couplings have the true
    conditional label marginals, item 77 gives
    `Etilde_A(Ju,G)=E_A(u,J^*G)=0` for `G in V`; its represented arch block
    reduces the pullback sector regardless of how the labels are coupled
    across y.

    Take the genuine centered threshold function
    `g=(-K''+K/4)/K`, for which
    `E_K(g)-(1/2)Var_mu(g)=0`.  On the positive chart, write the carrier
    density at `(a,m)` as `C(a)g_m(a)da`.  In the positive non-lumpable
    divisor lift, division by this density makes every incoming same-side
    term and every reflected term label-independent: the identities are
    `g_(qm)(a-log q)=q^(-1/2)g_m(a)` and a reflected conductance
    `Lambda(q)q^(-1/2)K(log q-a)g_m(a)`.  The sole label-dependent generator
    residual on the pullback Jg is therefore
    `R_m(a)=K(a)/C(a) sum_(q|m)Lambda(q)
       [g(a)-g(a+log q)]`.

    In particular
    `R_2-R_1=(log 2)K(a)/C(a)[g(a)-g(a+log 2)]` is not identically zero.
    The n=1 theta term gives rigorously
    `g(a)=-4pi^2 exp(4a)+22pi exp(2a)+O(1)`, so the bracket is nonzero on a
    sufficiently far compact positive-tail interval.  Choose
    `chi>=0` smooth and compactly supported there and set
    `h=chi[g(a)-g(a+log 2)]`,
    `G(a,2,+)=h`, `G(a,1,+)=-(g_2/g_1)h`, with every other label and the
    negative sign zero.  Then `J^*G=0` pointwise and exact orientation
    counting gives
    `Etilde_P(Jg,G)=(log 2)int chi(a)K(a)g_2(a)
       [g(a)-g(a+log 2)]^2da>0`.

    This G is in the hybrid arch form domain.  Near the diagonal, a true
    star/quantile coupling has mismatch mass `O(d)` while the smooth value
    difference is `O(d)`, cancelling `J(d)=O(1/d)`; away from the diagonal J
    is bounded, and the reflected `1/(a+b)` singularity is locally
    two-dimensionally integrable.  Variance and arch cross terms with Jg
    vanish.  Hence for
    `Qtilde=Etilde-(1/2)Var`,
    `Qtilde(Jg+tG)=2t Etilde_P(Jg,G)+t^2Qtilde(G)<0` for one sign and all
    sufficiently small nonzero t.  Thus no exact-marginal arch label coupling
    admitting the natural compact vertical core can yield the proposed full
    upstairs half-gap.  A singular coupling excluding that core collapses to
    pullbacks as in item 76 and supplies no repair.

106. **The first rotating non-diagonal translate covariance, and every finite
    shifted-Cauchy mixture, fails in the tail-to-center regime.**  Put
    `D=log p` for the plastic constant, `omega=2pi/D`, and
    `kappa(t)=3/(9+t^2)+(1341/1000)cos(omega t)/(4+t^2)`.
    It is positive definite.  The exact rational comparisons
    `(4+D^2/4)/(9+D^2/4)<447/1000
       <(4+D^2)/(9+D^2)` prove `kappa(D/2)<0` and
    `kappa(t)>0` for every `|t|>=D`.  Thus it has precisely the continuing
    tail rotation demanded by item 81, rather than the finite-feature
    collinearity already excluded there.

    Its spectral density is
    `rho(xi)=(1/2)e^(-3|xi|)+(1341/8000)
       [e^(-2|xi-omega|)+e^(-2|xi+omega|)]`.
    With `M_+/- (xi)=Xi(xi+/-i)/2`, define the half-line H-valued translate
    density
    `g(a,xi)=e^(-a)e^(ixi a)sqrt(rho)/M_+` for `a>=0` and
    `g(a,xi)=e^a e^(ixi a)sqrt(rho)/M_-` for `a<0`.
    The shifted Xi factors have no real zeros because they correspond to
    `Re(s)=-1/2` or `3/2`, and their standard vertical growth makes
    `g in L1(da;L2(dxi))`.  For
    `Phi(x)=int g(a)[K(x-a)/K(x)-1]da`, one has
    `int KPhi=0`, so `R(x,y)=<Phi(x),Phi(y)>` is a genuine infinite-rank PSD
    non-diagonal translate covariance satisfying `RK=0`.

    Write `Psi=KPhi`.  Direct half-line convolution gives exactly
    `Psi(x,xi)=sqrt(rho(xi))H_x(xi)`, where
    `H_x=e^((-1+ixi)x)M_+^(-1)int_(-infinity)^x
          K(t)e^((1-ixi)t)dt
       +e^((1+ixi)x)M_-^(-1)int_x^infinity
          K(t)e^((-1-ixi)t)dt
       -K(x)[((1-ixi)M_+)^(-1)+((1+ixi)M_-)^(-1)]`.
    At the center, the independently rerun 160-bit Arb certificate
    `scripts/translated_covariance_falsifier.py`, including an explicit
    `<1.925e-85` complex enclosure for the omitted half-Laplace theta tail,
    proves `H_0(0)<-2.684` and `H_0(omega)<-53.99`.

    Consequently `f=rho H_0` has positive derivative jumps
    `J_0=-3H_0(0)>8.05` and
    `J_omega=-4(1341/8000)H_0(omega)>36.20`.  Two distributional
    integrations by parts, justified by the exponential tails, give
    `int f(xi)e^(ixi x)dxi
       =-[J_0+2J_omega cos(omega x)]/x^2+o(x^(-2))`.
    Along `x=2pi n/omega` this is negative for every sufficiently large n.
    Since `R(x,0)` has this numerator times the positive factor
    `e^(-x)/[K(x)K(0)]`, it is negative on that unbounded sequence, while
    the required `q(x)` is positive.  Scaling cannot repair the violation.

    The same argument covers any nonzero finite positive sum of components
    `Ae^(-a|xi|)` and
    `B[e^(-b|xi-lambda|)+e^(-b|xi+lambda|)]`, with
    `a,b>pi/2`.  At each distinct cusp lambda, analytic `H_0` has some finite
    zero order; positivity of all weights prevents cancellation of the first
    nonsmooth coefficient.  The slowest Fourier term is a nonzero real
    trigonometric polynomial times an inverse power of x.  If it has a
    zero-frequency component its Bohr mean has the certified positive sign
    from `H_0(0)<0`; otherwise its mean is zero and it takes both signs.
    Recurrence supplies an unbounded sequence with the same forbidden
    negative tail-to-center value.  This finite-mixture obstruction does not
    apply to smooth continuous rho; moving the contour for that class is the
    live analytic question.

107. **No positive non-marginal arch allocation can cancel the divisor
    residual on the threshold module.**  Relative to the orthogonal splitting
    `L2(nu)=J L2(mu) direct-sum V`, write the proposed shifted upstairs form
    as the block matrix `[[H,B^*],[B,D]]`, where
    `H=T-(1/2)(I-P_1)` on physical pullbacks and
    `B=(I-JJ^*)Ttilde J`.  Positivity of this block forces `Bu=0` for every
    physical threshold radical `u=bar u_p`, because `Hu=0`.  Item 105 proves
    that true conditional-label arch couplings have `B_A=0` but the divisor
    prime block has `B_Pg!=0` already for
    `g=(-K''+K/4)/K`.

    The only possible repair would use a non-marginal positive arch
    allocation.  Write its normalized label weights as
    `gamma_(x,y)(m,n)>=0`, with total one and
    `gamma_(y,x)(n,m)=gamma_(x,y)(m,n)`, and put
    `r_m(x,y)=sum_n gamma_(x,y)(m,n)`.  It still preserves the physical
    pullback arch form.  Subtracting the m=1 and m=2 vertical generator rows
    shows that cancellation on every threshold radical requires, for almost
    every `x>0`,
    `int K(y)J(|x-y|)eta_x(y)[u_p(x)-u_p(y)]dy
       =-(log 2)[u_p(x)-u_p(x+log 2)]`,
    where
    `eta_x(y)=r_2(x,y)/g_2(x)-r_1(x,y)/g_1(x)` is bounded for fixed x.

    This identity is impossible.  Put `f_p=Ku_p=p(-D^2)K` and define the
    even tempered distribution obtained from
    `D_x(f)=int J(|x-y|)eta_x(y)
       [K(y)f(x)/K(x)-f(y)]dy
       +(log 2)[f(x)/K(x)-f(x+log 2)/K(x+log 2)]`.
    The bracket cancels the `J(d)~1/(2|d|)` singularity, so the Fourier
    transform of the arch part is continuous with at most logarithmic
    growth.  The asserted cancellation says `D_x(f_p)=0` for every
    polynomial p.  Since `fhat_p(t)=p(t^2)Xi(t)`, the even measure
    `Xi(t)Dhat_x(t)dt` has an exponential moment and every moment zero.
    Analytic Fourier-Laplace uniqueness therefore gives
    `Xi Dhat_x=0`, hence the even distribution `D_x` itself is zero.

    But its arch part is a regular density in neighborhoods of
    `y=+/- (x+log 2)`, whereas the last displayed prime term contributes
    nonzero atoms there, of coefficient
    `-(log 2)/(2K(x+log 2))` after even symmetrization.  A regular density
    cannot cancel those atoms.  This contradiction excludes every positive
    non-marginal arch allocation admitting the natural compact two-label
    vertical core.  If that core is assigned infinite energy, the domain
    collapses toward pullbacks as in item 76.  If positivity/carrier structure
    is discarded, the abstract Schur condition has principal block `H>=0`,
    exactly the original RH-equivalent Markov gap, and supplies no independent
    mechanism.

108. **Every constant-cost prime-shortcut metric fails sharp W1 curvature.**
    Let `d_a` be the intrinsic metric generated by the U-path line and every
    undirected prime-power edge `z<->z+log q` at the same cost `a>0`.  Two
    bounded contacts cover all a.  Every comparison below is certified at
    160-bit Arb precision by the independently rerun deterministic script
    `scripts/constant_shortcut_metric_certificate.py`.

    If `a<=8`, use the q=2 edge from `-ell/2` to `ell/2`, `ell=log 2`, and
    `f(x)=a S((x+ell/2)/ell)` on that interval, extended by zero and a,
    where `S(v)=3v^2-2v^3`.  The certificate proves `U'>18` throughout the
    interval and `2U(ell/2)>8`, while `|f'|<=12/ell<18`.  Thus f is
    U-path-Lipschitz, its whole range has diameter a so it respects every
    shortcut, and its endpoint difference a proves that the q=2 shortcut is
    a metric geodesic.  Reflection gives the exact two-endpoint defect
    `a[1/2-2L(f/a)(-ell/2)]`.  Explicit theta, archimedean-singularity, and
    all-prime tail enclosures give the bracket the strict lower bound
    `0.0188743254919100`.

    For large a define
    `R=U(7ell/8)-U(5ell/8)=6.36148844130359...<7` and use the bounded folded
    U-contact which is zero on `(-infinity,-ell/3]`, rises with U-slope below
    one to `U(ell/8)`, falls with slope -1 to zero, stays zero through
    `5ell/8`, rises with slope one to R at `7ell/8`, and stays R thereafter.
    Its range is `[0,R]`.  On the pair
    `(x,y)=(3ell/4,13ell/16)` it agrees with U plus a constant and has
    difference `U(y)-U(x)=1.64834723302228...<R`.  Hence for every `a>=R`
    it respects every shortcut, is globally 1-Lipschitz for `d_a`, and its
    endpoint difference proves `d_a(x,y)=d_U(x,y)`.  Direct q=2,3
    evaluation, a rigorous q>=4 tail, geometric cells at both removable J
    singularities, and theta tail bounds give
    `Lf(y)-Lf(x)+d_a(x,y)/2>0.0982055225680931`.

    Since `R<7<8`, the two parameter ranges overlap and cover every `a>0`.
    This is an all-parameter analytic no-go, not a sampled metric search.  It
    does not cover state- or channel-dependent shortcut costs.

109. **Every dominated finite-dimensional simplex/Hodge skeleton has global
    gap zero.**  Let `tau` be a finite `S_r`-symmetric measure on `R^r`,
    `r>=3`, of mass T, with vertex marginal `nu=4T mu` and pair marginal
    eta.  Assume its full ordered edge projection is dominated by the
    physical conductance, `r(r-1)eta<=e_A+e_P`, and put
    `E_eta(u)=(1/2)int|u(x)-u(y)|^2deta`.

    The exact link implication is dimension-independent.  If P is the
    eta-Markov operator and `m_x` and `ell_x` are the conditional vertex and
    pair marginals of `(X_2,...,X_r)` given `X_1=x`, then
    `int E_(ell_x)(u)dnu=E_eta(u)` and
    `int Var_(m_x)(u)dnu=||u||_nu^2-||Pu||_nu^2`.
    A uniform link gap lambda therefore gives
    `1-t>=lambda(1-t^2)` on the noninvariant spectrum and, when constants are
    the only invariants, the skeleton gap
    `delta>=2-1/lambda`.  Recursion through successive links gives the sharp
    sequence `2,3/2,4/3,...,r/(r-1)`; independently,
    `0<=E|sum_i f(X_i)|^2` gives `P>=-1/(r-1)` and hence
    `delta<=r/(r-1)`.  Domination would turn a skeleton gap delta into the
    physical bound
    `E_K>=4T r(r-1)delta Var_mu`; even at the maximal delta, a half-gap needs
    `T>=1/(8r^2)`.

    Prime support has an exact partition structure.  If two prime edges of a
    simplex share a vertex, fixing their two prime powers puts the third
    pair on a one-dimensional displacement line.  Such a line is singular
    to the diffuse arch measure, so domination forces the third edge to be
    prime.  Unique factorization then forces all three powers to have the
    same base prime.  Consequently the prime-edge graph is a disjoint union
    of same-base prime cliques, with arch edges between distinct cliques.
    Any fixed non-prime singular relation between cliques is dominated by
    neither physical component and has zero mass.

    Every proper prime clique has an arch bypass.  Given an internal prime
    edge ij and any vertex z outside its clique,
    `|u_i-u_j|^2<=2(|u_i-u_z|^2+|u_z-u_j|^2)` and both replacement edges are
    archimedean.  Summing over the finitely many edges gives, pointwise on
    every partition with at least two blocks, total simplex pair energy at
    most `C_r` times its arch-edge energy (for example
    `C_r=1+r(r-1)` suffices).  Ordered-edge domination then makes the whole
    proper-partition contribution `O_r(E_A(u_R))=o(D_R)` on the item-51
    shrinking tail packets, where `D_R=int|u_R|^2dmu`.

    For a one-clique all-prime configuration, sort its vertices as
    `x_1<...<x_r`.  They have the form
    `x_j=x+n_j log p` with strictly increasing integers `n_j`.  Select the
    directed cycle
    `x_1->x_2->...->x_r->x_1`.  The selected edge measure is a submeasure of
    the full ordered prime projection.  Its first `r-1` source marginals are
    bounded together by the right-going prime degree `d^+`; the wrap edge is
    a left-going power of exponent at least `r-1>=2`.  Exact role
    symmetrization therefore gives
    `r nu_all<=d^+ + d^-_(>=r-1)<=d^+ + d^-_(>=2)`.
    Item 102's theta estimates prove both degree ratios to mu tend to zero
    uniformly on the tail cells.  Their width is below `log 2`, so at most
    one clique vertex meets a cell; exact `r(r-1)`-orientation counting then
    gives
    `E_(eta_all)(u_R)=int|u_R|^2dnu_all=o(D_R)`.

    Combining all support partitions yields
    `E_eta(u_R)/Var_nu(u_R)->0`, so every fixed-r dominated simplex skeleton
    has gap exactly zero.  Higher links can only estimate this same
    zero-skeleton: pointwise
    `sum_(i<j)|u_i-u_j|^2=r sum_i|u_i-ubar|^2`.
    Thus tetrahedral `S_4` allocations and every fixed finite-dimensional
    simplex/Hodge extension fail before any Garland recursion; arbitrary
    mixtures of disconnected prime cliques are included.

110. **Smooth pole-cancelled translate spectra obey an exact imaginary-zero
    gate; the forced one-harmonic candidate fails.**  Let
    `M_+(z)=Xi(z+i)/2`, `M_-(z)=Xi(z-i)/2`, `m=M_+M_-`, and take the smooth
    nonnegative real spectral density
    `rho(z)=m(z)^2 sigma(z)^2`.  The shifted-Xi denominators in item 106's
    translate density then cancel.  For the tail-to-center numerator
    `L(x)=int rho(t)H_0(t)e^(itx)dt`, the center formula has a simple pole at
    `z=i`.  Its residue is
    `iK(0)M_+(i)^2M_-(i)sigma(i)^2`.
    Here `M_+(i)=xi(5/2)/2>0` and `M_-(i)=xi(1/2)/2>0`.  Shifting the contour
    upward for `x>0` therefore produces a strictly negative multiple of
    `e^(-x)` unless `sigma(i)=0`.  Since the required majorant has
    `R(x,0)>0` for all large x, the imaginary zero is necessary and is not an
    optional numerical tuning.

    For the first analytic trigonometric family
    `sigma(z)=1-r cos(Az)`, the gate forces exactly `r=sech(A)`.  Set
    `D=log p` for the plastic constant and test the natural placement
    `A=D/2`.  The independently rerun 120-bit Arb certificate
    `scripts/smooth_xi_multiplier_falsifier.py` proves, for
    `kappa(t)=int_R rho(xi)cos(txi)dxi`,
    `kappa(t)>1.5363*10^(-5)` uniformly on `|t|<=D`, but
    `kappa(3D/2)<-5.5927*10^(-5)`.  This is the reverse of the required
    saddle: local cancellation needs an obtuse direction inside D, while
    `q(t)>=0` requires nonnegative covariance outside D.

    The finite integrals are interval quadratures on `[0,50]`.  Their tails
    are not inferred from apparent decay: eight integrations by parts in
    `M_+(xi)=Fourier(e^tK(t))` and explicit incomplete-gamma theta bounds
    give `||(e^tK)^(8)||_1<1.4*10^10`, hence omitted spectral mass
    `<2.11*10^(-13)` and omitted second moment `<5.64*10^(-10)`.  These
    enclosures are included in the displayed strict signs.  More generally,
    if an analytic multiplier satisfies the necessary zero, then
    `rho(i)=0`, equivalently
    `int kappa(t)e^t dt=2int_0^infinity kappa(t)cosh(t)dt=0`; every nonzero
    candidate must change sign.  This identity alone does not locate the
    negative lobe, so higher-degree smooth multipliers with `sigma(i)=0`
    remain unclosed.

111. **Projective infinite cells and every positive unbounded-incidence
    simplex mixture also have zero skeleton gap.**  For a projectively
    consistent exchangeable cell law with finite vertex mass, normalize and
    apply de Finetti: `nu=E Pi` and `eta=E(Pi tensor Pi)` for a random
    directing probability Pi.  Physical edge domination gives
    `0=eta(Delta)=E sum_x Pi({x})^2`, so Pi is nonatomic almost surely.  For
    every prime power q it follows that
    `(Pi tensor Pi){y=x+log q}=int Pi({x+log q})dPi(x)=0`.
    Thus atomic directing measures are excluded by their unavoidable
    diagonal self-pairs, while nonatomic directing measures carry no prime
    line at all.  The dominated pair law is purely archimedean and the
    item-51 tail packets give gap zero.  Also
    `<f,Pf>_nu=E|int f dPi|^2>=0`.  If the unscaled full-incidence condition
    `r(r-1)eta<=e` is required for every r in one consistent family, letting
    r grow on each separated finite-conductance rectangle makes eta vanish
    off the diagonal; diagonal domination then makes the family trivial.

    The nonprojective case has a dimension-uniform obstruction.  Let
    `tau_r`, `r>=3`, be arbitrary positive `S_r` measures with vertex and
    pair marginals `nu_r,eta_r`, and assume only
    `sum_r nu_r=kappa mu`, `0<kappa<infinity`, and the global ordered-edge
    domination
    `sum_r r(r-1)eta_r<=e_A+e_P`.
    All positive dimension weights are absorbed into the tau_r, so this is
    the general reversible incidence normalization; Tonelli applies without
    a dimension-moment hypothesis.

    For every same-base prime clique of size at least three, select its
    sorted directed cycle.  Across all dimensions the selected measure is a
    submeasure of the dominated ordered prime projection, covers every such
    clique vertex once, and its wrap edges have exponent at least two.  If
    `nu_(r,>=3)` is the first-vertex marginal restricted to those cliques,
    exact role symmetrization gives
    `sum_r r nu_(r,>=3)<=d^+ + d^-_(>=2)`.
    Hence `3sum_r nu_(r,>=3)` is bounded by a measure whose ratio to mu tends
    uniformly to zero on the tail cells.  Their width is below `log 2`, so
    each clique has at most one active vertex and
    `sum_r E_(eta_(r,>=3))(u_R)
       <=sum_r int|u_R|^2dnu_(r,>=3)=o(D_R)`.

    A two-vertex prime clique `B={i,j}` has the averaged bypass
    `|u_i-u_j|^2<=[2/(r-2)]sum_(z notin B)
       (|u_i-u_z|^2+|u_z-u_j|^2)`.
    Every replacement edge is inter-clique archimedean.  After summing all
    two-cliques, an arch edge is used with coefficient at most
    `4/(r-2)<=4`, uniformly in r.  Since `r(r-1)>=6`, global domination gives
    `sum_r E_(eta_(r,A))<=E_A/6`; consequently the two-clique contribution
    is also `o(D_R)`.  Singleton and purely arch blocks are already included.
    Therefore, with `eta=sum_r eta_r` and `nu=sum_r nu_r`,
    `E_eta(u_R)/Var_nu(u_R)->0`.

    The Hodge frontier is thus closed for both projectively consistent
    infinite cells and arbitrary nonprojective sigma-finite/unbounded
    positive incidence mixtures having a finite physical vertex marginal
    and global ordered-edge domination.  Escaping this theorem requires
    abandoning a common reversible vertex marginal, positivity, or physical
    edge domination, so it is no longer a simplex/Hodge factorization of the
    Markov form.

112. **The full rate-calibrated state/channel shortcut family fails for every
    parameter pair.**  For a prime power q put `ell_q=log q`,
    `c_q=Lambda(q)/sqrt(q)`,
    `R_q(x)=c_q[K(x+ell_q)/C(x)+K(x)/C(x+ell_q)]`, and assign the undirected
    edge `x<->x+ell_q` the cost
    `a_q(x)=min{U(x+ell_q)-U(x),a_0/(1+alpha R_q(x))}`,
    with `a_0>0`, `alpha>=0`.  The independently rerun 160-bit Arb certificate
    `scripts/rate_calibrated_shortcut_certificate.py` excludes the entire
    two-parameter family without sampling its parameters.

    First, compact interval boxes plus the theta variance formula prove
    `U'(x)>18` globally; hence every prime-edge U-increment exceeds
    `18log 2>7`.  Second, the global maximum of `R_q(x)` over every q and x
    is attained in the q=3 channel, with a representative
    `x_* in [-1/50,0]`.  Reflection reduces each channel to
    `[-ell_q/2,infinity)`, direct boxes handle q below 11, and for q>=11 the
    coefficient bound `c_q<3/4` and endpoint dichotomy give the decreasing
    upper bounds
    `(3/4)2K(log(11)/4)<0.01123` and
    `(3/4)[K(0)/C(3log(11)/4)+K(3log(11)/4)]<0.23391`,
    both below the certified q=3 witness `0.2457790032`.  Exhaustive q=3
    boxes place every maximizer in the stated interval.

    Define a fixed contact F which is zero below `log 2/3`, linear in U up to
    `17log 2/24`, and one thereafter.  Its U-width is
    `7.2246972764...`, larger than
    `R_*=U(7log 2/8)-U(5log 2/8)=6.3614884413...`.  Uniformly for every
    `x in [-1/50,0]`, exact q=2,3,4,5 terms, a q>=7 theta tail, and bounded
    archimedean integrals prove
    `LF(x+log 3)-LF(x)+1/2>0.0053577405759`.

    Let b be the infimum of all shortcut costs.  If `b>=R_*`, item 108's
    bounded folded contact has range `R_*`, so it respects every varying edge
    cost and gives the same large-floor countercontact.  If `b<R_*`, the
    globally larger-than-seven U-increments cannot determine b.  Hence
    `b=a_0/(1+alpha max R_q)`, attained at the certified q=3 edge
    `(x_*,x_*+log 3)`.  The function `bF` has total range b, respects every
    shortcut, is U-path-Lipschitz, and saturates that edge; the preceding
    strict generator defect therefore violates rate-1/2 contraction.  These
    two cases cover every `a_0>0`, `alpha>=0`.  Arbitrary positive-floor costs
    not expressible by this rate calibration are not covered.

113. **Euler-local signed squares collapse exactly to prime edge squares, and
    every unrestricted escape is the full Weil/Douglas factorization.**  Let
    an Euler-local row be
    `(Au)(x)=sum_n v_n(x)u(x+log n)`.  The singular coefficient on the
    rational shift line `r=n/m` is exactly
    `C_r(t)=sum_(n/m=r)<v_m(t-log m),v_n(t-log m)>`.  Matching the Markov form
    requires `C_q(t)=-c_qK(t)K(t+log q)` for prime powers `q`, `C_r(t)=0` for
    every other rational `r`, and constant annihilation gives
    `sum_n v_n(x)=0`.

    Restrict to the divisor cell `{1,p,q,pq}` for distinct prime bases.  Its
    only allowed off-diagonal shift lines are the four sides.  Writing their
    weights as `a,b,c,d`, symmetry and the zero row sums uniquely force the
    diagonal to be `(a+b,a+c,b+d,c+d)`: the Gram is exactly the weighted
    four-cycle Laplacian.  The signed plaquette row `(1,-1,-1,1)` has `+1` on
    both forbidden diagonals `1<->pq` and `p<->q`; any correction that cancels
    those lines returns the total Gram to the same four prime edges.  At the
    specialization `{1,2,3,6}`, `Lambda(6)=0` but
    `(Lambda*Lambda)(6)=2Lambda(2)Lambda(3)>0`.  The corresponding pure
    Selberg polarization has matrix `((0,1),(1,0))` and determinant `-1`, so
    it is not a Hilbert square; adding its positive self-terms is precisely
    the already-forced edge completion.  The deterministic integer/polynomial
    audit is `scripts/multiplicative_square_cycle_falsifier.py`.

    There is also an exact domain obstruction.  Extend the shifted form by
    `h[u]=E_K[u]-(1/2)(||u||_mu^2-|<u,1>|^2/c)`, so `h[1]=0`.  Every threshold
    radical `u_p=p(-D^2)K/K`, modulo its centering constant, lies in the
    radical of any proposed positive factorization `h=||Au||^2`.  Item 45/52
    local density therefore makes every finite-propagation, locally
    `L2`-closable Euler row vanish.  The same conclusion holds for finite
    tempered archimedean-plus-Euler rows: after multiplication by `1/K`,
    analytic moment uniqueness on the nonzero entire function `Xi` kills the
    row distribution.  Thus any survivor must be global and non-locally
    closable at the reciprocal-theta tail scale.

    Without locality restrictions, the required off-line Gram kernel is
    exactly
    `K(x)K(y)[2C(x)C(y)-J(|x-y|)]`, together with the prescribed prime delta
    lines and closed-form diagonal.  This is the full shifted Markov/Weil
    Gram.  Its Kato/Douglas square root exists exactly when `h>=0`, namely the
    sharp gap in item 57.  Hence the unrestricted construction is not new
    structure but the original RH-equivalent positivity problem.

114. **Every stationary analytic translated-radical spectral law fails by a
    same-tail/center-residue dichotomy.**  Let `rho` be even, nonzero,
    nonnegative on `R`, and in `L1(R)`, and use exactly the half-line
    translated-radical Gram construction of item 106,
    `Psi(x,xi)=K(x)Phi(x,xi)`.  Assume
    `sqrt(rho)/M_+`, `sqrt(rho)/M_-`, and `sqrt(rho)H_0` are in `L2`; assume
    also that `rho` is holomorphic in `|Im z|<1+delta`, has the horizontal
    `L1` contour bounds used below, and that `F=rho H_0` is meromorphic in
    `0<=Im z<=1+delta`, with its sole pole there at `z=i` and an `L1` top
    boundary.  These hypotheses hold for `rho=m^2s` whenever `s` is even,
    holomorphic and pole-free in that strip and has the required decay: the
    exact identity
    `rho H_0=s[M_+M_-^2N_+ + M_+^2M_-N_-]` cancels every shifted-Xi zero
    algebraically, so no zero-free hypothesis is being used.

    The exact half-line formula gives
    `Psi(x,xi)=e^(-x+i xi x)sqrt(rho(xi))+E_x`, where `||E_x||_2` is at most
    `e^-x int_x^inf K(u)e^u du ||sqrt(rho)/M_+||_2`, plus
    `e^x int_x^inf K(u)e^-u du ||sqrt(rho)/M_-||_2`, plus
    `K(x) sum_+- ||sqrt(rho)/((1 mp i xi)M_+-)||_2`.
    Theta double-exponential decay makes this remainder `o(e^(-Nx))` for
    every fixed `N` after removal of the displayed leading term, uniformly
    when `x` is replaced by `x+t` for `t` in a fixed compact set.  Hence, for
    every fixed `t`,
    `<Psi(x),Psi(x+t)>=e^(-2x-t)kappa(t)+o(e^(-Nx))`, where
    `kappa(t)=int_R rho(xi)e^(i xi t)dxi`.

    If the normalized covariance majorized the required jump threshold
    `q(|t|)`, then a fixed `t!=0` with `kappa(t)<0` would, after division by
    `K(x)K(x+t)=o(e^(-Nx))`, tend to minus infinity.  This contradicts the
    finite local threshold when `|t|<D` and the nonnegative threshold when
    `|t|>=D`.  Thus `kappa(t)>=0` for all `t`; continuity and
    `kappa(0)=int rho>0` make it nonzero.  The strip shift gives
    `kappa(t)=O(e^(-(1+delta)|t|))`, so Fourier inversion, rather than a
    divergent exponential Fubini exchange, yields
    `2pi rho(i)=int_R kappa(t)e^t dt`
    `=2int_0^inf kappa(t)cosh(t)dt>0`.

    Finally put `A(s)=int_0^inf K(u)e^(-su)du` and
    `H_0=N_+/M_+ + N_-/M_-`, with
    `N_+-=A(1 mp iz)-K(0)/(1 mp iz)`.  Since
    `Res_(z=i)H_0=iK(0)/M_-(i)` and
    `M_-(i)=Xi(0)/2=int K>0`, shifting the `F` contour above `i` gives
    `int_R F(xi)e^(ixxi)dxi`
    `=-2pi K(0)rho(i)M_-(i)^(-1)e^-x+O(e^(-(1+delta)x))`.
    The same superexponential remainder estimate therefore gives
    `<Psi(x),Psi(0)>=-C e^(-2x)(1+o(1))`, `C>0`.  Dividing by
    `K(x)K(0)` tends to minus infinity, whereas the required threshold is
    positive for `x>=D`, a contradiction.  This closes the stated analytic
    stationary class without RH, a zero-free strip, or finite-to-infinite
    extrapolation.

115. **The sharp fixed-time Doeblin factorization is impossible.**  Normalize
    `pi=mu/c`, let `P_t=e^(-tT)`, fix `t_0>0`, put
    `r=e^(-t_0/2)` and `alpha=1-r`, and suppose the pointwise sharp
    minorization `P_(t_0)>=alpha Pi` held.  Then
    `Q=r^(-1)(P_(t_0)-alpha Pi)` is a reversible Markov operator.  Every
    centered threshold radical `bar u_p` satisfies
    `P_(t_0)bar u_p=r bar u_p`, so `Q bar u_p=bar u_p`.  For the symmetric
    edge measure `eta(dx,dy)=pi(dx)Q(x,dy)`, this says
    `int|u_p(x)-u_p(y)|^2deta=0` for every threshold radical.

    On every compact square `I_R^2`, both marginals of the restricted edge
    measure are dominated by `pi|I_R`.  Item 52's local even-`L2` density
    therefore extends zero energy from the threshold module to every even
    `f in L2(I_R)`.  Taking `f(x)=x^2` and exhausting `R` proves that `Q` is
    supported on `|x|=|y|`; hence it is the identity on the whole even
    sector.  Thus `P_(t_0)=rI` on centered even `L2`.  Injectivity of
    `lambda mapsto e^(-t_0 lambda)` in spectral calculus forces `T=1/2`
    there.  This is false: compact even tests `chi(x)cos(Nx)` have bounded
    nonzero `L2(pi)` norm while their archimedean energy grows at least
    `c log N`, from `J(h)~1/(2h)`.  Therefore no sharp pointwise regeneration
    exists.  A stronger minorization is already excluded by Markov
    contraction; a weaker one cannot imply the sharp gap by this
    decomposition.

116. **Every locally dominated finite-memory fixed-time path square is
    impossible.**  Let `r=e^(-t_0/2)` and
    `S_(t_0)=I-P_(t_0)-(1-r)(I-Pi)=r(I-Pi)-(P_(t_0)-Pi)`.  Suppose on the
    even form core that
    `<f,S_(t_0)f>=int |sum_(j=0)^m a_j(omega)f(X_j(omega))|^2dnu(omega)`,
    or a countable direct sum of such forms with one fixed finite `m`, and
    assume the natural local coordinate domination
    `sum_j (X_j)_*(|a_j|^2nu)|_I <= C_I pi|_I` on every compact interval.

    Every centered threshold radical `bar u_p` is a sharp eigenfunction, so
    `S_(t_0)[bar u_p]=0`.  Positivity of the displayed integral makes each
    analysis row annihilate every such radical almost everywhere.  Restrict
    to histories whose finitely many coordinates all lie in `I_R`.  Item
    52's local even-`L2` density, together with the coordinate domination,
    extends this row annihilation to every even `f in L2(I_R)`.  Exhausting
    the finite-coordinate path space by `R` proves that the analysis operator
    kills every compact even test.  Thus `S_(t_0)` vanishes on a dense even
    core and `P_(t_0)=rI` on centered even `L2`, contradicting the compact
    high-frequency tests of item 115.  Hence a positive path representation,
    if one exists, must use an infinite/global history functional that is not
    locally `L2`-closable in this sense.

    Sharp spectral contraction does not itself imply the pointwise
    regeneration rejected in item 115.  The uniform three-state Laplacian
    `T=[[5/12,-1/12,-1/3],[-1/12,5/12,-1/3],[-1/3,-1/3,2/3]]`
    has eigenvalues `0,1/2,1`.  At `t_0=2log2`,
    `P=[[5/8,1/8,1/4],[1/8,5/8,1/4],[1/4,1/4,1/2]]` has centered
    eigenvalues `1/2,1/4`, but `P_12=1/8<(1-r)/3=1/6`, so the sharp Doeblin
    minorant fails.  Its valid residual square is instead the global signed
    three-point row `(1/4)|<v,f>|^2`,
    `v=(1,1,-2)/sqrt(6)`, illustrating exactly why two-point regeneration is
    stronger than the desired gap.

117. **The minimal exactly normalized nonstationary chirp fails despite the
    correct moving negative-lag scale.**  Let `eta` be a finite even positive
    spectral measure with covariance
    `h(T)=int e^(i xi T)deta(xi)`, let `Theta` be increasing with
    `Theta(0)=0`, and put
    `D_T(xi)=(e^(i xi T)-1)/(i xi)=int_0^T e^(i xi s)ds`.  The primitive
    compensated chirp is
    `V_x=b(x)D_(Theta(x))` and
    `Psi_x=partial_xV_x=b'(x)D_Theta+a(x)e^(i xi Theta)`, where
    `a=b Theta'>0`; the candidate feature is `Phi_x=Psi_x/K(x)`.  If
    `||V_x||->0` at both ends, then
    `int K(x)Phi_x dx=int Psi_x dx=V_(infinity)-V_(-infinity)=0` exactly,
    and `<Phi_x,Phi_y>` is positive semidefinite.  A negative lobe of `h` at
    `u_0` moves to `t_x~u_0/Theta'(x)`, so this is the smallest construction
    that escapes item 114's fixed-negative-lag obstruction.

    Nevertheless the full primitive class fails.  Assume its necessary slow
    positive covariance tail is
    `h(T)~cT^(-alpha)`, with `c>0` and `0<alpha<1`.  Put
    `H(T)=int_0^T h(s)ds` and
    `G(T)=||D_T||^2=2int_0^T(T-u)h(u)du`.  Direct integration (equivalently
    Karamata) gives
    `H(T)~cT^(1-alpha)/(1-alpha)` and
    `G(T)~2cT^(2-alpha)/[(1-alpha)(2-alpha)]`.  Writing
    `B(T)=b(Theta^(-1)(T))`, exact normalization forces
    `B(T)T^(1-alpha/2)->0`.

    At the center, `Psi_0=a(0)`, and exactly
    `<Psi_x,Psi_0>=a(0)[a(x)h(T)+b'(x)H(T)]`, `T=Theta(x)`.  Eventual
    nonnegativity, which the positive center-to-tail majorant requires,
    would imply
    `T B'(T)/B(T)>=-T h(T)/H(T)=-(1-alpha)+o(1)`.  For any
    `epsilon<alpha/2`, integration gives
    `B(T)>=C T^(-(1-alpha+epsilon))`, and hence
    `B(T)T^(1-alpha/2)->infinity`, contradicting normalization.  Thus the
    center overlap is negative on an unbounded sequence and, after division
    by `K(x)K(0)`, violates the required positive majorant.

    The scaling assumptions are nonvacuous.  The explicit positive-definite
    covariance
    `h(u)=(1+u^2)^(-1/16)+(3/16)cos(4pi u)/(u^2+1/16)`
    has `h(1/4)<-1/2`, satisfies
    `h(u)>-1/(2|u|)` for `u!=0`, and obeys
    `h(u)~|u|^(-1/8)>0`.  Positivity follows because the generalized Cauchy
    term is positive definite and the second term is the product of the
    positive-definite cosine and Cauchy covariances.  It realizes the desired
    moving-lobe signs, but the theorem still kills its primitive chirp.

118. **Infinite-history martingale and tail-boundary factorizations are
    exactly circular.**  On `H_0=1^perp`, let `r=e^(-t/2)`,
    `S_t=rI-P_t`, and `H=T-1/2`.  Spectral calculus gives the exact identity
    `S_t=H Phi_t(T)`, where
    `Phi_t(lambda)=(r-e^(-t lambda))/(lambda-1/2)`
    `=int_0^t e^(-(t-s)/2)e^(-s lambda)ds>0`, with the removable value
    `Phi_t(1/2)=tr`.  Hence `S_t>=0` if and only if `H>=0`; no semigroup
    smoothing has weakened item 57.

    The stationary path quadratic variation makes the obstruction explicit.
    Put `tau=t/2`, and first use spectral cutoffs `epsilon<=T<=M`.  With
    `B_abs=(|T-1/2|/T)^(1/2)` and
    `g_s=e^((s-tau)/2)B_abs P_s f`, the compensated-jump/Fukushima
    martingale integral over `0<s<tau` obeys the exact Ito isometry
    `||A_abs f||^2=2int_0^tau <g_s,Tg_s>ds`
    `=int|e^-tau-e^(-2tau lambda)|dmu_f(lambda)=<f,|S_t|f>`.
    Although `B_abs` can be unbounded at zero, cutoff passage extends this
    integral uniquely on `H_0`, because `|S_t|` is bounded; this does not
    assume absolute summability of the infinite-activity jumps.  Replacing
    `B_abs` by `((T-1/2)/T)^(1/2)` gives a real Hilbert-space martingale
    exactly when `H>=0`.  A complex branch still yields only the absolute
    value, and
    `S_t=|S_t|-2E_T((0,1/2))|S_t|`; deleting that negative spectral part is
    precisely RH's Markov gap.

    The infinite-past boundary has the same gate.  For a stationary path,
    `Z_R(f)=e^(R/2)P_Rf(X_(-R))`
    `=e^(R/2)E[f(X_0)|X_(-R)]` has norm squared
    `int e^(-2R(lambda-1/2))dmu_f(lambda)`.  It is uniformly bounded for all
    `f in H_0` and converges strongly to `E_T({1/2})f` exactly when there is
    no spectral mass below `1/2`; any subthreshold vector makes it diverge.
    Thus neither infinite history nor a global stochastic integral supplies
    an independent positivity mechanism.

119. **Two-channel common-phase primitive cancellation also fails under every
    regular asymptotic regime.**  Let `eta` now be an even positive
    `2 by 2` spectral measure whose matrix covariance satisfies
    `H(T)=T^(-alpha)(C+o(1))` in operator norm, with `0<alpha<1` and
    `C>0`, together with the corresponding integrated asymptotics.  Put
    `V_x=eta^(1/2)B(T)D_T`, `T=Theta(x)`, for `B(T) in R^2`.  Then
    `Psi_x=Theta'(x)eta^(1/2)[B_T(T)D_T+B(T)e^(i xi T)]`, and
    `||V_x||^2~2T^(2-alpha)B(T)^TCB(T)/[(1-alpha)(2-alpha)]`.
    Exact normalization therefore requires
    `T^(1-alpha/2)||C^(1/2)B(T)||->0`.

    For a fixed phase `S`, define
    `p(S)=B(S)+SB_T(S)=(SB(S))_T` and
    `W(T)=B(T)+TB_T(T)/(1-alpha)=[p(T)-alpha B(T)]/(1-alpha)`.
    Matrix Karamata gives the separated-tail asymptotic
    `<Psi_x,Psi_y>~Theta'(x)Theta'(y)T^(-alpha)W(T)^TCp(S)`.
    Thus eventual positivity against every fixed center point requires the
    exact dual-cone condition `W(T)^TCp(S)>=0` for every fixed `S` and all
    sufficiently large `T`.

    This already kills every fixed asymptotic channel mixture.  If
    `B(T)=T^(-gamma)(v+o(1))` and
    `TB_T=-gamma T^(-gamma)(v+o(1))`, normalization needs
    `gamma>1-alpha/2`, so the leading coefficient of `W` is strictly
    negative.  But `v^TC[SB(S)]` starts at zero and is positive for all
    sufficiently large `S`; hence at some finite `S_0` its derivative
    `v^TCp(S_0)` is positive.  The displayed Gram asymptotic is then
    negative.  Persistent logarithmic rotation with nonzero angular speed
    likewise gives both Gram signs; if every `p(S)` were orthogonal to that
    rotation, `(TB)'=p` would reduce it to the fixed-direction case.

    The remaining acute-cone case has a complete regularity no-go.  Conjugate
    `C` to the identity, set `F=TB`, and let `P` be the closed cone generated
    by `{p(S):S>=0}`.  If `P` is not pointed, strict far-tail positivity
    already fails.  If it is pointed, choose two actually attained rays
    within angle `epsilon` of its extreme rays.  Positivity against these two
    fixed tests and planar cone geometry give
    `F(T) dot W(T)>=-epsilon||F(T)|| ||W(T)||` eventually.  Under the natural
    bounded logarithmic derivative
    `||TB_T||<=L||B||`, one has
    `||W||<=C_(L,alpha)||F||/T`.  Choose the two ray approximants so that
    `epsilon C_(L,alpha)(1-alpha)<alpha/2`.  Since
    `p=alpha F/T+(1-alpha)W`, it follows that
    `F dot p>=(alpha/2)||F||^2/T`, and therefore
    `(||F||^2)'>=alpha||F||^2/T`.  Every nonzero tail obeys
