180. **The limiting half-mass tail law can be transported through every core
    anchor with strict reserve, and ordinary PNT makes the construction exact
    at every sufficiently remote finite source.**  Put
    `d=99/100`, `X=[-3/5,3/5]`,
    `beta^+(dz)=2exp(-z/2)K(z)dz`, and
    `a_x(du)=K(u)J(|u-x|)C(x)^(-1)du`.  The beta mass is exactly one half.
    For `z=x+w`, respectively `z=x-w`, with `w>=1`, map
    `z` to `u=x+w-d`, respectively `u=x-w+d`.  The Jacobian is one and
    `|u-z|=d<1`.  A 200-bit interval certificate plus an analytic theta-tail
    estimate proves the pointwise domination
    `a_x(u)>=R_* beta^+(z)`, `R_*=101/100`,              `(180.1)`
    simultaneously for every `x in X` and both signs.  On
    `1<=w<=4`, 110,400 rational cells give the stronger minimum
    `1.01235827922569611364526999723039`; for `w>=4`, the one-term lower
    bound for `K(|z|-d)`, geometric upper bound for `K(|z|)`, and
    `J(h)>=exp(-h/2)` give a ratio above `4.145*10^1051` at the worst tail
    endpoint and an increasing exponent thereafter.  The two anchor images
    lie on opposite sides of the gap `(x-.01,x+.01)` and are disjoint.

    This limiting calculation has an exact finite-source consequence.  For a
    positive outer source s let
    `P_s=sum_(q=p^k) Lambda(q)q^(-1/2)K(s-log q)C(s)^(-1)
             delta_(s-log q)`.
    With `r=q/exp(s)`, ordinary PNT says
    `exp(-s)d psi(exp(s)r)=>dr` uniformly in distribution functions on every
    compact subinterval of `(0,infinity)`.  Integration by parts against the
    C1 weight `r^(-1/2)K(-log r)`, together with
    `exp(s/2)/C(s)->2`, therefore gives
    `sup_I |P_s(I)-beta^+(I)|->0`                       `(180.2)`
    for all intervals whose endpoints lie in any fixed compact z-set.  This
    is a uniform all-real-s statement, not a finite-height sample, and uses
    only `psi(t)~t`.

    Fix `lambda<1/2`.  Uniform beta tightness and bounded density give
    `eta>0,W<infinity` such that, for every core anchor,
    `E_x={|z-x|<=1-eta} union {1+eta<=|z-x|<W}` has
    beta mass strictly above lambda.  Partition the two exterior relative
    intervals into finitely many half-open bins I of width `h<1-d`; use one
    consistent endpoint convention.  Translate each I by distance d toward
    x to a disjoint anchor bin U.  Equation (180.1) gives
    `a_x(U)>=R_*beta^+(I)`.  The beta masses of this finite moving-bin family
    have a positive uniform minimum, so (180.2) yields one S with
    `P_s(I)<=R_*beta^+(I)<=a_x(U)` and `P_s(E_x)>lambda` for every
    `x in X,s>=S`.

    Couple `P_s|I` to
    `alpha_I=P_s(I)a_x|U/a_x(U)` by the normalized product, and use the
    central restriction of `P_s` as an outer-only clock.  These pieces have
    total outer rate `P_s(E_x)>lambda`, disjoint anchor projections dominated
    by `a_x`, and support at separation at most `d+h<1`.  Thinning by
    `lambda/P_s(E_x)` gives an exact rate-lambda subcoupling.  Moving-bin
    indicators and locally finite prime sums are Borel; buffered anchor
    integrals are continuous, and zero-mass bins are assigned zero, so the
    family is measurable.  Reflection treats negative s and asynchronous
    residual kernels complete both marginals.

    Reproduce (180.1) with
    `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/pvdeps python3 -u
       scripts/coupling_anchor_beta_transport_certificate.py`.
    Root and an independent agent reran all 110,400 cells and audited the
    theta monotonicity, analytic tail, beta normalization, injection,
    Jacobian and marginal ledger.  A second independent audit reconstructed
    the uniform weighted-PNT passage, finite moving-bin reserve, Borel
    allocation and exact thinning, and required the now-recorded common
    half-open endpoint convention.  The remaining one-core-to-unit entrance
    sources are the compact middle set
    `|x|<=3/5, 5/4<|s|<S(lambda)` outside the target; no numerical S or
    finite-height extrapolation is being assumed.

    A stronger companion certificate replaces `d=99/100` by `d=1/2`.
    The same 110,400 boxes then give the density ratio
    `2.2605826791826389898>11/5`, and the analytic tail gives
    `8.93*10^770`.  Exterior bins may consequently have any width below one
    half while their complete product target still has separation below one.
    Reproduce with
    `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/pvdeps:scripts python3 -u
       scripts/coupling_anchor_beta_wide_bin_certificate.py`.  Root reran it,
    checked that only the two exact rational profile constants change, and
    obtained PASS under byte compilation and diff check.

181. **A finite chain of buffered hard stages has an exact renewal
    criterion, and rates above one half alone do not satisfy it.**  Fix
    `kappa>0`.  Let stage i start in `K_i`, stop at time `sigma_i` on either
    success into `K_(i+1)` or exit, and put
    `A_i=sup E[exp(kappa sigma_i);success]`,
    `Q_i=sup E[exp(kappa sigma_i);exit]`.  Suppose every exit state has a
    global return to `K_1` with conditional exponential moment at most M.
    If `V_i` is the worst remaining moment from `K_i`, strong Markov gives
    `V_i<=A_i V_(i+1)+Q_i M V_1`, `V_(n+1)=1`.          `(181.1)`
    Iterating this finite recursion yields
    `V_1 <= prod_i A_i
       +M V_1 sum_(j=1)^n Q_j prod_(i<j)A_i`.          `(181.2)`
    Consequently the whole chain has a finite moment whenever
    `M sum_j Q_j prod_(i<j)A_i<1`, with the explicit denominator obtained by
    moving the second term to the left.

    If stage i has an independently thinned exact success clock of rate
    `h_i>kappa`, then `A_i<=a_i:=h_i/(h_i-kappa)`.  For residual exit time
    `xi_(i,R)`, item 178 gives
    `Q_i<=exp(kappa T)P(xi_(i,R)<=T)+exp(-(h_i-kappa)T)`. `(181.3)`
    A sufficient explicit allocation is
    `Q_i<=[2nM prod_(l<i)a_l]^(-1)`, which makes the renewal sum at most one
    half and gives `V_1<=2prod_i a_i`.  First choose T to make the second
    term in (181.3) half this allowance, then enlarge a domain on which the
    same stage clock remains valid until the compact-containment term is
    below the other half.  This precisely states the domain obligation; it
    does not infer it from a fixed-box certificate.

    The obligation is necessary.  In the three-state continuous-time chain
    `0 --h_0--> 1 --h_1--> final`, add a reset `1 --L--> 0`.  Both designated
    rates can exceed one half, but the final hitting-time survival exponent
    is
    `lambda_L={h_0+h_1+L
       -sqrt((h_0+h_1+L)^2-4h_0h_1)}/2`,              `(181.4)`
    which tends to zero as L tends to infinity.  Here
    `M=h_0/(h_0-kappa)` and `Q=L/(h_1+L-kappa)`;
    `MQ<1` is exactly the quadratic inequality `kappa<lambda_L`.  Thus the
    current local bands cannot be composed until their exit factors are
    bounded or their success clocks are extended through exhausting
    buffered domains.  Root independently expanded (181.1), checked the
    renewal denominator and diagonalized the two-state transient generator;
    no Markov property beyond the stated stopped stages and strong-Markov
    restarts is hidden.

182. **The two opposite inward-prime tail laws admit a rigorously bounded
    displacement coupling, so every sufficiently remote pair has an exact
    finite-source compact unit-entrance clock.**  Normalize the two mass-one-
    half measures
    `beta^+(dz)=2exp(-z/2)K(z)dz` and
    `beta^-(dz)=2exp(z/2)K(z)dz`.  Put
    `U(t)=-(log K)'(t)`.  A 4,096-cell 200-bit certificate on `[0,1]` proves
    `U'(t)>18.6527653108151743690`; a first-theta-term relative-tail estimate
    proves `U'(t)>92.8536174294` for `t>=1`.  Evenness extends the weaker
    bound `U'>18` to the line.

    Let `d=3/50` and let `f_+`, `f_-` be the two densities.  Then
    `R_d(t)=log(f_-(t+d)/f_+(t))
       =t+d/2+log K(t+d)-log K(t)`, and
    `R_d'=1-[U(t+d)-U(t)]<1-18d=-2/25`.               `(182.1)`
    Evenness gives `R_d(-d/2)=0`, so the shifted density difference is
    positive and then negative with exactly one crossing.  Its cumulative
    integral starts and ends at zero, hence
    `F_+(t)<=F_-(t+d)`.  Also `f_+>f_-` on the negative half-line and the
    inequality reverses on the positive half-line, with equal total masses;
    thus `F_-(t)<=F_+(t)`.  After shifting variables, these are precisely
    `F_+(t-d)<=F_-(t)<=F_+(t+d)`, the one-dimensional quantile criterion.
    Therefore
    `W_infinity(beta^+,beta^-)<=3/50<1/10`.           `(182.2)`

    This gives an exact finite-source theorem, not a limiting experiment.
    Fix `lambda<1/2`, discard equal quantile mass epsilon from both ends so
    `1/2-2epsilon>lambda`, and choose one common finite partition of the
    quantile parameter interval.  Strictly positive continuous densities on
    that compact range make every image cell for either sign have diameter
    below a, with `d+2a<1`.  Corresponding opposite-sign cells then have
    product diameter below one; equal-sign cells are paired identically.
    Item 180's uniform weighted-PNT lemma makes every finite-source cell mass
    converge uniformly to its common quantile mass.  Hence, for all four sign
    pairs and all source magnitudes above one S, the sum of the cellwise
    minima is above lambda.  Scale each pair of restricted marginals to its
    minimum, take their normalized product, sum the disjoint cells, and thin
    to lambda.  This is a Borel marginal-dominated clock into separation
    below one whose two targets lie in one fixed compact interval.

    Reproduce (182.1)--(182.2) with
    `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/pvdeps:scripts python3 -u
       scripts/coupling_beta_quantile_transport_certificate.py`.
    Root and an independent agent reran it to PASS and separately checked
    the exact theta derivative polynomials, the `K,K',K''` omitted tails,
    the variance representation for U', the one-crossing/CDF argument, the
    equal-mass quantile criterion and the common-quantile finite PNT ledger.
    This removes the remote--remote part of the global unit-entrance problem;
    mixed compact--remote sources and the finite compact square remain.

183. **The lower compact four-fifths band has an exact hard entrance into
    the half-separation/characteristic union, but the high-coordinate part is
    separate.**  Define
    `S_(1/2)={(u,v):c(u,v)<=2/5 or |u-v|<=1/2}`.  After ordering and
    reflection, this certificate covers
    `c(x,y)>2/5`, `1/2<y-x<=4/5`, and `y<=1`.  A live source necessarily has
    `x>=0` and `c=y-(y-x)/2`; hence it lies in the containing rectangle
    `13/20<=y<=1`, `1/2<=r<=4/5`, and a cell is skipped only when the exact
    correlated upper envelope is already at most `2/5`.

    On each source leaf, first take two disjoint archimedean single-jump
    intervals forcing separation strictly below one half.  Add signwise arch
    intervals forcing `c<2/5`, subtracting a rational gap around the first
    allocation.  Their rates are rigorous lower Darboux sums; a 256-bin pass
    is recomputed, not added, with 512 bins before source subdivision when
    needed.  Qualifying one-coordinate prime atoms from
    `q=2,3,4,5,7,8,9` are summed as a correlated function H(s) of each
    marginal source.  If L is its exact rational uniform lower bound, common
    thinning by `L/H(s)` produces an exact rate-L subclock while preserving
    every atom target.  The unused signed atoms are nodes of an exact
    Fraction-valued bipartite max flow; every retained edge rechecks the
    complete rectangle target, and no marginal atom is reused.

    The exhaustive `28 by 30` rational base cover terminates with 866
    certified leaves, 157 already-target skips, maximum quadrisection depth
    two and maximum arch mesh 512.  The worst leaf is
    `y in[.9875,.99375]`, `r in[.760,.765]`; it uses x singles q=2,3, y
    singles q=2,3,4,5 and ten paired-prime edges.  Its 180-bit bound is
    `h_(1/2)>=.50000091844568356979913053947101629375`, `(183.1)`
    with strict reserve `9.1844568356979913*10^(-7)`.

    Reproduce with
    `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/pvdeps:scripts python3 -u
       scripts/coupling_four_fifths_to_half_local_certificate.py`.
    Root and two independent agents completed full reruns to PASS and audited
    the symmetry geometry, skip implication, closed target boundaries and
    strict interior allocation gaps,
    disjoint arch pieces, 256/512 recomputation, correlated thinning, signed
    q control flow, exact flow capacities and adaptive cover.  The companion
    `coupling_four_fifths_to_half_split_singles_falsifier.py` includes the

    give only `.48908484013895734713` on the exact cell
    `y in[.9875,1]`, `r in[.79,.8]`; correlated thinning is therefore a
    genuine part of the proof.  This item does **not** cover
    ordered targets with `1<y<=7/5`; point tests there genuinely fall below
    one half with the present single-arch ledger, and a joint arch--arch
    allocation is the live repair.

184. **Archimedean target rates on one side of a source now have an exact
    positive-series integral, removing the Darboux-resolution bottleneck in
    the high compact band.**  For the positive theta summand `K_n`, put
    `A_n=pi n^2`, `p=5/4+b/2`, and `u=A_n exp(2t)`.  Direct substitution gives
    `int_L^U K_n(t)exp(bt)dt`
    `=A_n^(1-p)/2 {2 Gamma_[u_L,u_U](p+1)
                    -3 Gamma_[u_L,u_U](p)}`.           `(184.1)`
    Every theta summand is positive.  Also
    `J(h)=sum_(k>=0)exp(-(2k+1/2)h)` has positive terms.  Thus truncating both
    sums gives a rigorous lower bound for
    `int_L^U K(z)J(|z-s|)dz` whenever the whole target interval is on one
    side of s; there is no quadrature extrapolation or omitted-tail sign.

    With four theta terms and eighty Levy terms, the exact 200-bit corner
    calculation at `(x,y)=(3/5,7/5)` gives existing single-arch rate
    `.106452290025159800057491220422...` and a common extra target interval
    `[-.9,-.8]` of rates at least
    `1.0116609705362559156*10^(-6)` in the y marginal and
    `1.9004492643440883*10^(-6)` in the x marginal.  Hence the common part is
    the y lower bound.  Root and an independent agent checked the
    substitution, signs, source-side condition, separate C(s) maximum and
    positivity of every omitted term, and reran the script to PASS.  This is
    an exact integration tool and calibrated corner reserve, not yet a cover
    of the full `1<y<=7/5` band.  Reproduce with
    `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/pvdeps:scripts python3 -u
       scripts/coupling_exact_arch_integral.py`.

185. **The complete limiting prime law has an exact full-mass coupling
    through every core anchor at displacement below two fifths.**  For
    `sigma=+/-1` put
    `beta_sigma(dz)=2exp(-sigma z/2)K(z)dz`; evenness of K and
    `int C K=1/4` give `beta_sigma(R)=1/2`.  If `nu_x` is the physical jump
    measure, then for every `|x|<=3/5` there is a finite Borel measure
    `Gamma_(x,sigma)` with
    `pr_1 Gamma=beta_sigma`,
    `pr_2 Gamma<=nu_x+(1/2)delta_x`, and
    `supp Gamma subset {|z-u|<2/5}`.                  `(185.1)`
    The atom at x is free holding: it records an asynchronous jump of the
    other coordinate and does not consume the anchor jump marginal.

    For `|z|>=3/2`, translate z toward zero by `1/10`.  A uniform analytic
    theta bound gives arch-density ratio at least
    `1229.5597318805135`; the images lie outside `(-7/5,7/5)`.  Split the
    middle into 600 half-open bins and the middle arch capacity into 560
    bins.  Eight-subcell midpoint enclosures give rigorous *upper* masses
    for every beta bin.  Uniform lower capacities use the arch bins, the
    exact holding capacity `1/2`, and the complete compatible prime-power
    list `q=2,3,4,5,7,8,9,11`.  On twelve closed anchor cells of width
    `1/20`, retain only product rectangles of diameter below `399/1000` and
    run exact Fraction-valued earliest-deadline flow.  All cells pass without
    refinement; the worst is `x in[.55,.6], sigma=+1`, with current-flow
    reserve `6.5316453282642896*10^(-12)`.

    The upper ledger is a genuine continuum proof.  If `U_i` is the upper
    mass of beta bin `I_i` and the exact flow is `c_(ij)`, multiply its ith
    row by `beta_sigma(I_i)/U_i`.  The row becomes the exact beta restriction
    and every supply column decreases.  Normalized products with the
    constant dominated arch measure or the indicated atom realize the
    scaled entries.  The tail and middle arch supports meet only at null
    endpoints.  Half-open anchor leaves give a piecewise-Borel family; no
    mesh limit or finite-to-infinite extrapolation is used.

    There is also an exact finite-source corollary.  Fix `lambda<1/2` and a
    core anchor.  Choose W so `beta_sigma([-W,W])>lambda`, retain the fixed
    middle bins and finitely partition the two tails through W.  Every
    middle upper ledger has strict finite slack above its true beta mass;
    every translated tail bin has the displayed density reserve.  The
    uniform weighted-PNT convergence (180.2) therefore gives one S such that
    the inward prime measure of every outer source `|s|>=S` fits the same
    finite ledgers and has retained mass above lambda.  Taking tail bins
    sufficiently narrow makes every complete product target have separation
    below one half.  Thin to exact rate lambda.  Thus every sufficiently
    remote core--outer pair has a finite marginal-dominated hard entrance
    clock directly into `r<1/2`.                     `(185.2)`

    Root and two independent agents reran the current 200-bit script and byte
    compilation to PASS.  Their reconstructions explicitly checked
    all 600 worst-leaf rows, 999 positive edges, 511 used supplies, the
    exact half holding cap, q completeness, EDF exchange, row scaling,
    reflection, endpoint nonreuse and every used rectangle.  Reproduce with
    `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/pvdeps:scripts python3 -u
       scripts/coupling_mixed_anchor_beta_full_certificate.py`.
    This closes the remote part of the post-core half-entrance problem; the
    finite middle source range and the global exit/retry ledger remain live.

186. **The finite-source half-mass cancellation has an exact prime-free
    remainder, and the full concentric high-middle Hall family is strictly
    favorable.**  Write
    `P(y)=sum_q Lambda(q)q^(-1/2){K(y-log q)+K(y+log q)}` and
    `c_0=log(pi)-psi(1/4)=gamma+pi/2+log(8pi)`.  Expanding the
    unconditional item-51 radical equation `A_tilde K=-C/2` gives the
    ordinary absolutely convergent pointwise identity
    `C(y)/2-P(y)=c_0K(y)
       +int_0^infinity J(t){K(y+t)+K(y-t)-2K(y)}dt`.   `(186.1)`
    The prime series is locally uniformly absolute by
    `Lambda(n)<=log n` and the Gaussian theta tail.  At zero the symmetric
    second difference is `O(t^2)` while `J(t)=O(1/t)`; at infinity the
    integrand is `O(exp(-t/2))`.  Thus the smooth-core cutoff passage in
    item 51 applies to K without a distributional-to-pointwise gap.

    For epsilon positive, let `M_epsilon(y)` be the complete prime rate plus
    both archimedean jump directions with `|t|>=epsilon`, divided by C(y).
    Subtraction in (186.1) gives exactly
    `C(y){1/2-M_epsilon(y)}=R_epsilon(y)`,             `(186.2)`
    `R_epsilon(y)=K(y){c_0-2I_epsilon}
       +int_0^epsilon J(t){K(y+t)+K(y-t)-2K(y)}dt`,
    where
    `I_epsilon=int_epsilon^infinity J(t)dt
       =atanh(exp(-epsilon/2))+atan(exp(-epsilon/2))`. `(186.3)`
    This proves that the apparent prime-plus-arch equality seen at ordinary
    precision is not an identity; its beyond-precision remainder is governed
    entirely by K and J, not by an unproved PNT error estimate.

    For `epsilon=1/10`, `7/5<=y<=4` and every core anchor
    `|x|<=3/5`, an exact 260-cell positive-theta calculation proves
    `nu_x((y-3/5,y+3/5))>R_epsilon(y)/C(y)`.           `(186.4)`
    It retains the moving slice `[y-3/5,y-11/20]`.  The anchor density is
    minimized at `x=-3/5`, since for `u>x`
    `d_x log[J(u-x)/C(x)]
       =1/2+2/(exp(2(u-x))-1)-tanh(x/2)/2>0`.
    The defect upper bound drops the favorable negative tail term and uses
    `R_epsilon<=c_0K(y)+(epsilon^2/2)sup|K''|`; two
    positive theta terms and a geometric differentiated-theta tail make this
    rigorous.  The worst cell `[1.40,1.41]` has anchor lower bound
    `5.0651471234296331e-7`, defect upper bound
    `8.3428416248072653e-14`, and ratio above `6,071,249`.

    For the separation-only target `|u-v|<=D`, the nontrivial holding-node
    min cuts have the exact form
    `inf_(A superset B_D(y)) {nu_x(A)
       +nu_y(B_D(x) union N_D(A^c))}`.                `(186.5)`
    Equation (186.4) proves every concentric choice
    `A=B_(D+epsilon)(y)` for `D=1/2` on the displayed band.  It does not
    prove (186.5) for disconnected A.  After filling complement gaps shorter
    than `2D`, the first genuinely new family has
    `A^c=[a,b] union [c,d]`, `c-b>=2D`; its four-endpoint inequality is the
    exact live Hall obligation.  No favorable concentric calculation is
    being promoted to all-cut transport.

    Root reran the 200-bit certificate and an independent agent reconstructed
    the radical normalization, constant, convergence, cutoff passage and
    closed form (186.3).  Reproduce (186.4) with
    `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/pvdeps:scripts python3 -u
       scripts/coupling_high_middle_radical_cut_certificate.py`.

187. **The divergent diagonal drift makes the terminal local failure factor
    arbitrarily small, and an explicit Poisson construction closes the local
    Markov-selection gap.**  Fix `kappa<lambda`, a compact entry set `K_0`, and a larger
    compact pair domain `K_R`.  Item 154 gives, in a sufficiently thin tube,
    a bounded continuous nondecreasing extension g with `g(0)=0` and, after
    choosing A, the stopped inequality
    `QW<=-lambda W`, `W=1+Ag(|x-y|)`.                 `(187.1)`
    Put `tau` for diagonal coupling, let
    `sigma_0=inf{t:|X_t-Y_t|>=r_0}` (the inequality is essential because a
    jump may overshoot), let `xi_R` be compact exit, and stop at
    `sigma=tau wedge sigma_0 wedge xi_R`, giving compact exit priority on a
    tie.  If
    `W_in=sup_(K_0,r<=r_in)W` and
    `W_0=inf_(r>=r_0)W>=1+Ag(r_0)`, the stopped exponential
    supermartingale implies, for every T,
    `E[e^(kappa sigma);sigma<tau]
       <=W_in/W_0+e^(kappa T)epsilon_R(T)
          +e^(-(lambda-kappa)T)W_in`,                 `(187.2)`
    where `epsilon_R(T)=sup_(z in K_0)P_z{xi_R<=T}`.

    Indeed separation exits use `W>=W_0` and
    `e^(kappa sigma)<=e^(lambda sigma)`.  Early compact exits cost at most
    `e^(kappa T)epsilon_R(T)`.  On every exit after T, `W>=1` and
    `e^(kappa sigma)<=e^(-(lambda-kappa)T)e^(lambda sigma)W`.
    Optional stopping is first applied at `sigma wedge t`; `W>=1` also gives
    `P{sigma>t}<=W_in e^(-lambda t)`, so Fatou supplies the terminal bounds.

    The right side can be made arbitrarily small in a valid order.  First
    choose T using the prospective bound `W_in<=2`, then R by physical
    marginal compact containment.  On this fixed `K_R`, choose the tube
    where `Qg+lambda g<0`, increase A until
    `A(Qg+lambda g)+lambda<=0`, and finally decrease `r_in` so both
    `W_in<=2` and `W_in/W_0` have the desired size.  Increasing A is
    indispensable: decreasing `r_in` alone leaves the floor
    `1/(1+Ag(r_0))`.

    If every failure state has a return to the entry set with conditional
    `kappa`-moment at most M, the restarted terminal moment obeys
    `V<=W_in+M q V`, where q is the left side of (187.2). `(187.3)`
    Taking `q<1/M` proves `V<=W_in/(1-Mq)`.

    Here is the missing local process, constructed without a selection
    theorem.  Put `a_s(w)=J(|w-s|)K(w)/C(s)`,
    `c_(x,y)(w)=min(a_x(w),a_y(w))`, `d=|x-y|`, and
    `M=max(C(x)/C(y),C(y)/C(x))`.  Choose the symmetric locally Lipschitz
    cutoff
    `delta=min{d/4,delta_bar,
       (1/2)J^(-1)(M J(3d/4))}`.                      `(187.4)`
    For `0<u<delta`, cancellation of K in `a_x/a_y` and monotonicity of J
    give
    `a_x(x+sigma u)>=a_y(x+sigma u)` and
    `a_y(y+sigma u)>=a_x(y+sigma u)`.  Thus the two residual ray densities
    `b^x_sigma(u)=a_x(x+sigma u)-a_y(x+sigma u)` and
    `b^y_sigma(u)=a_y(y+sigma u)-a_x(y+sigma u)` are positive.  Their tails
    `H^i_sigma(u)=int_u^delta b^i_sigma(v)dv` decrease continuously from
    infinity to zero.  If `R^i_sigma` denotes the inverse, the image of dt
    under
    `t ->(sigma R^x_sigma(t),sigma R^y_sigma(t))`      `(187.5)`
    couples the complete two residual restrictions and has finite first
    variation.  The common density c jumps directly to `(w,w)`; outside the
    two delta-balls use the finite asynchronous residuals, and use the usual
    common-prime minima plus residual prime clocks.  Both projections are
    exactly the physical Levy measures and the kernel is exchange symmetric.

    On every compact off-diagonal annulus the inverse-tail identity gives
    `int_0^infinity|R_z(t)-R_z'(t)|dt
       =int_0^infinity|H_z(u)-H_z'(u)|du
       <=int_0^infinity u|b_z(u)-b_z'(u)|du
       <=L_K|z-z'|`.                                  `(187.6)`
    Here `uJ(u)` is bounded at zero, (187.4) is locally Lipschitz, and all
    K/C derivatives are bounded on the compact.  The common and finite
    residual densities are likewise locally Lipschitz in weighted L1: near
    either moving pole the minimum is the other nonsingular density, and
    away from the poles ordinary differentiation is integrable.  Gaussian
    theta tails sum the same estimate over all prime powers.  A fixed marked
    Poisson space therefore realizes the whole jump map G with
    `sup_K int|G(z,u)|dmu<infinity` and
    `int|G(z,u)-G(z',u)|dmu<=L_K|z-z'|`.               `(187.7)`

    Picard iteration for the uncompensated finite-variation equation
    `Z_t=z+int_0^t int G(Z_(s-),u)N(ds,du)` converges in expected path
    supremum by the factorial Gronwall bound from (187.7), and the same
    estimate gives pathwise uniqueness up to compact/diagonal exit.  The
    solution is a measurable nonanticipating function of the Poisson measure;
    shifting its marks at a stopping time proves the strong-Markov property.
    Localize through off-diagonal annuli.  If their lifetimes accumulate at
    distance zero, the locally bounded first variation gives limits of both
    coordinates and the limits agree; set them equal and continue with the
    synchronous one-particle Poisson solution.  This constructs the actual
    coalescent process, not just finite Levy cutoffs.

    Finally (187.5) does not spoil item 154's drift.  Since
    `delta=O(d)`, its absolute g-contribution is
    `O(sup_[3d/4,5d/4]|g'(r)| int(u+v)dGamma)
       =O(log(r_0/d)^(-p-1))`; the common diagonal term remains
    `-[K(x)/C(x)]log(r_0/d)^(1-p)`, and the finite corridors and prime
    mismatch remain `O(1)`.  Hence (187.1), Dynkin and optional stopping
    apply directly to this strong process.  Root and an independent agent
    reconstructed (187.1)--(187.7), including overshoots, the
    `lambda>kappa` late-exit factor, inverse-tail regularity, Poisson Picard
    construction, diagonal patch and drift preservation.  The sole scope
    left by this item is the global return-to-`K_0` kernel with moment M.

188. **The extreme high-coordinate corner has a continuous exact
    half-entrance above one half; the irrational prime-switch boundary no
    longer causes infinite refinement.**  On
    `4/5<=x<=9/10`, `x+1/2<=y<=7/5`, retain the x-arch restriction
    `[9/10,x+1]` and, in the y marginal, the first two positive theta and
    first sixteen positive Levy summands on `[-9/10,13/10]` together with
    inward `q=2,3,4,5,7,8,9`.  The x rate is increasing, because for a
    target above x
    `d_x log[J(u-x)/C(x)]
       =1/2+2/(exp(2(u-x))-1)-tanh(x/2)/2>0`; hence its
    value at `x=4/5` is a valid common lower bound.

    Arch and prime derivatives nearly cancel, so their boxwise minima cannot
    be separated.  The certificate instead differentiates their finite
    analytic sum F(y) twice.  On 100 rational cells Taylor's theorem uses
    the exact midpoint F,F' and an interval upper bound for `|F''|`; a
    certified rational bracket around `y=log 4` treats the sole absolute-K
    cusp directly.  The resulting 102-cell lower cover has worst cell
    `[1.399,1.4]` and
    `F>=.50000049525239759821537189035538`.             `(188.1)`

    The selected y measure is realized without a hidden scalar rate.  Pair
    the arch tail `[-.9,-.8]` identically; transport the three arch blocks
    `[-.8,-.4],[-.4,0],[0,.4]` and individual
    `q=3,4,5,7,8,9` nodes through four x-arch blocks and individual
    `q=2,3,4,5` nodes; and leave `[.4,1.3]` plus y's q=2 as
    one-coordinate clocks.  Every product edge is checked on its full box
    for `r<=1/2` or `c<=2/5`.  The exact Fraction flow terminates after
    4,345 attempted cells with 3,259 leaves, maximum depth six, and at most
    8 x nodes, 9 y nodes and 55 edges.

    On a half-open source leaf, scale every exact upper-demand column by its
    actual selected mass divided by the strict rational upper ledger.  This
    serves the actual demand and only decreases all x uses.  Scale and split
    the corresponding finite-series x-arch restrictions or prime atoms,
    then use normalized products.  The construction is Borel, all marginal
    target pieces are disjoint up to assigned null endpoints, and no mesh
    limit is taken.  The zero displayed worst flow reserve is harmless:
    every demand ledger is a strict rational upper bound before row scaling.

    Root and an independent agent reran both 240-bit scripts, byte-compiled
    them and audited the derivatives, cusp, square parametrization
    `1.4-y=b(.9-x)`, all target edges, marginal nonreuse and continuum lift.
    Reproduce with
    `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/pvdeps:scripts python3 -u
       scripts/coupling_high_coordinate_rate_taylor_certificate.py` and
    `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/pvdeps:scripts python3 -u
       scripts/coupling_high_coordinate_selected_hall_certificate.py`.
    This closes exactly the x-at-least-four-fifths corner.  The adjacent
    `x<4/5`, `1<y<=7/5` portion of the high band remains live.

189. **Every sufficiently remote pair has an exact finite-source entrance
    into one fixed compact one-tenth tube at every subcritical rate.**  Fix
    `lambda<1/2`.  Choose epsilon so
    `m=1/2-2epsilon>lambda`, and parametrize the two continuous positive
    beta laws by quantiles `Q_sigma(u)` on
    `epsilon<=u<=1/2-epsilon`.  Item 182 gives
    `|Q_+(u)-Q_-(u)|<=d=3/50`; equal signs use the identical quantile.
    Choose `a<1/50` and one finite half-open quantile partition whose image
    cells for both signs have diameter below a.  Corresponding cells for all
    four sign pairs then obey the complete product bound
    `|z-w|<=d+2a<1/10`.                               `(189.1)`

    Every cell has the same beta mass `m_j` for the two signs.  Put
    `eta=(m-lambda)/(2N)`.  Ordinary weighted-PNT convergence on this one
    finite interval list supplies S such that both finite-source inward-prime
    cell masses `p_j,q_j` differ from `m_j` by less than eta whenever the
    signed source magnitudes exceed S.  Hence
    `H(s,t)=sum_j min(p_j,q_j)
       >=m-N eta=(m+lambda)/2>lambda`.                 `(189.2)`
    If `mu_j,nu_j` are the two cell restrictions and
    `c_j=min(p_j,q_j)`, then
    `Gamma_j=[c_j/(p_jq_j)]mu_j tensor nu_j`, with the zero convention,
    has mass `c_j`, dominated projections and support in the product cell.
    The disjoint half-open cells prevent reuse.  Multiplying their sum by
    `lambda/H(s,t)` gives exact selected rate lambda and support in one fixed
    compact subset of `r<1/10`.

    All cell restrictions are locally finite prime-power sums with Borel
    weights and indicators; minima, normalized finite products and the final
    thinning factor are Borel.  Reflection patches the four tail signs.  No
    PNT error term or sampled source height is used.  The same proof gives
    every prescribed target diameter `D>3/50`.  It does not prove 3/50 is
    optimal: equal-sign limiting laws have zero W-infinity distance, while
    opposite signs only have the established upper bound `D_*<=3/50`.

    Root and an independent agent reconstructed the quantile cells, uniform
    finite-list PNT passage, exact minimum ledger, product support and Borel
    lift; root reran the unchanged 200-bit curvature certificate and byte
    compilation to PASS.  Reproduce with
    `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/pvdeps:scripts python3 -u
       scripts/coupling_beta_quantile_transport_certificate.py`.

190. **The entire remaining compact four-fifths band has an exact hard
    entrance into the half-separation/characteristic union above rate one
    half.**  Put
    `S_(1/2)={(u,v): |u-v|<=1/2 or c(u,v)<=2/5}`.  Consider
    `|x|,|y|<=7/5`, `c(x,y)>2/5`, and
    `1/2<|x-y|<=4/5`.  After ordering and applying the reflection
    `(x,y)->(-y,-x)`, take `x<y` and nonnegative midpoint.  Item 183 covers
    `y<=1`.  If `y>1`, then
    `x=y-(y-x)>1/5` and `x<y-1/2<=9/10`; hence the three disjoint ownership
    strips
    `1/5<x<=3/5`, `3/5<x<4/5`, and `4/5<=x<9/10`
    exhaust the complement.  The low, middle and item-188 high policies
    cover their closed containing boxes, so `y=1`, `x=3/5` and `x=4/5`
    are assigned explicitly rather than inferred by endpoint continuity.

    On the low strip retain the x-arch interval
    `[x+3/10,x+9/10]`, the first two theta and sixteen Levy terms of the
    y-arch interval `[-9/10,x+1/2]`, and inward
    `q=2,3,4,5,7,8,9`.  The central y interval and q=2,3,4,5 are
    one-coordinate `S_(1/2)` clocks; the remaining y tail and q=7,8,9 are
    transported through four disjoint x-arch blocks and x's q=2,3,4,5
    atoms.  The selected rate decreases with x.  At x=3/5, the y interval
    crosses its source for `1<=y<11/10`; splitting it at y gives exactly
    `A'=rho(A_R-A_L)` and
    `A''=rho^2(A_L+A_R)-2rho K_2(y)`.  Positive incomplete-gamma
    integrals enclose both pieces.  Taylor cells plus direct brackets at
    `log 3,log 4` give
    `h_low>=.500000134066195498126...`.               `(190.1)`

    On the middle strip retain the x-arch interval `[9/10,x+9/10]`, the
    finite y-arch interval `[-9/10,11/10]`, and the same inward prime list.
    Here live correlation gives `y>x+1/2>11/10`, so the y interval is
    wholly left of its source, while the x rate is increasing and x=3/5 is
    worst.  The one-variable Taylor/cusp proof gives
    `h_mid>=.500000134061752507368...`.               `(190.2)`
    Its exact Hall supply uses only x-arch blocks in `[-11/10,3/5]` and
    x's q=2,3,4,5 atoms.  The final block was deliberately truncated from
    `[2/5,7/10]` to `[2/5,3/5]`: the discarded portion crosses sources
    below 7/10 and a one-sided exponential integral would have overstated
    its lower capacity.  The conservative network still closes after 7,361
    attempted cells, with 5,521 certified leaves and maximum depth seven.

    Every finite Hall edge checks its complete product rectangle for
    separation at most 1/2 or characteristic at most 2/5.  Upper demand
    rows are scaled by actual/upper mass, which serves the actual y measure
    and only decreases x-column use; scaled restrictions and normalized
    products give a Borel continuum coupling.  All single, arch and prime
    ledgers are disjoint up to one half-open endpoint convention.  Combining
    (190.1), (190.2), item 188 and item 183 gives the uniform hard rate
    `h_*>=.500000134061752507368...>1/2`.             `(190.3)`
    Every selected target lies in the fixed square
    `[-3/2,19/10]^2`.  Thus this is also a bounded-support compact stage;
    it is not merely a pointwise rate calculation.

    Root and an independent agent reran and reconstructed both repaired
    240-bit pairs.  The audit found the two invalid one-sided uses just
    described before certification; after the crossing-source split in the
    low rate and conservative middle Hall truncation, target geometry,
    correlated source coverage, finite theta/Levy positivity, cusp boxes,
    exact Fraction flows, marginal nonreuse, Borel lift, byte compilation

191. **The complete right-tail Hall term in the high mixed band is dominated
    by an explicit displacement-one-half injection.**  Let
    `f_s(z)=K(z)J(|z-s|)/C(s)` and let `nu_s` include this arch density and
    every prime-power atom.  For all `|x|<=3/5`, `y>=7/5`, and `q>=y`,
    `nu_y([q,infinity))>=nu_x((q+1/2,infinity))`.     `(191.1)`
    Split the y arch tail into two equal ledgers.  The first receives x's
    arch target u by `u->u-1/2`.  Source monotonicity reduces domination to
    `f_(7/5)(w)>2f_(3/5)(w+1/2)`, `w>=7/5`.  Item 182 gives
    `U(0)=0,U'>18` for `U=-(log K)'`, so K is strictly decreasing on the
    positive half-line as used below.

    If `u=x+log n>q+1/2` is an x prime target, then n is at least four.
    Allocate its complete mass from the second y half-ledger on
    `I_n=[u-1/2,u-1/2+1/(4n)]`.  These intervals are disjoint because
    `log(1+1/n)>1/(n+1)>1/(4n)`, they lie above q, and every point is within
    one half of u.  The required atom domination reduces to
    `K(u-7/16)/K(u)
       >=8C(7/5)(u+3/5)exp(u-3/160)`.                `(191.2)`
    For `t>=19/10`, positivity of the first theta term and a geometric upper
    tail give, for `0<d<=1/2`,
    `K(t-d)/K(t)>=(1/2)exp(-9d/2+pi exp(2t)(1-exp(-2d)))
       {1-16exp(-3pi exp(2t))}`.                     `(191.3)`
    At the base point, the continuous ratio is
    `2.7777*10^37>2`; for d=7/16 the kernel ratio is
    `2.5650*10^34` against the required `164.721`.  The logarithmic slope
    lower bound `163.783` exceeds the comparison slope upper bound 1.4 and
    increases thereafter.  Thus (191.1) holds on the whole unbounded band,
    not just at sampled heights, and includes every prime power.

    For a mandatory Hall component `[v-1/2,q+1/2]`, (191.1) proves that its
    cut is minimized at `q=infinity`.  The remaining mandatory inequality is
    exactly
    `nu_x([v-1/2,infinity))+nu_y((−infinity,v])>=1/2`,
    `x+1/2<v<=y`.                                    `(191.4)`
    Extra disconnected components separately require
    `nu_y([p,q]\B_(1/2)(x))
       <=nu_x([p-1/2,q+1/2])`.                       `(191.5)`
    Neither is being assumed.  In particular, one-sided inequalities do not
    imply (191.5): the exact atomic pair
    `.5 delta_2,.5 delta_1.05` satisfies the analogues of (191.1),(191.4)
    at `x=0,y=2` but fails (191.5) on `[1,1.1]`.  Hence a physical
    single-crossing/local-interval mechanism is still required.

192. **Every disconnected Hall component to the right of the mandatory high

193. **The mandatory high-mixed half-line cut is strictly favorable for its
    entire small-left-extension range, uniformly to infinite source height.**

    majorant and `tJ(t)<=exp(3t/2)/2` bound that local term; overcounting
    prime powers by all integers and using `Lambda(n)<=log n` bounds the

    majorant.                                           `(193.2)`

    `1.7979077296*10^(-14)`, a certified ratio above 330,480.  Exact

    Thus no finite-height extrapolation occurs.

195. **The large-left-cut archimedean increment in the high mixed Hall

196. **Every positive-target tail cell of the remaining mandatory prime
    ledger is closed by one outward prime atom.**  In item 195's variables

    `|x|<=3/5`, `y>=7/5`, `e>=log 2`, and `a>=1`, one has the stronger

        nu_x^prime([a,infinity))
          > sum_(log n<=e) w_n K(y-log n)/C(y).        `(196.1)`

    The captured side is bounded without a y cutoff.  If
    `theta(T)=sum_(p<=T)log p`, primes in `(n,2n]` divide

    `theta(T)<4(log 2)T`.  The identity

    `sum_(q<=N)w_q<12sqrt(N)`.  With `N=exp(e)` and
    `C(y)>=exp(y/2)/2`, therefore

        Ypr < 24 exp(-(a+1/2)/2) K(a+1/2).             `(196.2)`

    For `2/5<=d<=log25`, the least prime power from

    For `d>log25`, Nagura's theorem supplies a prime
    `exp(d)<p<(6/5)exp(d)`; at `d=log25`, take p=29 directly.  Thus the
    overshoot is below `log(6/5)` and its
    coefficient exceeds `d exp(-d/2)/sqrt(6/5)`.  Since `a=d+x`, every
    elementary d-exponential cancels against `(196.2)`.  At the worst
    boundary `d=log25,x=-3/5`, the kernel quotient is
    `2.45831897945*10^327`, against a required `8.975720438`; its certified
    logarithmic slope is again positive.  Nagura's published hypothesis
    `X>25` matches `X=exp(d)` off the checked endpoint.  Closed x-tail and
    captured-y switch conventions are
    included.  No PNT or RH-strength error estimate enters.

          +nu_x^arch(E^D)-(1/2)nu_x^arch(I),           `(197.2)`

    with `I=E+4/25` in L and `I=E-1/5` in MR.  Exact switch coordinates are:

    `p=x+1/2+a`, `q=p+b`, `r=y-q-1`, the two prime-log ranges are

199. **The entire compact mandatory high-mixed halfline residual is now

    `d=v-x-1/2>0`, `e=y-v>=log 2`, `a=x+d`, so

200. **The proposed centered high-thin funnel has an exact marginal Hall
    obstruction.**  Put

        F={c<1/5} union {r<13/50 and c<18/25}.

    At the source `(m,r)=(2,1/5)`, hence `(x,y)=(19/10,21/10)`, every
    coordinate of every point of F lies in
    `B=(-17/20,17/20)`.  On the first branch this follows from
    `c>=max(|u|,|v|)/2`; on the second, a same-sign pair has maximum
    coordinate below `18/25+13/100=17/20`, while an opposite-sign pair has
    each coordinate below its separation.  Since both held source
    coordinates lie outside B, no one-coordinate event enters F.  Every
    entrance therefore consumes both marginal projections in B, so its
    rate is at most `nu_(21/10)(B)`.

215. **Hard nested-shell renewal is invalid, and the tested legitimate smooth
    replacement fails the `.499` HJB gate.**  If phase `i` is retained on
    residual jumps and only selected successes advance it, the required
    supersolution inequality is

        kappa W_i(z)
          + integral (W_{tau_i(z,z')}(z')-W_i(z)) Gamma_i(z,dz') <= 0. `(215.1)`

    At `(-1/5,4/5)`, every positive outward y-arch increment leaves the hard
    characteristic shell `S_.40`.  These increments have logarithmically
    infinite intensity near zero, whereas the x-capacity that can pair them
    into the radius-`.1` tube is finite.  Therefore physical-shell exit cannot
    be treated as a finite reset clock.  In the equality-marginal diagnostic,
    `QW/W+kappa` changes from `-1.1837e-4` at cutoff `.0005` to
    `+1.2958e-4` at `.0002` and `+4.3543e-4` at `.0001`.  Ninety smooth,
    residual-retaining ramps were then tested at `kappa=.499`; none passed,
    and the best sampled defect was `+1.224409e-4`.  This finite search is not
    an all-smooth impossibility theorem, but it rejects the current hard-phase
    mechanism and supplies no replacement supersolution.

    The optimistic two-mode restart would additionally need

        L < 2(h-1/2)(g-1/2) = 7.73...e-9,              `(215.2)`

    using the measured `h=.5000927566`, `g=.5000416639`; computed residual
    exit flows are `5.8e-7` to `1.0e-5`.  Those figures are diagnostic, not a
    continuum lower bound.  The reproducible program has SHA-256
    `6ed6a88a471e53a40dc21f8f078e0bbaba6b112190964343edaffc86ab5d98ea`.
