217. **Five-gate route decision: do not select the present nested terminal
    route for further end-to-end investment.**  Gate 1 passes exactly at the
    sharp point; Gate 3 passes on one small uniform box.  Gate 2 fails because
    hard shell resets have infinite activity and no tested residual-retaining
    smooth HJB closes.  Gate 4 fails exactly for every direct target through
    `S_.45`, while `S_.49` lacks both continuum Hall and descent certificates.
    For Gate 5, write `epsilon=1/2-kappa` and choose
    `lambda=1/2-epsilon/2` in item 176.  A restarted compact attempt has

        M=lambda/(lambda-kappa)=(1-epsilon)/epsilon,
        q_kappa < 1/M = epsilon/(1-epsilon).             `(217.1)`

    This bypasses the explicit `y>4` splice only if every exit returns to the
    same compact entry set, the compact success clock survives exhausting
    domains, residual policies have uniform finite-horizon compact
    containment, and fresh strong-Markov restarts are proved.  The current
    buffers do not establish all of these requirements, in particular the
    exhausting-domain success clock and uniform residual containment, so
    item 176 is not a bypass.  The local theorem `(214.1)` is retained for reuse.  Reopen
    this route only with a new structure proving both (i) a continuum
    residual-retaining augmented-phase HJB at every `kappa<1/2`, and (ii) an
    exact high-thin entrance plus descent (currently `S_.49 -> S_.40`), or a
    single joint policy that makes both obligations obsolete.  More threshold,
    cutoff, or mesh tuning is not such a mechanism.  This is a route-selection
    decision, not a conclusion about RH.  An independent route audit reached
    the same decision and identified the same reset, Hall, descent, and
    martingale-limit gates.

218. **The exact terminal `S_.36` contact excludes every age-only or
    residual-credit-neutral lift.**  At `(x,y)=(4/5,6/5)` and
    `F={c<=9/25} union {r<=1/10}`, the closed channel-coloured cover

        A=(-infinity,-11/10]
          union [6/5-log(8)-1/10,6/5-log(7)+1/10]
          union [9/10,infinity)

    together with y-arch targets in `[-6/5,1]` and every y-prime channel
    except the inward `q=7,8` channels covers all simultaneous, held, and
    boundary edges.  Complete theta, prime-power, and analytic-tail bounds
    give

        cap(A,B) <= .4998645374847561613363891553.      `(218.1)`

    Hence at `kappa=4999/10000` the deficit is at least
    `3.5462515243838664e-5`.  If residual jumps are credit-neutral and only
    F-entrances lower continuation, a positive age factor obeys
    `w'+delta w<=0`; this is impossible on a period and forces `w->0` on a
    half-line.  A surviving lift must therefore use genuinely joint
    state-credit residual updates with a negative generator contribution at
    this contact.  Coboundary credits are only conjugacies, and
    `1+exp(-alpha a)Psi(z)` loses all drift as `a->infinity`.  The audited
    240/320-bit certificate is
    `scripts/coupling_terminal_s36_hall_contact_certificate.py`, SHA-256
    `dd1ae9831e33de6ad0b8f2e12155a8cc9ac97b7a4d07cf66207ca216e4abc29c`.

219. **Independent random-horizon and killed-square repairs do not close the
    terminal renewal gap.**  If a compact killed domain has survival overlap
    `a_D(T)`, a horizon-T renewal at exponent kappa requires
    `M exp(kappa T)[1-a_D(T)]<1`, where item 176 gives
    `M=lambda/(lambda-kappa)`.  Every bounded-domain threshold mode leaves
    `1-a_D(T)>=c exp(-T/2)`, while a fixed domain also has a positive exit
    hazard.  Therefore no fixed square works as `kappa->1/2`.  An independent
    random horizon H still has
    `Q_kappa>=c E exp[-(1/2-kappa)H]`; exponential/memoryless choices fail.
    Any possible survivor needs both a growing domain and horizons at least
    of order `epsilon^(-1)log(1/epsilon)`, and must prove the resulting
    compact-overlap decay uniformly.  That missing estimate is not supplied
    by the killed-square construction and the route is not reopened.

220. **The beta-CDF midpoint current has an exact tiny scalar profile but a
    fatal nonconservative/Douglas mismatch.**  Put `c_q=Lambda(q)/sqrt(q)`,
    `ell=log q`, and

        chi(s)=4 int_(-infinity)^s exp(-u/2)K(u)du
              =4pi exp(-3s)sum_(n>=1)n^2exp[-pi n^2exp(-2s)]. `(220.1)`

    Then `chi'=4exp(-s/2)K`, `chi(-infinity)=0`,
    `chi(infinity)=1`, and the grouped orbit current of item 149 satisfies
    exactly

        J_q(x)=H_q(x)chi(x-ell),
        H_q(x)=Lambda(q)exp(x/2)/(4q).                  `(220.2)`

    Orient the physical q-edge `(z,z+ell)` from upper to lower only when
    `z+ell/2>0`.  Its missing scalar source is

        R_q(x)=c_q{K(x-ell)1_(x<ell/2)
                    +K(x+ell)1_(x>-ell/2)},            `(220.3)`

    and
    `G_q(x)=-exp(x/2)int_x^infinity exp(-t/2)R_q(t)dt`
    obeys `(partial-1/2)G_q=R_q`.  The localization is genuinely new:
    both terms of (220.3) lie beyond distance `ell/2` in a theta tail.  Exact
    endpoint formulas give

        |G_q(x)| <= b(q)
          =2pi log(q)q^(3/4)exp(-pi q)/(1-4exp(-3pi q)). `(220.4)`

    Since the squared successive all-integer ratio is at most
    `R=(log3/log2)^2(3/2)^(3/2)exp(-2pi)`, Arb proves

        sup_x sum_(q prime power)|G_q(x)|^2
          <= b(2)^2/(1-R)
          <.00018871628<.000189.                        `(220.5)`

    Thus the old moving-transition congestion is absent.  The deterministic
    certificate
    `scripts/midpoint_prime_current_bound_certificate.py`, SHA-256
    `3183d8af6816b6280f4fd88d3696cbf5ed926a774abe9fdf81bff8864dc20934`,
    passes independent 240- and 320-bit runs.

    This does **not** construct a physical current.  The proposed right side
    `K D_q=c_qK(x)K(x-ell)` has strictly positive integral, whereas every
    physical or demand divergence annihilates constants.  The operator
    `(partial+U-1/2)(KG_q)=KR_q` contains a killing sink; it is not the
    nonlocal conservative arch divergence.  A small kernel
    `exp(-|x-y|/2)sum_qG_q(x)G_q(y)` is only an auxiliary sub-conductance and
    does not identify its graph divergence with the ODE.  Multiplying a
    column by arbitrary spatial data also creates the uncancelled terms
    `a'G_q` and `a(x-ell)-a(x)`.

    Even granting one balanced routed source would not prove item 56.  The
    required object is a contraction `R:H_F->H_E` on **every** continuum
    demand current, with `Div_E R=Div_F`; its missing label is the complete
    demand edge `(a,b)`, equivalently midpoint and separation.  On three
    vertices,
    `B_Eu=(u_1-u_0,sqrt(epsilon)(u_2-u_0))` and
    `B_Fu=(u_1-u_0,u_2-u_0)` route the first balanced source isometrically,
    but at `u=(0,0,1)` give `E=epsilon<F=1`.  Diagonal column bounds therefore
    cannot replace the full cross-Gram inequality.  Reopen this mechanism
    only with an actual conservative two-parameter demand-column selector
    and its uniform operator-domain and norm proof; shifting the present
    profile is not covariant and deconvolution by `Khat=Xi` reintroduces the
    zero divisor.

221. **Balanced beta and centered-q repairs fail as full demand frames.**
    For a selector p on the q-edge, write
    `s_q^+=c_qK(x)K(x-ell)` and `s_q^-=c_qK(x)K(x+ell)`.  Its physical
    divergence satisfies

        delta_q 1=s_q^+-s_q^-,
        s_q^+-delta_q p=s_q^-+delta_q(1-p).             `(221.1)`

    Thus reflection balancing cancels the identical residual and leaves
    exactly the already-present full q-edge.  Differentiating the smooth
    midpoint selector `p_m(z)=chi(z+ell/2-m)` produces a genuine beta dipole,
    but still only on that same q-edge.  Direct arch transport of a beta
    dipole at separation r has limiting squared-norm ratio

        2cosh(r/2)/J(r)=(exp(r)+1)(1-exp(-2r)).          `(221.2)`

    It exceeds one for `r>.2811995743...` and equals `9/4` at `r=log2`.
    Fixed-scale translated beta dipoles also fail the full form dual in the
    tails.  Shifting the item-220 ODE is not covariant: it creates explicit
    seam errors involving `K(x-ell)-K(x-t-ell)` and
    `K(x-t+ell)-K(x+ell)`.

    Centering the incoming q-source gives the unique balanced column

        b_q=c_qK(x)K(x-ell)-4c_qA_q C(x)K(x),
        A_q=int K(x)K(x-ell)dx.                         `(221.3)`

    Even granting mutually orthogonal physical currents `j_q` with these
    divergences and energies `eta_q`, the normalized column analysis is
    compact.  Testing its divergence on the tail cut
    `1_[log(q)/8,infinity)` gives
    `||b_q||_1^2/eta_q<=64E_K(1_[log(q)/8,infinity))`; the exact theta tail
    makes the sum over q finite.  Hence for
    `u_N=chi_0 exp(iNx)`, `chi_0 in C_c^infinity`, dominated
    Riemann--Lebesgue convergence gives

        sum_q |<b_q,u_N>|^2/eta_q ->0,
        F_K(u_N)->2c int |chi_0|^2dmu>0.                `(221.4)`

    No q-orthogonal balanced-column family can therefore supply the Douglas
    lower frame.  The same conclusion holds for arbitrary scalar Bessel
    rescalings of (221.3): normalized cosine modes at Hardy zeros tend weakly
    to zero against every column, with a summable all-q majorant.  A
    nonorthogonal near-singular cross-label synthesis is not excluded, but it
    is precisely new operator structure and is not a consequence of the
    midpoint identity.

222. **The positive real-zero residue system hides RH in weighted spectral
    synthesis.**  For a hypothetical even subthreshold eigenfunction put
    `r=K/C`, `g=Cv in L2(r)`.  Item 62 proves translate annihilation, but
    does not make g tempered; the correct statement is only
    `g in N=M_Xi^perp` in the item-63 weighted RKHS.  Translations are
    unbounded on `L2(r)`, so classical local mean-periodic synthesis does not
    pass to its Hilbert or generator graph norm.  If
    `S` is the closed span of all real-zero exponential-polynomial jets,
    every vector in `N minus S` is invisible to every real-zero residue.

    This hidden space is not a technical possibility that can be discarded.
    For any Xi zero `z_0` of multiplicity m,

        Q_(z_0)(z)=Xi(z)/(z-z_0)^m                     `(222.1)`

    belongs to the exact RKHS.  For a simple zero, solve
    `(-i partial-z_0)q=K` by either theta tail integral

        q(x)=i exp(iz_0x)int_(-infinity)^x exp(-iz_0t)K(t)dt
            =-i exp(iz_0x)int_x^infinity exp(-iz_0t)K(t)dt; `(222.2)`

    equality uses `Xi(z_0)=0`, and `q/r` is the RKHS preimage.  Iteration
    handles multiplicity.  If `z_0` is nonreal, (222.1) vanishes with every
    required jet at every real Xi zero but is nonzero at `z_0`.  Hence the
    off-line root mode is outside S already in `L2(r)`.  The assertion
    `S=N` therefore itself excludes every off-line zero and already implies
    RH.

    Conditional on graph-norm synthesis and simple real zeros, residue
    extraction would give

        (lambda-1/2)a_j
          =m_j sum_k R(i(gamma_j-gamma_k))a_k.          `(222.3)`

    Multiplying by `conjugate(a_j)/m_j` makes the right side the positive
    `L2(r)` Gram and contradicts `lambda<1/2`.  Without synthesis, (222.3)
    has an unavoidable off-line/projection/vertical-contour remainder; with
    multiple zeros it is additionally a jet-block system.  Setting that
    remainder to zero is exactly the RH-strength division/hereditary-
    completeness statement, not a consequence of translate annihilation or
    the eigen equation.

223. **Integrated exponential modes give an exact martingale frame, while
    every endpoint-local mixer collapses; prime--arch triangle cycles are the
    first live nonlocal class.**  Put `alpha_k=2k+1/2` and

        A_k(a)=int_(-infinity)^a exp(alpha_k x)K(x)dx,
        B_k(a)=int_a^infinity exp(-alpha_k x)K(x)dx,
        sigma_k=A_k/B_k.                                `(223.1)`

    Geometric summation of
    `J(h)=sum_(k>=0)exp[-alpha_k h]` gives, for `s<=t`,

        G_A(s,t)=sum_k A_k(s)B_k(t),
        A_k(min)B_k(max)
          =int B_k(s)1_(a<s)B_k(t)1_(a<t)sigma_k'(a)da. `(223.2)`

    The integrated series is locally convergent because
    `A_k(a)B_k(a)<=||K||_infinity^2/alpha_k^2`; the raw edge modes have a
    divergent diagonal and may not be separated first.  Integration against
    `u'` yields the exact reverse-tail martingale identity

        E_A(u)=sum_k int sigma_k'(a)B_k(a)^2
          |E_(nu_(k,a))u-u(a)|^2 da,
        nu_(k,a)(dx)=1_(x>a)exp(-alpha_k x)K(x)dx/B_k(a). `(223.3)`

    This does not create new physical capacity.  On an arch edge of length h
    let

        r_k(h)=sqrt[exp(-alpha_k h)/J(h)]
              =sqrt(1-exp(-2h))exp(-kh).                `(223.4)`

    Then `||r(h)||_(ell2)=1` and the parallel gradient is exactly the scalar
    full-J gradient tensored with `r(h)`.  The fiberwise Householder unitary
    sending `r(h)` to `e_0` sends all other mode directions to divergence-
    free parallel-edge circulations.  Equivalently, among currents with
    `sum_k I_k=I`, Cauchy--Schwarz gives

        sum_k |I_k|^2/[exp(-alpha_k h)K(x)K(y)]
          >=|I|^2/[J(h)K(x)K(y)],                       `(223.5)`

    with equality only at `I_k=exp(-alpha_k h)I/J(h)`.  Prime fibers live on
    measures singular to the arch endpoint measure.  Consequently every
    endpoint-decomposable partial isometry is precisely conductance thinning
    by scalars `0<=theta_A(x,y),theta_q(x)<=1`.  Such a selector would force
    pointwise domination of the target crossing kernel, contradicted by the
    exact negative entry in item 75 at
    `(-log(2)/2,log(2)/2)`.  Thus neither mode rotations nor endpoint-local
    arch--prime mixing survive.

    There is nevertheless a new exact nonlocal probability kernel.  Write
    `m=CK`, `barM(a)=int_a^infinity m`,
    `w_k=exp(-alpha_k x)K`, and

        rho_k=m/w_k=C exp(alpha_k x)
             =[exp((2k+1)x)+exp(2kx)]/2.

    Integration by parts proves that

        Pi_(k,a)=rho_k(a)B_k(a)/barM(a) delta_a
          +1_(b>a)rho_k'(b)B_k(b)/barM(a) db            `(223.6)`

    is a probability measure and, exactly,

        E_(mu(.|>a))u-u(a)
          =int_{b>=a}[E_(nu_(k,b))u-u(b)+u(b)-u(a)]
             Pi_(k,a)(db).                              `(223.7)`

    A fixed-mode Jensen contraction already fails before paying for the path
    increment.  Its use density is

        L_k(b)=2cB_k(b){m(b)rho_k(b)/barM(b)
                   +rho_k'(b)log[c/barM(b)]},

    whereas the available density is
    `W_k=sigma_k'B_k^2`.  At `k=0,b=0`, symmetry gives the exact deficit

        L_0(0)-W_0(0)
          =B_0(0)[2K(0)+(log2)/4]>0.                    `(223.8)`

    Infinite-mode splitting is not excluded by (223.8), because its raw
    central intensity diverges while the integrated innovations shrink.

    The first physical class able to create signed cross-edge correlations
    consists of prime--arch triangle cycles.  For
    `x<z<x+ell_q`, amplitudes `tau_q(x,z)` define

        (Ztau)_q(x)=-int_x^(x+ell_q)tau_q(x,z)dz,
        (Ztau)_A(a,b)=sum_(ell_q>b-a)
          [tau_q(a,b)+tau_q(b-ell_q,a)].                `(223.9)`

    The triangle identity makes `Div_E Ztau=0` exactly.  A source current for
    the target Brownian column is

        I^0_(s,A)(x,y)=2m(x)m(y)1_(x<s<y), I^0_(s,q)=0, `(223.10)`

    whose divergence is
    `2c m(z)[1_(z>=s)-barM(s)/c]dz`.  The precise live lemma is to construct
    a closable linear choice `tau_s` such that `I_s=I_s^0+Ztau_s` and

        ||sum_i a_i I_(s_i)||_cur^2
          <=2c sum_(i,j)a_i conjugate(a_j)H(s_i,s_j)    `(223.11)`

    for every finite family.  Here the current norm uses the exact reciprocal
    arch and prime conductances.  Equations (223.9)--(223.11) would give the
    item-56 Douglas contraction and hence RH.  They are not being assumed;
    deriving the cycle normal equations and falsifying restricted cycle
    classes is the current construction task.

224. **The subthreshold eigen-equation isolates a signed smoothed-PNT
    obstruction; proved exterior decay does not make `Cv` tempered.**  Let
    `Tv=lambda v`, `0<lambda<1/2`, `delta=1/2-lambda`,
    `f=Kv=(K/C)g`, and `g=Cv`.  The pole-free equation is

        delta g=P f+c_0f-A_arch f,                       `(224.1)`

    where

        Pf(x)=sum_(n>=2)Lambda(n)/sqrt(n)
                    [f(x-logn)+f(x+logn)],
        A_arch f=int_0^infinity J(t)
                    [2f(x)-f(x-t)-f(x+t)]dt.

    Hilbert Cauchy--Schwarz and the double-exponential K tail give every
    exponential moment of f.  Evenness and zero mu-mean give
    `int exp(+/-x/2)f(x)dx=0`.  With

        S(t)=sum_(logn<=t)Lambda(n)/sqrt(n),
        V(t)=S(t)-4sinh(t/2),                            `(224.2)`

    subtraction of the continuous main term is therefore exact in
    distributions:

        Pf(x)=int_[0,infinity)[f(x-t)+f(x+t)]dV(t).      `(224.3)`

    The other terms in (224.1) are tempered.  Ordinary PNT gives only
    `V(t)=o(exp(t/2))` (effectively
    `V(t)<<exp(t/2-c sqrt(t))`), which permits
    `g(x)<<exp(x/2-c sqrt(x))`, not polynomial growth.  The actual form
    domain supplies no positive Sobolev order, so a pointwise integration by
    parts against `V` is also unavailable.

    This logical gap persists for positive smooth PNT-quality arithmetic
    data.  Replacing the prime measure by
    `dpsi_alpha(u)=(1+u^(-alpha))du`, `0<alpha<1/2`, gives
    `psi_alpha(X)=X+o(X)` but a mismatch with term
    `X^(1/2-alpha)/(1/2-alpha)`.  For an even compact smooth phi, the
    pole-neutral test

        f_a(y)=phi(y-a)+phi(y+a)-2cosh(a/2)phi(y)

    has nonzero Laplace moment at `1/2-alpha`; its counterpart of (224.3) is
    a nonzero multiple of `exp[(1/2-alpha)x]` for large x.  Positivity,
    smoothness, compact support, and ordinary PNT therefore do not imply
    temperedness.  Taking absolute values destroys the two pole moments and
    restores the positive `exp(x/2)` main term, so Markov/Kato domination
    cannot repair the cancellation.

    The exact unproved lemma is the eigenfunction-specific estimate

        int_[0,infinity)[f(x-t)+f(x+t)]dV(t)
          =O((1+|x|)^N)                                 `(224.4)`

    in a justified distributional norm.  A polynomial global bound on V
    proves it but is square-root PNT and RH-equivalent by items 49 and 55.
    For one f, (224.4) need not deconvolve V because its bilateral Laplace
    transform may vanish at the offending exponents.  Thus this branch
    remains a possible eigenfunction-specific route, but its missing signed
    arithmetic cancellation is not supplied by item 62, ordinary PNT,
    positivity, or the existing domain theory.

225. **Every positive diagonal split of the integrated martingale frame
    fails, even with infinitely many modes.**  For measurable
    `theta_k(a)>=0`, `sum_k theta_k(a)=1`, put

        h(a)=m(a)/barM(a),
        H_k(b)=int_(-infinity)^b h(a)theta_k(a)da.

    The exact use density of the reverse innovation in (223.7) is

        L_(k,theta)(b)=2cB_k(b)
          [h(b)rho_k(b)theta_k(b)+rho_k'(b)H_k(b)],     `(225.1)`

    while the available density is

        W_k(b)=K(b)[exp(alpha_kb)B_k(b)
                         +exp(-alpha_kb)A_k(b)].        `(225.2)`

    A fiberwise diagonal contraction exists exactly when
    `L_(k,theta)<=W_k` for every k almost everywhere.  The theta tail gives,
    for fixed k and `b->infinity`,

        W_k(b)/[2cB_k(b)rho_k'(b)]
          ~[2pi M_k/(c(2k+1))]exp[(1-2k)b],
        M_k=int exp(alpha_kx)K(x)dx>0.                  `(225.3)`

    Hence (225.1)--(225.2) force `H_k=0`, and therefore
    `theta_k=0`, for every `k>=1`.  Thus `theta_0=1` almost everywhere.  At
    zero, evenness gives `A_0=B_0`, `rho_0=1`, `rho_0'=1/2`, and
    `H_0(0)=log2`, so on a whole neighborhood

        L_(0,theta)(0)-W_0(0)
          =B_0(0)[2K(0)+(log2)/4]>0.                   `(225.4)`

    More sharply, the atom `b=a` in `Pi_(0,a)` has no path increment and at
    `a=0` alone loads `4K(0)B_0(0)` against available density
    `2K(0)B_0(0)`.  Thus the path cross term cannot repair even the atomic
    deficit.  For the continuous part, the two functionals
    `E_(nu_(k,b))u-u(b)` and `u(b)-u(a)` use the disjoint derivative regions
    `(b,infinity)` and `(a,b)`; the local inequality
    `gamma|I+D|^2<=A|I|^2+P|D|^2` holds for all I,D iff
    `gamma(A^(-1)+P^(-1))<=1`, so there is no automatic favorable
    cancellation.  A finite prime-only path from a has endpoint in the
    countable set `a+log(Q_>0)`, whereas the breakpoint law has positive
    density at every `b>a`.  Consequently every positive mode-by-mode
    innovation allocation, every separate path ledger, and every finite
    positive prime recursion are closed.  A survivor would have to synthesize
    the reverse innovation itself through a signed nonlocal cross-label
    operator.

226. **The positive theta refinement of the breakpoint law uses exactly zero
    prime capacity and high modes return the original demand.**  The stronger
    form of (223.6) is the measure disintegration

        1_(x>a)m(x)dx/barM(a)
          =int nu_(k,b)(dx)Pi_(k,a)(db),                `(226.1)`

    whose conditional breakpoint law, for fixed `a<x`, is

        [rho_k(a)delta_a(db)
          +1_(a<b<x)rho_k'(b)db]/rho_k(x).              `(226.2)`

    Thus it literally splits `u(x)-u(a)` at a continuously distributed b.
    For `a>=0`, the positive theta expansion is

        B_k(a)=sum_(n>=1)n^(2k)
          int_(a+logn)^infinity exp(-alpha_ky)phi(y)dy. `(226.3)`

    A divisor edge connects
    `x=y-logn` to `x+logq=y-log(n/q)`.  One of the two breakpoint legs equals
    this edge only on a singleton `b=x-logq` or `b=a+logq`; the sole atom in
    (226.2) is at b=a, and the exceptional endpoint
    `x=a+logq` is also null.  Countably many prime powers remain null.  Hence
    the direct positive theta/divisor refinement consumes exactly no prime
    conductance.

    Adding such atoms changes the current by the signed cycle

        e_A(r,z)+e_A(z,r+ell_q)-e_q(r),                 `(226.4)`

    rather than refining (226.1).  This is precisely the nonlocal triangle
    mixer (223.9).  Moreover, uniformly on compact sets as `k->infinity`,

        exp(alpha_kb)B_k(b)=K(b)/alpha_k+O(alpha_k^(-2)),
        E_(nu_(k,b))u-u(b)=u'(b)/alpha_k+O(alpha_k^(-2)),
        W_k(b)=2K(b)^2/alpha_k+O(alpha_k^(-3)),         `(226.5)`

    while `Pi_(k,a)` converges weakly to `mu(.|>a)` and its conditional
    breakpoint converges to b=x from the left.  The innovation vanishes and
    the remaining path is the original full increment `u(x)-u(a)`.  Thus
    arbitrarily high modes do not open a limiting shortcut; they hand the
    complete demand back to the cycle/Douglas problem.

227. **All-q prime--arch triangles span the full cycle space; smaller natural
    length classes fail exact tail tests.**  Let
    `w_A(x,y)=J(y-x)K(x)K(y)` and
    `w_q(x)=c_qK(x)K(x+ell_q)`.  On the finite smooth triangle core, Z is
    closable in the reciprocal-conductance current Hilbert space and

        (Z^*I)_q(x,z)=I_A(x,z)/w_A(x,z)
          +I_A(z,x+ell_q)/w_A(z,x+ell_q)-I_q(x)/w_q(x) `(227.1)`

    for `x<z<x+ell_q`; its maximal domain is given by square integrability of
    these triangle voltages with any fixed summable positive auxiliary core
    weight.  Hence a minimizing correction, if attained, solves
    `Z^*(I_s^0+Ztau_s)=0`; otherwise the same equation holds for the
    projection onto `closure(Ran Z)` and tau is a minimizing sequence.

    In fact `closure(Ran Z)=ker Div_E`.  To see this, let I be orthogonal to
    every triangle and write `F=I_A/w_A`, `G_q=I_q/w_q`.  Then for every
    `L=nlog2` or `m log3`,

        F(x,z)+F(z,x+L)=G_L(x)                          `(227.2)`

    almost everywhere on `x<z<x+L`.  The cocycle defect

        T_x(r,s)=F(x,x+r)+F(x+r,x+r+s)-F(x,x+r+s)

    is periodic in x with every sufficiently large length from both towers.
    Their irrational ratio makes it independent of x.  Comparing the
    reflection maps `(r,s)->(s,L-r-s)` for the two towers and using
    `m log3-n log2` dense in R makes T invariant under every admissible
    translation in r and s; local-L1 translation continuity therefore gives
    `T=constant=kappa`.  Thus

        F(x,y)=h(y)-h(x)+kappa.                         `(227.3)`

    If `kappa!=0`, choose a positive-measure compact set on which h is
    uniformly continuous.  Since `w_A(x,x+t)~K(x)^2/(2t)`, integrating over
    its small self-overlaps makes the current norm diverge.  Therefore
    `kappa=0`, and (227.2) gives
    `G_q(x)=h(x+ell_q)-h(x)`.  Value truncation, spatial cutoff using the
    double-exponential K tail, and mollification identify these as exactly
    the closure of finite-energy gradient currents.  Orthogonal complements
    now give `closure(Ran Z)=ker Div_E`.

    Consequently the all-q triangle projection is the full electrical
    minimizer, and its Brownian-column Gram bound is exactly the item-56
    Douglas inequality, not a new mechanism.  Proper natural subclasses do
    not suffice.  For a finite q-set with `L_*=max ell_q`, every correction
    leaves arch edges of length at least `L_*` untouched.  With

        A=int C(x)^2exp(-x/2)K(x)dx>0,
        N(s)=int_s^infinity C(y)^2exp(y/2)K(y)dy,

    the untouched current obeys

        inf_tau ||I_s^0+Ztau||_cur^2 >=R_L(s)~4A N(s).

    Writing `Y=pi exp(2s)`, exact theta-tail asymptotics give

        barM(s)~(pi/2)exp(3s-Y),
        N(s)~(pi/4)exp(4s-Y),                           `(227.4)`

    so its ratio to the target diagonal `2M(s)barM(s)` is asymptotic to
    `4Aexp(s)` and diverges.  A finite union of complete prime towers also
    fails: its incoming tail degree divided by C tends to zero, and the
    item-51 narrow packets make its arch-plus-tower energy/target ratio tend
    to zero.  Thus finite sets/towers fail, while the all-q class is exactly
    the original RH-equivalent problem.

228. **Finite state-independent credit updates cannot repair the exact
    `S_.36` Hall contact, and the certified surplus box is too narrow for a
    direct two-region cycle.**  At `(x,y)=(4/5,6/5)`, item 218 gives the
    universal success bound

        h_i<99973/200000,
        kappa=4999/10000=99980/200000,
        delta_i=kappa-h_i>delta_*=7/200000.             `(228.1)`

    Grant value zero to every success.  Any finite, channel-dependent but
    physical-state-independent residual credit updater has a conservative
    generator Q, so a positive contact vector would have to satisfy

        [diag(delta_i)+Q]v<=0.                          `(228.2)`

    At an index minimizing v, `(Qv)_i>=0`, contradicting (228.1).  Equivalently
    Collatz--Wielandt gives spectral abscissa at least `delta_*`; multiplying
    around a deterministic credit cycle gives the same impossible strict
    product.  A fixed nonidentity credit update on all sufficiently small
    arch jumps has infinite exit rate because their Levy density is
    `A/(2|h|)+O(1)`.  Integrably thinning the updates produces the finite Q
    already excluded.  Thus two, three, or any finite number of credit-only
    states do not defeat the contact.

    Genuine spatial revaluation is different.  In the minimal two-region
    model, let the contact deficit be delta, the second region have surplus
    epsilon, and let the forward and return rates be a,b.  A strict positive
    supersolution exists exactly when

        a epsilon>delta(b+epsilon).                    `(228.3)`

    The uniform item-214 surplus box has y-width `7/10^6`.  From `y=6/5`
    its arch distance is greater than `39/100`, whence `J<2`; there is no
    prime target in the box.  Its complete entrance rate therefore obeys

        a<14/10^6<delta_*=35/10^6.                     `(228.4)`

    It fails even if assigned value zero.  Reserving item 214's certified
    surplus `epsilon_*=101/10^6`, (228.3) requires
    `a>(35/101)b+35/10^6`, again impossible for this direct edge.  The
    smallest transport class not excluded is consequently a genuinely
    spatial contact--intermediate--surplus--return HJB (or a proved negative
    continuous spatial drift).  No such physical multiregion kernel is yet
    certified, so the former nested-shell route is not being silently
    reopened.

229. **All-zero residue synthesis restores the exact off-line Weil
    indefiniteness; only the real-zero restriction was positive.**  Center
    a zeta zero by `s=rho-1/2` and put

        R(z)=int r(x)exp(-zx)dx, r=K/C.

    Conditional on all-zero graph-norm synthesis and a controlled contour
    remainder, simple roots would obey

        (lambda-1/2)a_j=m_j sum_k R(s_j-s_k)a_k.        `(229.1)`

    The genuine Hilbert Gram of root modes `exp(s_jx)` is instead

        G_jk=R(conjugate(s_j)+s_k).                     `(229.2)`

    The two kernels coincide, up to transpose, only when
    `conjugate(s_j)=-s_j`, namely on the critical line.  Calling the residue
    matrix a Gram off the line already assumes the desired conclusion.

    For a quartet `s_(epsilon,eta)=epsilon a+i eta gamma`, `a,gamma>0`, let

        d_e=(1,-1,-1,1), d_o=(1,-1,1,-1).

    Direct expansion of (229.1) gives the two strict identities

        d_e^*K d_e
          =-16m int r(x)sinh(ax)^2 sin(gamma x)^2dx<0,
        d_o^*K d_o
          =-16m int r(x)sinh(ax)^2 cos(gamma x)^2dx<0. `(229.3)`

    The corresponding `L2(r)` norms are the same integrals multiplied by
    `+16`, so each residue-to-Hilbert quotient is exactly `-m`.  These are
    the even and odd negative directions of an off-line quartet.  For a root
    of multiplicity `m_j`, the required jets
    `x^q exp(s_jx)/q!`, `0<=q<m_j`, have confluent block

        (-1)^q R^(p+q)(s_j-s_k)/(p!q!).                `(229.4)`

    The scalar quartet block remains, multiplied by positive multiplicity;
    jets cannot change its sign.  Finite truncation also has an outside-row
    remainder `sum_(k outside F)R(s_j-s_k)a_k`; deleting it assumes an
    unproved invariant interpolation subspace.

    Equivalently the zero-side form pairs `F(s)` with
    `F(-conjugate(s))`: on line it is a square, while off line it contains an
    indefinite cross pair.  Thus all-zero synthesis need not be circular by
    itself, but it supplies no sign; positivity of its action system is the
    original Weil/RH assertion.  The real-zero-only positive system in item
    222 was positive precisely because excluding the off-line blocks already
    encoded RH.

230. **The bilateral-moment bootstrap of the V convolution annihilates its
    own off-line coefficient.**  For `f=rg`, `r=K/C`, define the entire
    moment

        F(s)=int exp(-sx)f(x)dx.

    Evenness gives `F(-s)=F(s)` and the pole moments give
    `F(+/-1/2)=0`.  If `rho=1/2+z`, `Re z>0`, is a zero of multiplicity m,
    its functional-equation pair contributes

        dV_(z,-z)(t)=-m[exp(zt)+exp(-zt)]dt.

    Direct changes of variables, justified by all exponential moments of f,
    yield the exact identity

        (V_(z,-z)*f)(x)=-2mF(z)cosh(zx),                `(230.1)`

    and the full quartet gives `-4m Re[F(z)cosh(zx)]`.  Thus polynomial
    growth would require `F(z)=0` at every off-line exponent.

    But `kappa(s)=int exp(-sx)K(x)dx` is a nonzero constant multiple of
    `xi(1/2+s)`, so

        K*cosh(z .)=kappa(z)cosh(z .)=0.                `(230.2)`

    Convolving the eigen-equation with K therefore deletes both sides of the
    coefficient relation `delta a_z=-2mF(z)`; it cannot force `F(z)=0`.  The
    obstruction is realized inside the exact weighted space.  Put

        g_*(x)=cosh(conjugate(z)x), f_*=r g_*.

    Then `K*g_*=0` and both pole moments vanish, while

        F_*(z)=int r(x)|cosh(zx)|^2dx>0.                `(230.3)`

    A real even component retains a nonzero moment.  For a zero of order m,
    the additional K-null jets `partial_z^j cosh(zx)`, `j<m`, only enlarge
    this obstruction; the logarithmic-derivative forcing itself still has
    residue m and no higher x power.

    Formal bilateral transforms falsely hide (230.1): the meromorphic
    continuations of the z and -z terms cancel, but they have no common
    convergence strip because `cosh(zx)` grows as `exp(Re(z)|x|)`.  The two
    prime Dirichlet series likewise live in disjoint half-planes.  Requiring
    a pole-free common continuation is the desired tempered conclusion in
    disguise.  Hence translate annihilation, the pole moments, and the
    eigen-equation do not bootstrap (224.4).  A survivor must use additional
    arithmetic structure that controls the off-line root coefficient rather
    than convolving it with its zero divisor.

231. **Each triangle chord has an exact Schur projection, but local
    Kaczmarz/Euler-product schedules fail uniform Brownian-column control.**
    Fix a chord `(x,y)`, `y=x+ell_q`, with conductance `w_0=w_q(x)`.  For
    intermediate z let `w_1=w_A(x,z)`, `w_2=w_A(z,y)`,

        g(z)=w_1w_2/(w_1+w_2), G=int_x^y g(z)dz,
        a(z)=I_1(z)/w_1+I_2(z)/w_2-I_0/w_0.

    The exact orthogonal triangle correction `(-T,tau,tau)` is

        A=int g a,
        tau(z)=g(z)[a(z)-A/(w_0+G)],
        T=w_0 A/(w_0+G),                               `(231.1)`

    and its energy decrement is

        int g|a|^2-|int ga|^2/(w_0+G).                 `(231.2)`

    For the Brownian column, a vanishes unless `x<s<y`; inside that interval
    it equals

        2C(z)C(y)/J(y-z), z<s;
        2C(z)C(x)/J(z-x), z>s.                         `(231.3)`

    Polarization of (231.2) gives an exact two-column decrement.

    This conditional formula does not factor over primes.  A fixed x is a
    null fiber in the physical dx/dxdy Hilbert space; positive-measure blocks
    overlap in their arch arms.  Distinct q,r triangles sharing one short
    arch edge have strictly positive inner product from that edge and
    distinct prime chords, so their projectors are noncommuting.  A legitimate
    ordered schedule has only the Pythagorean identity

        <R_N I_s,R_N I_t>=<I_s,I_t>
          -sum_(n<=N)<P_nR_(n-1)I_s,P_nR_(n-1)I_t>.    `(231.4)`

    Every finite prefix has a largest used length L.  It leaves every arch
    edge of length at least L unchanged, whence the exact item-227 tail lower
    bound gives

        ||R_N I_s^0||^2/[2M(s)barM(s)]
          >=(4A+o(1))exp(s)->infinity.                 `(231.5)`

    Therefore no common finite/local schedule converges in operator norm or
    uniformly in the required cross-Gram norm on normalized Brownian columns.
    Source-dependent orders give different column maps and do not establish
    one linear contraction.  Projecting all unbounded q globally gives the
    full electrical minimizer by item 227, and bounding it is the original
    Douglas lemma.  Thus the local Kaczmarz or order-independent Euler-product
    mechanism is closed, while a genuinely global analytic column formula is
    not excluded.

232. **The existing exact coupling certificates do not assemble into the
    surviving spatial three-region HJB.**  For a contact deficit delta, an
    intermediate deficit eta, a terminal surplus epsilon, forward rates a,c,
    and return rate b, the most favorable directed three-state Metzler system
    has a positive strict supersolution exactly when

        a>delta, c>eta,
        b/(b+epsilon)<(1-delta/a)(1-eta/c),             `(232.1)`

    equivalently `(a-delta)(c-eta)(b+epsilon)>acb`.  Any uncovered exit or
    earlier return makes this stricter.

    Even optimistically treating items 175, 177, and 179 as a contained
    forward chain, their exact rates

        a_0=.58458247336501032691,
        a_1=.51224345014371263303,
        a_2=.50243823019917168732

    at `kappa=.4999`, together with the item-218 deficit
    `delta_*=7/200000`, give the product

        (1-delta_*/a_0)(1-kappa/a_1)(1-kappa/a_2)
          =.00012172575593434... .                     `(232.2)`

    The item-214 uniform box has surplus
    `epsilon=.5000010038854654-.4999`; (232.1) would require
    `b<1.229628*10^(-8)`.  Without simultaneously crediting the item-218
    terminal success, the necessary return rate is below `1.782*10^(-9)`.

    More importantly, the optimistic chain is not physical.  Item 177's
    selected q=16 target on an explicit source cell can exceed `5/4` while
    remaining outside item 179's target, so it is not contained in item 179's
    source square.  Deleting that channel loses the certified rate.  Item 179
    lands in the broad union `{c<2/5 or r<4/5}`, not the tiny item-214 source
    box; no certified positive-rate edge joins the two, and the item-218
    deficient contact itself lies in the broad union.  Finally, a hard lower
    value on the item-214 box has infinite outward arch exit charge because
    `J(h)=1/(2|h|)+O(1)`.  Smoothing removes that divergence only after a full
    first-variation, nonlocal-exit, and return estimate which no recorded
    certificate supplies.  Item 178's exhausting-domain retry does not match
    these fixed source domains.

    Hence items 175, 177, 179, 214, and 218 alone cannot form continuum HJB
    rows.  A new spatial mechanism would need a contained bridge into a
    positive-width surplus region and a smooth total leakage/return charge
    below the bound in (232.2); no such theorem is presently proved.

233. **Prime-log irrationality gives dense but nonclosed triangle range;
    the bad modes are exponentially invisible to Brownian columns.**  Put
    `A(h)=int K(x)K(x+h)dx` and
    `W_q=c_qA(logq)`.  The theta tail gives
    `W_q<=C(logq)q^C exp(-cq)`, hence `sum_qW_q<infinity`.  For a voltage

        F_eta(x,y)=sin[eta(y-x)], I_(eta,A)=w_A F_eta,

    the triangle curl at length L and intermediate distance r is

        D_L(r)=sin(eta r)+sin[eta(L-r)]
              =2sin(eta L/2)cos[eta(r-L/2)].           `(233.1)`

    Let `p_j/q_j` be continued-fraction convergents to
    `log3/log2` and `eta_j=2pi q_j/log2`.  Then (233.1) vanishes on every
    `2^n` tower length and is at most
    `2pi m|q_jlog3/log2-p_j|` on length `m log3`.  In the conductance-paid
    curl norm this is `O(q_j^(-2))`.  Meanwhile

        ||I_eta||_cur^2
          =int_0^infinity J(h)A(h)sin(eta h)^2dh
          =[A(0)/4]log eta+O(1).                       `(233.2)`

    Its distance squared from every gradient is also at least `c log eta`.
    Indeed the additive triangle defect of any gradient is zero, whereas
    `sin u+sin v-sin(u+v)` has positive mean square on every large dyadic
    shell; the three edge marginals are controlled by
    `w_A(x,x+h) asymp 1/h` on a central compact set.  After normalization,
    the currents stay a fixed distance from gradients while their curls tend
    to zero.  The closed-range theorem proves that the two-tower triangle
    range is not closed.

    This extends to all primes.  Given epsilon, summability of W leaves a
    finite set F with tail below epsilon; Kronecker recurrence chooses
    arbitrarily large eta with
    `|exp(i eta logq)-1|<epsilon` on F.  Equation (233.1) then gives total
    curl square at most
    `epsilon^2 sum_(q in F)W_q+4epsilon`.  Thus every all-prime curl norm paid
    by a summable multiple of the actual conductances has approximate
    nongradient cocycles and no bounded global homotopy.  This is compatible
    with item 227's dense closure.

    Crucially, it is not yet a Brownian-source obstruction.  For

        I_u^0(x,y)=2m(x)m(y)[u(y)-u(x)], m=CK,

    antisymmetrization gives exactly

        <I_eta,I_u^0>_cur
          =2 mhat(eta)int m(y)u(y)sin(eta y)dy,
        mhat(eta)=1/2[Xi(eta+i/2)+Xi(eta-i/2)].          `(233.3)`

    The unconditional gamma factor makes `mhat(eta)` exponentially small,
    while the Diophantine loss above is only polynomial.  Hence normalized
    bad cocycles pair uniformly to zero with all unit `L2(m)` Brownian
    columns.  Qualitative density and a uniform homotopy are therefore
    closed, but (233.3) leaves a genuinely source-specific inverse plausible.
    Its existence alone is insufficient: the exact remaining gate is the
    sharp correction/gradient norm at most one, including every non-length
    voltage mode and the full cross-Gram domain.

234. **The Brownian-specific pseudoinverse is the original Douglas
    projection; the naive length-Fourier curl inverse fails exactly.**  Let
    `B_E` be the closed physical gradient, `L_E=B_E^*B_E`, and write
    `B_E=U_E L_E^(1/2)`.  For any finite-energy source current `I` with
    `d=B_E^*I`, polar decomposition gives

        d in Dom(L_E^(-1/2)),
        J_min=U_E L_E^(-1/2)d=P_(closure Ran B_E)I,
        ||J_min||^2=<d,L_E^dagger d><=||I||^2.          `(234.1)`

    Thus item 233's shifted-Xi decay proves only compatibility with the
    unbounded inverse.  For the demand gradient `B_F`, requiring the sharp
    source norm is

        ||U_E L_E^(-1/2)B_F^*h||<=||h||

    for every h.  By Douglas this is exactly
    `B_F^*B_F<=B_E^*B_E`, the item-57/RH gate.  The physical factors
    `K(x)K(y)` destroy translation covariance, so the Fourier Brownian source

        d_eta(x)=2m(x)[c exp(-i eta x)-mhat(eta)]/(i eta) `(234.2)`

    is not an eigenmode of `L_E` and has no scalar Bloch inverse.

    The exact all-q zero-curl length voltages have no hidden complement.  If
    `F(r)+F(L_q-r)=G_q` for every prime-power length, the incommensurable 2/3
    towers force `F(h)=ah+b`; finite current norm at
    `J(h)A(h)~A(0)/(2h)` forces `b=0`, so `G_q=aL_q`.  These are precisely
    gradients of `x mapsto ax`.

    The complete finite-energy two-dimensional Fourier trial is
    `f_c(h)=cos(eta h)-1`, `f_s(h)=sin(eta h)`.  With

        theta_q=eta L_q,
        v(theta)=1/2+sin(theta)/(2theta)
                 -[2sin(theta/2)/theta]^2,
        u_q=(cos(theta_q/2),sin(theta_q/2)),

    optimizing the constant voltage on each prime chord gives the exact
    defect matrix

        Phi(eta)=4 sum_q W_q v(theta_q) u_q u_q^*.     `(234.3)`

    In the sine sector, `B_s(y)=2mhat(eta)sin(eta y)` and the attempted
    full-current curl inverse has squared constant

        C_s(eta)^2=mhat(eta)^2[c-mhat(2eta)]/[c Phi_ss(eta)]. `(234.4)`

    The Arb certificate
    `scripts/triangle_fourier_curl_falsifier.py` proves at `eta=5` that
    `Phi_ss<.0016`, `mhat(5)>.137`, and `mhat(10)<.019`, hence
    `C_s(5)^2>10.8`.  This rigorously falsifies only the construction which
    sends the whole nonzero-divergence Brownian current into curls.  It is not
    a Douglas counterexample: physical gradients must first be projected out,
    and that projection is (234.1).

235. **No finite scale block or fixed summable local triangle descent is
    coercive, even modulo affine gradients.**  For one length L put

        C_L(F)=inf_G L^(-1)int_0^L|F(r)+F(L-r)-G|^2dr.

    If `R_LF(r)=F(L-r)` and `barF` is the interval mean, exact orthogonal
    projection gives

        C_L(F)=4||P_(R_L=+1)(F-barF)||_(L2(dr/L))^2.   `(235.1)`

    Constants and every reflection-antisymmetric function are invisible.
    For every finite set of lengths, simultaneous Diophantine approximation
    gives `eta_n->infinity` with `eta_nL_j mod 2pi->0`; then
    `F_n(h)=sin(eta_nh)` has every `C_(L_j)(F_n)->0` while its distance from
    affine functions on any fixed interval tends to `1/2`.  Thus every
    finite prime/dyadic block has zero quotient gap.  At low frequency the
    exact affine quotient obeys

        D_L(sin(theta ./L))=theta^6/2800+O(theta^8),
        C_L(sin(theta ./L))=theta^6/720+O(theta^8),     `(235.2)`

    so their ratio tends to `9/35`; even the tempting constant `1/4` fails.

    More generally, no fixed summable average S of positive-measure local
    Schur decrements can geometrically remove the excess.  The arch current

        I_(eta,A)(x,x+h)=w_A(x,x+h)sin(eta h), I_(eta,q)=0

    has divergence

        d_eta(x)=K(x)int_0^infinity J(h)
                    [K(x-h)-K(x+h)]sin(eta h)dh.       `(235.3)`

    The singularity cancels at zero; integration by parts and theta
    derivative bounds give `||d_eta||_(L2(1/m))=O(eta^(-1))`, whereas
    `||I_eta||^2=[A(0)/4]log eta+O(1)`.  After normalization its Brownian
    target tends to zero and its energy tends to one, but every fixed local
    decrement tends to zero.  Dominated summation gives
    `<I_eta,SI_eta>->0` for every fixed summable local S.  Hence the proposed
    uniform excess-coercivity iteration is false.  Taking S to be the full
    cycle projection makes that condition equivalent to RH; it supplies no
    smaller lemma.

236. **A scale-adaptive prime selector is asymptotically forced and
    isometric; only the exact sharp remainder remains.**  Put
    `M(s)=int_(-infinity)^s m`, `barM(s)=int_s^infinity m`, and
    `D(s,t)=2M(min(s,t))barM(max(s,t))`.  Every current with the Brownian
    cut divergence at s has net flow `D_s=D(s,s)` through `[s,infinity)`.  If
    `C_s=G(s,s)` is the physical cut conductance, cut Cauchy gives

        D_s^2<=C_s||I_s||^2,
        int_(e crosses s)w_e|I_s(e)/w_e-D_s/C_s|^2
          <=D_s-D_s^2/C_s                             `(236.1)`

    for any putative sharp contraction.  Items 138--141 give
    `C_s/D_s->1`, with the arch crossing negligible and the prime crossing
    asymptotic to D_s.  Therefore every contraction is forced to voltage one
    on almost every prime chord crossing a remote cut; any fixed positive
    thinning loses the sharp constant.

    The forced chord-start distribution is nevertheless exact at leading
    scale.  For bounded continuous phi,

        D_s^(-1)sum_q int_(s-L_q)^s
          c_qK(x)K(x+L_q)phi(x)dx
          ->4int_R exp(-x/2)K(x)phi(x)dx.              `(236.2)`

    Thus `q~exp(s-x)` with the displayed probability law.  More importantly,
    the common-prime two-column Gram is

        G_P(s,t)=sum_(L_q>=|s-t|)c_q
          int_(max(s,t)-L_q)^(min(s,t))K(x)K(x+L_q)dx. `(236.3)`

    On the microscopic tail scale `s_u=S+u/(2pi exp(2S))`, both (236.3) and
    the target Gram, after the same diagonal normalization, tend to
    `exp[-max(u,v)]`.  Fixed positive separations also have matching
    double-exponential decay.  Hence neither diagonal nor two-column tail
    asymptotics obstructs the selector.

    For an oscillatory column combination alpha, the forced chord current

        J_(alpha,q)(x)=w_q(x)int_x^(x+L_q)alpha(s)ds   `(236.4)`

    produces the same small divisor as item 233; shifted-Xi decay suppresses
    it on Brownian sources.  The undecided term is the exact remainder
    `C_s-D_s=Q_W(K 1_[s,infinity))` and its polarized finite-column matrices,
    at a scale beyond every retained tail asymptotic.  Block-diagonal
    dominance cannot decide it because (236.1) forces full chord overlap.  A
    surviving proof needs an exact all-chord identity or sign mechanism for
    this remainder, not another tail expansion.

237. **The cubic low-frequency plateau is an auxiliary graph-norm warning,
    not an RH test.**  Define

        E_n=int_0^infinity J(h)A(h)h^n dh,
        P_n=sum_q W_qL_q^n, D_2=E_2+P_2.

    The optimized sine voltage has the form-norm expansion

        V_eta=eta grad(x)-eta^3B_3+O(eta^5),           `(237.1)`

    where `B_(3,A)` has voltage `h^3/6` and `B_(3,q)` has voltage
    `L_q^3/12`; its triangle defect is
    `eta^6P_6/720+O(eta^8)`.  Projection only on `grad(x)` uses

        kappa=(E_4/6+P_4/12)/D_2.                      `(237.2)`

    More generally, if `p_N` is the physical-energy projection of B_3 on odd
    polynomial gradients through degree N, the Brownian analysis residual is

        b_N(y)=mu_2 y+(c/3)y^3-2c p_N(y),
        C_N^2=360||b_N||_(L2(m))^2/(cP_6).             `(237.3)`

    Equation (237.3) is a necessary dual test for one auxiliary
    triangle-amplitude homotopy.  It is not the physical minimum-current
    energy and therefore cannot disprove the Markov gap even at its limit.
    Ordinary polynomials are dense in the physical form norm: exponential
    potentials are Banach-entire there by the theta tails and prime
    summability, and Fourier inversion reaches the compact smooth core.
    However the Brownian adjoint used in (237.3) is unbounded from the current
    norm to `L2(m)`; its central-to-positive-tail edge degree divided by m
    grows like `exp(x)`.  Hence form convergence of the polynomial projections
    does not imply convergence of b_N.  A graph-norm tail bound would be
    required, and obtaining it is the same source-specific Poisson gate.
    Finite or apparently stable numerical values of C_N consequently have no
    RH implication and are not being extrapolated.

238. **The exact all-chord overlap splits into positive arch modes plus the
    localized Weil form; its signed arithmetic remainder is psi-minus-x.**
    Let

        nu=sum_(n>=2)Lambda(n)n^(-1/2)delta_(log n),
        R_0(h)=nu([0,h])-2[exp(h/2)-1],
        J_1(h)=J(h)-exp(-h/2)
              =exp(-5h/2)/(1-exp(-2h)),
        Sigma(s)=int_(-infinity)^s sinh(x/2)K(x)dx,

    and define the interval-overlap profile

        a_(s,t)(h)=1_(h>=|s-t|)
          int_(max(s,t)-h)^(min(s,t))K(x)K(x+h)dx.     `(238.1)`

    Splitting the prime measure as `dnu=exp(h/2)dh+dR_0` and pairing its
    continuous baseline with the lowest arch mode gives the exact identity

        G_A(s,t)+G_P(s,t)-D(s,t)
          =2Sigma(s)Sigma(t)
             +int_0^infinity J_1(h)a_(s,t)(h)dh
             +int_[0,infinity)a_(s,t)(h)dR_0(h).       `(238.2)`

    The first term is rank-one PSD.  Expanding J_1 into modes k>=1 makes the
    second term a sum of Green Gram kernels and hence PSD.  The last term is
    the only sign problem.  If `X=exp h` and `E(X)=psi(X)-(X-1)`, partial
    summation gives exactly

        R_0(h)=X^(-1/2)E(X)
          +(1/2)int_1^X E(v)v^(-3/2)dv,                `(238.3)`

    while for `Re z>1/2`,

        int_0^infinity exp(-zh)dR_0(h)
          =-zeta'/zeta(z+1/2)-1/(z-1/2).              `(238.4)`

    Thus ordinary PNT supplies decay but no sign.

    The normalization is also exact on the zero side.  For
    `f_s=K 1_[s,infinity)` and
    `A_z(s)=int_s^infinity K(x)exp(zx)dx`, item 51 gives

        G(s,t)-D(s,t)=2Sigma(s)Sigma(t)+Q_W(f_s,f_t),  `(238.5)`

    and absolute symmetric-height summation gives

        Q_W(f_s,f_t)=sum_rho
          A_(rho-1/2)(min(s,t))
          A_(1/2-rho)(max(s,t)).                       `(238.6)`

    Half-line steps generate a form core after polarization and smoothing.
    Consequently, requiring the signed term in (238.2), or equivalently all
    cut-packet matrices in (238.5), to have the sharp sign is localized Weil
    positivity and is RH-equivalent.  The all-chord overlap identity fixes
    every constant but does not create an independent sign mechanism.

239. **The eigenfunction-specific signed-PNT estimate is exactly off-line
    pole removal; graph synthesis of subthreshold modes is RH-equivalent.**
    In item 224 let `f=Kv`, `g=Cv`, `delta=1/2-lambda>0`, and
    `F(s)=int_R exp(-sx)f(x)dx`, which is entire.  For every t, direct
    splitting of the half-line gives

        int_0^infinity exp(-sx)[f(x-t)+f(x+t)]dx
          =exp(-st)F(s)+exp(st)F_+(s;t)
             -exp(-st)F_+(-s;t),                       `(239.1)`

    where `F_+(s;t)=int_t^infinity exp(-su)f(u)du`.
    All exponential moments make the boundary-tail series locally uniformly
    convergent.  Hence the one-sided Laplace transform of g continues
    meromorphically and, at every centered zero
    `z=rho-1/2` with `Re z>0`, has

        Res_(s=z) G_+(s)=-m_rho F(z)/delta.             `(239.2)`

    Therefore the desired polynomial bound (224.4) is precisely the
    condition `F(z)=0` at every right-half off-line zero, with the matching
    jet conditions for multiplicity, plus standard vertical bounds.  Evenness
    gives `F(-z)=F(z)` rather than cancellation.  The exact obstruction

        g_z(x)=cosh(conjugate(z)x),
        f_z=(K/C)g_z,
        F_z(z)=int (K/C)|cosh(zx)|^2dx>0               `(239.3)`

    has all exponential moments and `K*g_z=0` at a zeta zero, so translate
    annihilation and analytic continuation alone permit exactly the pole
    (239.2).

    The graph-synthesis version has the same status.  Under the unitary
    `Uv=Cv` let `N=M_Xi^perp` be the translate-annihilator and let `S_R` be
    the closed real-zero jet span.  The assertion that every subthreshold
    eigenvector in N lies in the graph-norm closure of `S_R` implies, by the
    positive real-zero residue Gram, that no subthreshold eigenvector exists;
    item 62 and the Markov equivalence then give RH.  Conversely RH makes the
    assertion vacuous.  Off-line quotient functions
    `Xi(z)/(z-z_0)^m` produce nonzero elements of `N` orthogonal to `S_R`, and
    their physical exponential jets obey the same exterior scale as item 62.
    Pairing the eigen-equation with them yields the indefinite all-zero
    quartet matrices of item 229.  Thus tail regularity, finite translation
    graph norms, and raw residue synthesis cannot remove (239.2) without an
    independent RH-strength argument.

## Completed derivations in this continuation

- No complete theorem resolving RH has yet been proved; items 36--42 contain
  new exact intermediate theorems and audited equivalences.
- The cutoff-free Galerkin entries are exactly a divided-difference matrix
  plus the signed rank-two pole term, with even/odd blocks assembled in
  `scripts/cvs-parity-interlacing-audit.py`.
- Finite ground-parity gaps shrink rapidly with level, so no numerical gap is
  being used as a continuum lower bound.
- From Suzuki, arXiv:2606.09096: the lowest localized-Weil eigenvalue is
  continuous in support width; its nondegeneracy for every width is
  RH-equivalent.  The small-width positivity-improving Dirichlet form has
  jump kernel `|x-y|^{-1}` and killing potential `-log(1-x^2)`, giving a
  simple positive even ground state.  The proposed expanding-support
  characteristic-function convergence remains an unproved corollary
  hypothesis, not a theorem.
- At CA boundary parameter `F(p,1)`, if `N_p^+=pC_p` and `L=log C_p`, then
  exactly
  `f(N_p^+)/f(C_p)=(1+1/p) log(L)/log(L+log p)`.
  The critical root satisfies `L<Pcrit(L)<L+2log L`.  Combining the published
  CA bounds with Littlewood's unconditional two-sided oscillation of
  `theta(p)-p` proves infinitely many locally increasing boundary pairs and
  infinitely many locally decreasing ones.  Thus eventual local monotonicity
  is false, while neither sign controls the global record deficit.

## Fatal counterexamples inherited

See `ADVERSARIAL_AUDIT.md` and `DERIVATIONS.md`.  In addition, any proposed
theta or heat-flow condition that is just a standard Laguerre--Polya or
de Bruijn--Newman equivalent is to be rejected unless an independent
theta-specific proof is supplied.

New fatal calculation: the canonical prime-filtration Nyman approximant does
not exploit a tensor-product shortcut.  For `P_y=product_(p<=y) p`,
`a_y=phi(P_y)/P_y`, and
`F_y(x)=sum_(d|P_y) mu(d) rho(1/(d x))`, inclusion--exclusion gives
`1+F_y(x)=a_y/x` whenever `1/x` is below the next prime after `y`.
Consequently its squared error on that interval is
`a_y^2(p_next-1)`, which tends to infinity.  Enforcing the Nyman coefficient
constraint by subtracting `a_y rho(1/x)` changes the error there to
`a_y floor(1/x)` and has the same divergent lower bound.  Thus Boolean
prime-divisor factorization is defeated by the `x=0` tail and does not reopen
the compactness-killed Nyman route; optimized Gram coefficients still lack a
multiplicative factorization.

Heat calibration: non-strict positive definiteness is insufficient even for
an exact polynomial heat family.  `F_t(x)=x^3+(1-6t)x` satisfies
`partial_t F=-partial_x^2 F`; its zeros are `0,+/-i` at `t=0` and
`0,+/-sqrt(2)` at `t=1/2`, while
`F_t'^2-F_tF_t''=3x^4+(1-6t)^2>=0`, with equality at the collision.
Fixed-time strict Laguerre positivity also does not imply real-rootedness:
`f(z)=(1+z^2)sin(pi z)/(pi z)` has zeros `+/-i` but
`(f'^2-ff'')/f^2=pi^2 csc^2(pi x)-1/x^2+2(x^2-1)/(x^2+1)^2>0`
on the real axis.  Therefore only a theta-specific strict inequality for the
whole heat interval together with a proved no-escape theorem could keep this
route alive; neither follows from the convolution identity alone.
